READ FIRST

Why Exemplars Matter

The shorter atlas cards are useful for coverage. They are not always enough to show why a method works mathematically. These exemplars make the reasoning explicit: what is being estimated, constrained, routed, preserved, or certified, and why that object changes the problem rather than merely changing the implementation.

The shared picture is that modern AI papers are about preserving a claim through a transformation. The transformation may be training, reasoning, compression, sampling, evaluation, intervention, or deployment. The mathematics is the rule that says which transformations are allowed and which evidence proves the preserved claim survived.

A useful paper-level read should not stop at naming the object. It should say what the world is allowed to change without changing the claim, what cannot change without breaking the claim, and which observation proves the difference. That is the deeper pattern below: the method works when it protects the variable that carries truth while ignoring variation caused by format, coordinate choice, sampling accident, timing accident, or measurement noise.

EXEMPLAR 01

RAGEN-2

Reasoning Collapse As Lost Dependence

First-Principles Read

A reasoning trace is useful only if it is a bridge from the particular problem to the particular action. RL can reward the appearance of that bridge: longer text, familiar decomposition, confident intermediate claims. RAGEN-2 asks whether the bridge still carries load. If two different tasks produce the same reasoning skeleton in the places where the tasks differ, the model has not learned a stronger reasoning process; it has learned a ritual that survives the reward function.

Problem

Agentic RL can increase the amount of reasoning text while the reasoning stops depending on the particular input. The visible trace is longer, but the information carried by the trace is lower. This matters because an answer can improve on the training verifier while the process becomes less conditional on the evidence that would support transfer.

State Space

The state is an interaction trajectory: prompt, internal reasoning trace, actions, observations, verifier feedback, and final answer. The important object is the whole path, because the final answer alone cannot show whether the intermediate state used the right evidence.

Object

The mathematical object is conditional dependence between the trace and the task instance. A healthy trace changes when the problem changes in a way that should matter; a collapsed trace is a template whose local words may vary but whose decision-relevant structure ignores the input.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is task-specific information. Training may change style, length, and policy logits, but it should not erase information from the input that is needed to solve the task. The trace can compress the prompt, but it cannot compress away the facts that decide the next action.

Allowed Movement

RL may move the policy toward higher reward, but only in ways that keep the trajectory conditioned on the environment and not just on the reward model's preferred surface pattern.

Why It Works

The method works because it checks the basic property real reasoning must have: when the important facts in the problem change, the work path should change too. A receipt is useful because it is tied to the purchase; a copied receipt shape is not evidence of what happened. Reasoning traces have the same rule. Information cannot affect a later action unless it is present somewhere in the state that produced that action. If the trace no longer changes with the input, then it cannot be carrying the input-specific decision, no matter how sophisticated it sounds. Name used later: input-trace dependence is the preserved link between the problem evidence and the work path.

healthy trace: the work path still changes with the problem evidence while reward rises; collapse: reward rises but the work path stops depending on the problem

Evidence Check

Perturb the input, hold task format fixed, and measure whether trace structure, action choice, and intermediate claims move with the perturbation. Compare entropy with input-dependence because entropy alone can look healthy while templates persist.

Failure Boundary

The diagnostic can miss collapse if the perturbations do not touch the features the policy actually uses, or if the model preserves shallow input markers while losing the causal reasoning path.

Connection

h1 tries to preserve learning signal across longer horizons; The Tell-Tale Norm turns internal state magnitude into a compute-control signal; ParetoPO asks whether tool use is worth its cost. All are versions of one question: when should a system spend reasoning, and how do we know that spending is still tied to the task?

EXEMPLAR 02

ATLAS

Evaluation As Adaptive Measurement

First-Principles Read

ATLAS starts from a simple fact: capability is not directly visible. The evaluator sees answers to tasks, and those answers are noisy shadows of the capability claim. A good benchmark is therefore closer to a measuring instrument than a school exam. It should spend questions where the answer will most reduce uncertainty about the hidden ability, the way a doctor orders tests that distinguish the remaining diagnoses instead of repeating tests whose result is already obvious.

Problem

A fixed benchmark wastes many items because easy questions do not distinguish strong models and irrelevant questions do not reduce uncertainty about the ability being claimed. It can also overstate rank differences when two models differ by less than the noise of the instrument.

State Space

The state is the evaluator's current guess about model ability, plus a bank of questions. Each question has a rough difficulty, a degree to which it separates nearby models, and noise. After each answer, some ability guesses become less believable and others become more believable.

Object

The object is a measuring device for a hidden skill. The skill itself is not directly visible; the evaluator only sees answers. A useful question is one whose answer would separate ability levels that are still plausible. If every plausible model gets the question right, the question adds little. If every plausible model gets it wrong, it also adds little. Name used later: an item-response model is the rule that connects hidden ability to observed answers.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is rank or ability information. Evaluation should compress many possible questions into a smaller test without changing the capability claim.

Allowed Movement

The test may adaptively choose the next item based on current uncertainty after the answers seen so far. It may not choose items that maximize convenience while losing information about the target ability.

Why It Works

Adaptive testing works because not every question can change what we know. A question far below the model's level gives the same answer for many possible abilities. A question far above the level also gives little separation. The useful item is near the boundary where plausible abilities would produce different outcomes. In plain terms, information means a result that would look different under different still-plausible skill levels. Name used later: Fisher information is a sharpness score for how well a question separates those still-plausible levels.

choose the next item that best separates the ability levels still plausible after earlier answers

Evidence Check

Report uncertainty intervals after observing answers, item-information gain, agreement with full-test rankings, sensitivity to starting estimates, and cases where adaptive paths choose different items for different model regimes.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the item-response model is misspecified, when item banks omit important capability dimensions, or when models exploit item-selection patterns.

Connection

FRABench and UFEval factor scores and uncertainty; Micro-Benchmarking Reliability asks when ranks are statistically stable; CounselBench and WebDevJudge change the observation channel. All say that evaluation is measurement design before it is ranking.

EXEMPLAR 03

Rare Event Analysis And Tail Risks

Safety As Tail Probability

First-Principles Read

Safety is mathematically different from ordinary accuracy because harm is not evenly spread across samples. If one output in ten thousand creates severe damage, an average score can look excellent while the system is unacceptable. Rare-event work asks where the dangerous probability mass is hiding and how to estimate it without pretending that not seeing it in ordinary samples means it is absent.

Problem

Average benchmark behavior can look safe while rare failures dominate deployment harm. Random sampling often misses the events users most need measured, especially when the harmful set is narrow, adversarially selected, or defined by a sequence of steps rather than one answer.

State Space

The state is the set of possible outputs, actions, or work paths, plus the subset that would cause harm. The dangerous object is how much chance lands in that subset, not how pleasant the ordinary cases look on average.

Object

The object is the chance of landing in the dangerous corner, not the average experience in the middle. If a system is safe on ordinary requests but sometimes causes severe harm, the important number lives in that small corner. If some bad cases are worse than others, the object also counts how much damage they cause. Names used later: tail probability is the chance of those rare bad cases, and tail loss is their damage-weighted version.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is the real deployment estimate. The evaluator may look for failures more aggressively, but the final number must still answer the original question: how often would this happen in deployment?

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may draw examples from a search process that visits rare regions more often, but it must keep a receipt for how that search process differs from deployment.

Why It Works

The method works because probability can be measured from a different search process if we keep a receipt for how that process was biased. Ordinary random testing may almost never enter the dangerous corner, so the evaluator deliberately visits that corner more often. The count is then corrected so it answers the deployment question rather than the stress-test question. Name used later: importance sampling means looking where rare evidence is easier to see, then reweighting the count back to the real setting.

sample rare bad cases more often, then correct the count so it still estimates deployment risk

Evidence Check

State the event grammar, report confidence intervals, effective sample size, proposal sensitivity, boundary sensitivity, and examples near the event threshold.

Failure Boundary

The estimate fails if the harmful event set is wrong, if the proposal misses a failure mode, or if likelihood weights have extreme variance.

Connection

CSPO measures distance to violation; SandboxEscapeBench and Jailbreak Foundry apply adaptive pressure; FALCON and SRMC face the same sampling-support problem in molecular and generative settings.

EXEMPLAR 04

Midtraining Bridges

Training As Timed Distribution Movement

First-Principles Read

A model is not equally changeable throughout training. Early on, many representations are still soft; later, the model has already carved a geometry that makes some changes easy and others expensive. Midtraining Bridges treats data as a timed force on that geometry. The same data can be useful when it connects what the model knows to what it will later need, and weak when it arrives outside the window where the representation can still bend.

Problem

Specialized data can help at one training phase and do little or harm at another. Data value depends on where the model currently is, not only on the dataset's standalone quality. The real object is not a pile of examples but the update those examples cause at that moment.

State Space

The state is the model parameters and optimizer state moving through a sequence of training distributions: broad pretraining, specialized bridging data, and posttraining targets. Each phase changes which directions in parameter space are still easy to move.

Object

The mathematical object is a path through distributions. A data mixture is useful when it moves the model toward the target while preserving plasticity and avoiding interference.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is transferable capability. The model should gain specialized structure without losing the general representation needed for later alignment or task adaptation.

Allowed Movement

The training process may change data mixture weights over time, but the movement should remain inside a region where gradients still produce useful updates.

Why It Works

A bridge distribution works when its examples push the model in directions that the later target will need while the model is still able to absorb that structure. Mathematically, it is a path problem: local updates accumulate. A useful bridge reduces the distance between broad pretraining features and final task features without spending the model's remaining flexibility on a narrow shortcut.

use data D_b at time t when alignment(g_b,t, g_target,t) is high and interference with g_base,t is bounded

Evidence Check

Compare early, middle, and late insertion; measure target gains, general-capability loss, whether example pushes agree with the target push, representation drift, and whether the same data helps under different training states.

Failure Boundary

The bridge fails if it arrives after plasticity has narrowed, if it over-specializes the representation, or if the target distribution was misidentified.

Connection

DiReCT chooses late data by how sharp or forgiving each update direction is; Sequential Data Values makes added data value order-dependent; BLL-Loss and OPUS select examples by optimizer effect. All treat data as movement, not fuel.

EXEMPLAR 05

Quotient-Space Diffusion

Generating Molecules After Ignoring Observer-Only Changes

First-Principles Read

The same molecule can be described by many coordinate arrays. If a method treats every rotated copy as a different object, it asks the model to spend learning capacity on a fact the world has already settled: orientation in an arbitrary coordinate frame is not molecular identity. Quotient-Space Diffusion works by building that sameness into the space before learning the distribution.

Problem

Molecular coordinates contain irrelevant degrees of freedom. Rotating or translating the molecule changes coordinates but not molecular identity. A raw-coordinate generator can therefore confuse coordinate description with chemical state.

State Space

The state is a molecular configuration, but the meaningful space is not raw coordinates. It is the molecule after ignoring rotation, translation, and other observer-only changes, so many arrays map to the same physical object.

Object

The object is the same molecule represented by many coordinate arrays. The model should group those same-molecule descriptions together before learning what can chemically change.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is molecular identity and geometry that affects chemical behavior. Coordinate frame accidents should not alter likelihood, denoising direction, or generated structure.

Allowed Movement

The sampler may move through noisy molecular states, but movement must respect same-molecule descriptions and should not learn artifacts of arbitrary orientation.

Why It Works

The method works because it separates two kinds of change: changes that only rename the same object, and changes that alter the object. Grouping same-object descriptions forces the model to ignore the first kind and spend learning effort on the second. The universe lets this work because rigid transformations preserve distances between atoms and chemical relationships; they change the observer's coordinates, not the molecule.

learn over molecule identity after grouping coordinate descriptions that differ only by observer frame

Evidence Check

Test rotation and translation invariance, molecular validity, energy or property preservation, sample diversity, and whether denoising trajectories remain consistent across equivalent coordinate frames.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails if the quotient removes information that actually matters, or if downstream tasks need orientation relative to a binding pocket, surface, or external field.

Connection

Protein hierarchy papers preserve residue and fold constraints; TideGS and RadioGS preserve physical fields; HDFlow and PACT preserve action feasibility. All start by choosing the native space where validity is defined.

EXEMPLAR 06

Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates

Generalization Through Visible Signal Placement

First-Principles Read

Generalization is not controlled by size alone. A representation can be huge and still hide the target in directions that finite data cannot estimate. The key idea is that data sees some directions clearly and others weakly. Alignment-sensitive rates ask whether the task signal lies in the visible part of the representation or in the buried part where noise dominates.

Problem

Two representations can have similar size or kernel complexity but different generalization because the target signal sits in different directions. Counting dimensions misses the fact that some directions are learnable with finite samples and others are effectively invisible.

State Space

The state is a learned feature or kernel space split into ordered directions with different visibility and noise levels. Strong directions are directions the data exposes more clearly; weak directions are harder to estimate reliably.

Object

The object is whether the target lines up with the leading recoverable directions. The question is how much of the true task lives in directions that finite samples can distinguish from noise.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is target signal above noise. Generalization depends on whether the learned representation keeps the task-relevant part in directions that finite data can estimate.

Allowed Movement

Learning may reshape the representation, but useful movement increases target mass in high-signal directions instead of spreading it through noisy components.

Why It Works

The argument works because finite evidence has resolution limits. Repeated data reveals directions that appear often and strongly; it cannot reliably estimate every faint direction in a large space. If the target mostly lives in the visible span, learning has enough evidence to recover it. If the target lives outside that span, more nominal expressivity does not solve the evidence problem.

risk controlled by effective span: sum target_mass_j over directions where lambda_j exceeds noise threshold

Evidence Check

Measure how concentrated the target signal is in visible directions, sample-size scaling, perturbation sensitivity, and pairs of representations with similar complexity but different alignment.

Failure Boundary

The theory can mislead if the visible-direction structure changes during training, if the noise model is wrong, or if downstream tasks rely on nonlinear structure not captured by the ordered basis.

Connection

Single-Head Attention studies signal recovery in attention; BBP transitions ask when spikes separate from noise; LoRA theory asks whether task movement lives in a low-rank subspace. All are signal-visibility arguments.

EXEMPLAR 07

Distributional Equivalence

Causal Discovery As Saying Which Stories Are Still Possible

First-Principles Read

Many different hidden stories can create the same data. If rain and traffic both change commute time, a table of observations may not tell us which one caused which unless the right variation is present. Distributional Equivalence is valuable because it refuses to pretend that one story has been proven when several stories still fit the evidence. The mathematical win is honesty about what has actually been ruled out.

Problem

The same table of observations can fit several cause stories, especially when some causes are unseen or effects can feed back into causes. Returning one diagram can make the evidence sound stronger than it is.

State Space

The state is the set of cause diagrams and unseen cause stories that would all produce the observed patterns. The formal handle is a family of causal graphs with hidden structure.

Object

The object is the whole set of causal stories that still fit the observations and assumptions. If the data cannot separate those stories, the method should keep them together instead of picking one.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay clear is the line between what the evidence has ruled out and what is merely one possible story. The formal handle is identified versus still-plausible structure.

Allowed Movement

The algorithm may remove stories that violate measured patterns, but it cannot choose among stories the data still cannot tell apart unless it adds new assumptions.

Why It Works

It works because a causal claim is only as strong as the alternatives it defeats. If every still-possible graph says the same thing about a cause, then the claim does not depend on guessing the exact hidden graph. If two still-possible graphs disagree, the method must say "not identified." That refusal is not weakness; it is the exact boundary of what the evidence can support.

claim c is identified if c(G) is the same for every G with P_obs(G) = P_obs and satisfying assumptions A

Evidence Check

Report how many causal stories remain possible, rank-constraint calibration, hidden-variable stress tests, false edge and missing edge rates, and examples where the method correctly refuses to identify a unique graph.

Failure Boundary

The output is only as good as the promises made about which cause stories are allowed and the tests used to decide whether a pattern is really present.

Connection

Local Covariate Selection finds the background facts needed for one fair effect comparison; DiCoLa checks when relationships disappear after the right facts are held fixed; CausalGame chooses interventions to separate cause stories. All protect the same boundary: what the evidence distinguishes from what a model can narrate.

EXEMPLAR 08

Steer Like The LLM

Prompting As An Internal Push

First-Principles Read

A prompt works by changing the model before it answers. The words are not magic; they are a handle that moves internal numbers into a different pattern. Steer Like The LLM asks whether we can learn that internal move rather than keep repeating the words. If the same internal move causes the same behavior across many inputs, then control can happen inside the model state instead of through fragile prompt phrasing.

Problem

Prompt words are an indirect control interface. They may steer behavior, but they also consume context, interact with unrelated instructions, and make the control signal hard to isolate.

State Space

The state is the model's internal numbers as they change across layers and tokens, plus the resulting spread of possible next words. Names used later: hidden activation trajectory and output distribution.

Object

The object is the internal push caused by an instruction: how the model's hidden state changes when the prompt is present compared with when it is absent.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is the intended behavioral change caused by the prompt, separated from the incidental surface words that produced it.

Allowed Movement

The method may move internal states in the learned direction that causes the desired behavior, but it should not disturb unrelated internal facts or make all non-target outputs collapse into one narrow pattern.

Why It Works

It works when the prompt has a repeatable physical effect inside the network. If adding an instruction usually moves the hidden state in a similar direction, then a small learned controller can reproduce that movement. The key is separation: keep the intended behavior change, drop the accidental wording, and avoid moving unrelated features that the original prompt happened to touch.

learn delta h ~= h(prompted, x) - h(base, x); apply h' = h + delta h_target

Evidence Check

Compare word-prompt control and internal-state control on the same tasks, remove candidate layers, test off-target behavior, measure how the spread of possible answers changes, and check whether the same steering move transfers across inputs.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails if the prompt's internal effect only works in one context, changes differently across layers, or is tangled with facts that should not move.

Connection

The Tell-Tale Norm also turns hidden-state measurements into control. MINT, Causal Route Gating, and hidden-route oracle work change internal routes to ask which one actually causes the output.

EXEMPLAR 09

RLVepsR

Verifier Labels As Imperfect Pushes On Behavior

First-Principles Read

A checker is a teacher only if its mistakes do not point the learner in the wrong direction. Random noise can sometimes average out. A repeated blind spot does not average out; training follows it. RLVepsR matters because it treats checker feedback as a push on behavior, not just a label. The real question is whether that push moves the model toward true success or toward whatever fools the checker.

Problem

Learning from checker feedback can improve a model only if checker labels usually agree with the real target. Weak checkers can produce learning, no learning, or anti-learning depending on which mistakes they make.

State Space

The state is the model's current answer habits, the spread of outputs it tends to produce, and the checker that maps artifacts to noisy pass/fail labels or rewards. Names used later: policy distribution and verifier observation channel.

Object

The object is whether the checker's reward pushes the model's answer habits in the same direction real task success would. If those pushes disagree, training can improve the checker score while making the real task worse.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is the link between getting rewarded and getting genuinely better. The checker may be cheap or weak, but its reward must point the model roughly toward real correctness.

Allowed Movement

The model may move toward higher checker reward only while that reward remains a useful stand-in for the target. When false positives dominate, movement should stop or defer to a stronger check.

Why It Works

It works when verifier errors are mostly harmless noise around the right signal. In that case, many updates still point in roughly the right direction. It fails sharply when the verifier has a consistent blind spot, because repeated improvement searches for exactly those blind spots. The model does not know the verifier is a stand-in measurement; it only sees that certain outputs get rewarded.

useful RL needs the verifier reward push to agree with the true-success push

Evidence Check

Sweep verifier false-positive and false-negative rates, measure learn/stall/anti-learn regimes, audit disagreement cases, and compare weak-verifier updates to stronger oracle or human judgments.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when checker errors are systematic in a direction the model can exploit, or when the checker watches the wrong artifact.

Connection

This is the training version of benchmark validity. It connects JudgeBench, WebDevJudge, CyberGym, formal proof checking, and checker-gaming chains: training inherits the measuring tool's errors.

EXEMPLAR 10

DiReCT

Data Choice As Avoiding Fragile Update Directions

First-Principles Read

A training example is not just good or bad by itself. It pushes the model. Near the end of training, some directions are like flat ground: the model can move there without breaking much. Other directions are like tight wires: small movement can damage behavior that has already been learned. DiReCT chooses data by asking where each example would push the model, not by judging the example in isolation.

Problem

Late-training examples can improve held-out behavior or damage fragile parts of the model. Data quality alone is not enough; where the example pushes the current model decides whether the update is safe.

State Space

The state is the model near the end of training, the push from each candidate example, and the validation directions where small movement causes large loss changes.

Object

The object is a proposed model move split into forgiving directions and fragile directions. Forgiving directions can absorb change; fragile directions can break useful behavior after a small push.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is validation performance in fragile directions. The model can move, but not through directions where small changes cause large loss increases.

Allowed Movement

Select examples whose pushes agree with useful forgiving directions and stay small in fragile harmful directions.

Why It Works

It works because the same-sized update can have very different consequences depending on direction. A move along a flexible direction can add capability. A move across a sensitive direction can undo a lot. Data selection becomes safer when it chooses examples whose push is useful and avoids examples whose push cuts across fragile behavior.

prefer examples that push useful forgiving directions and avoid fragile directions where validation loss rises sharply

Evidence Check

Measure how much each candidate push enters fragile directions, how held-out behavior moves, selected-versus-random data, timing sensitivity, and ablations that use only push size without checking local fragility.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if the fragile-direction estimate is noisy, if validation sensitivity misses deployment failures, or if forgiving directions encode shortcuts.

Connection

Midtraining Bridges studies when data should arrive; Sequential Data Values studies order-dependent value; LoRA theory studies constrained parameter movement. DiReCT gives the local movement-shape version of the same idea.

EXEMPLAR 11

ReaSyn And MORetro*

Molecules As Route-Constrained Objects

First-Principles Read

A molecule on a screen is not enough. In drug design, a molecule also has to be reachable by reactions, made from available pieces, and compatible with the target context. ReaSyn and MORetro* matter because they stop treating synthesis as an afterthought. They build the route into the object being optimized, so the model searches among candidates that can plausibly exist in a lab.

Problem

A generated molecule can have attractive predicted properties while being hard or impossible to synthesize. The valid object is not only a graph; it is a graph with a route.

State Space

The state includes the molecule graph, available building pieces, reaction steps, backward synthesis plan, 3D shape, target context, and property limits.

Object

The object is a molecule paired with a route for making it. A candidate is valid only if the molecule and the route satisfy the chemistry rules together. The formal handle is a feasible set of molecule-route pairs.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is practical chemical reachability while properties improve. Searching for better properties should not move the molecule outside the set reachable by plausible synthesis.

Allowed Movement

The generator may change the structure, route, or building pieces, but every move must keep reaction rules and target rules consistent.

Why It Works

It works because impossible candidates are removed before they can dominate the search. A property model may love a molecule that no reaction path can produce. If route feasibility is inside the state, the optimizer must improve property while staying connected to a viable path. The search becomes smaller, harder to cheat, and closer to the real scientific task.

optimize property(m) subject to exists route r with C_reaction(m,r) and C_target(m) satisfied

Evidence Check

Report route validity, building-block availability, reaction-template success, property-route tradeoffs, novelty, and whether optimizing property increases infeasible candidates.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if route templates are incomplete, if predicted reactions do not hold experimentally, or if route feasibility conflicts with biological target constraints.

Connection

Quotient-Space Diffusion handles when two coordinate descriptions name the same molecule; DCFold and protein hierarchy papers handle folded structure; HDFlow and PACT handle physically possible action. All make validity part of the state before search begins.

EXEMPLAR 12

Error Propagation In Quantized Diffusion Models

Compression As Small Rounding Mistakes That Build Up

First-Principles Read

Diffusion generation is a long chain of corrections. A tiny rounding error at one step can be harmless, or it can be carried forward and magnified by later steps. Quantized diffusion papers matter because they treat compression as a path problem. The question is not "was this one approximation close?" but "after many repeated approximations, did the sample travel through the same kind of output cloud?"

Problem

Using fewer number levels can look accurate at one cleanup step but drift across the full sample-making path. The final image or sample inherits accumulated numerical error.

State Space

The state is the sample-making path: the noisy sample, which step it is on, the model's cleanup prediction, the rounded version of that prediction, and the next sample that will be fed into the following step. Papers often call this step-by-step path a sampler trajectory.

Object

The object is how rounding mistakes travel through a repeated process. Each step takes the current sample, changes it, and hands it to the next step. A small early mistake can matter more than the same mistake near the end because the early mistake is reused by many later steps. Name used later: an iterative dynamical system is a process where the next state is made from the current state again and again.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the cloud of final samples reached after the whole path, not only one local cleanup prediction. Quantization should preserve both the step-level cleanup and the kind of images, molecules, or outputs reached after many rounded steps.

Allowed Movement

The method may round weights or internal numbers, but the rounding must know where it happens in the path. An early error and a late error do not have the same effect, because an early error can be reused and amplified by many later steps.

Why It Works

It works because the sample-making procedure is not a collection of isolated predictions. Each state becomes the input to the next state. If a step is sensitive, an error there changes everything that follows. A good low-bit method spends precision where later steps depend on it most and saves precision where the path is naturally stable. The formal handle is a Jacobian: a local sensitivity map for how a small mistake at one step is carried into the next step.

final error depends on how each step carries forward earlier error plus the new rounding error added at that step

Evidence Check

Measure which timesteps are fragile, how final samples degrade, whether the generated outputs move away from the intended output pattern, long-step stability, calibration data effects, and comparisons against one-step quantization metrics.

Failure Boundary

The model fails if the simple small-error estimate misses large step-by-step behavior changes, or if rounding removes rare output types that ordinary image scores barely count.

Connection

LiftQuant, TetraJet, FOCUS/RePAIR, and finite-precision theory all say compression is behavioral: numerical changes can alter the function, path, or reasoning loop being preserved.

EXEMPLAR 13

VISUALSWAP And MINT

Multimodal Answers As Depending On The Right Evidence

First-Principles Read

A correct answer is not proof of visual understanding. The model may have guessed from the question wording or common text patterns. VISUALSWAP and MINT ask the direct question: if the visual evidence had been different, would the answer have changed? Grounding means the answer depends on the right evidence channel, not merely that an image was present.

Problem

A multimodal model can answer from language priors while appearing visually grounded. The final answer does not prove which modality caused it.

State Space

The state includes image evidence, text evidence, the internal place where those sources are combined, routes through the model, and the spread of possible final answers.

Object

The object is whether the answer actually depends on the intended evidence source. If the picture is supposed to decide the answer, changing the relevant picture fact should change the answer.

Preserved Quantity

The preserved quantity is grounding: the answer should change when relevant visual evidence changes and remain stable when irrelevant nuisance evidence changes.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may swap visual evidence, change an internal route, or patch memory, but the change must isolate the source being tested instead of changing several things at once.

Why It Works

It works because cause is tested by controlled change. If swapping the relevant visual fact leaves the answer unchanged, the image was probably not carrying the decision. If changing irrelevant background changes the answer, the route is fragile. The method separates evidence that should control the answer from decoration that should not.

grounded answer needs y(e_visual, e_text) to vary with relevant e_visual and stay stable under nuisance changes

Evidence Check

Run visual swaps, route ablations, memory patching, attribution over time, text-prior controls, and cases where visual and language priors disagree.

Failure Boundary

The method fails if swaps introduce artifacts, if the intervention changes multiple channels at once, or if attribution is measured at a layer that is not causally upstream of the answer.

Connection

MoCA separates seeing failure from reasoning failure; Causal Route Gating suppresses routes where text habit overrides the image; DLMR tracks whether a memory came from observation or later summary; PRISM and Mind-Omni translate noisy brain signals into usable concepts. All ask which evidence channel did the work.

EXEMPLAR 14

Skill-Pro

Agent Experience As Reusable Procedure

First-Principles Read

A useful skill is not a memory of text. It is a reusable way to change the world under the right conditions. Skill-Pro matters because it compresses a successful episode into a procedure with a start condition, actions, and a stop condition. That is what makes reuse possible: the agent knows when the old solution applies, what to do, and when to stop doing it.

Problem

An agent that solves a task once often wastes future compute rediscovering the same workflow. But blindly replaying a past run is unsafe because a workflow is valid only when the same needed conditions are present.

State Space

The state is the user goal, what the environment currently shows, tool calls, file or world changes, checker outputs, and the condition that proves the job is done. Names used later: agent trajectory, verifier outputs, and termination condition.

Object

The object is a saved procedure that knows when to start, what steps to take, and when to stop. The formal handle is an executable skill: a partial policy with activation conditions, internal steps, and a stopping rule.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay preserved is procedural correctness. Compressing an episode into a skill should keep the dependency chain that made the original run succeed.

Allowed Movement

The system may abstract away incidental details of one episode, but it must keep the variables that decide whether the skill applies and when it should stop.

Why It Works

It works because reuse is valid when the repeated part of the world is real. If the same start conditions lead to the same useful action pattern, the agent can stop searching from scratch and call the saved procedure. The saved procedure protects three things: when to activate, which state changes matter during execution, and what observation proves completion.

skill o = (I_o, pi_o, beta_o): activate in I_o, execute pi_o, terminate with beta_o

Evidence Check

Test skill reuse on shifted tasks, precondition failures, stale environment state, wrong termination, and comparison with replaying raw trajectories or solving from scratch.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the start rule omits a hidden condition, when the environment changes its behavior, or when the saved skill drops a dependency needed for correctness.

Connection

CyberGym and WebDevJudge check artifacts that must run; ThinKV keeps context needed later; h1 composes short dependencies into longer reasoning paths. Skill-Pro is the agent-memory version of keeping enough state for the next decision.

EXEMPLAR 15

ParetoPO

Tool Use As Spending For A Better Answer

Problem

Imagine hiring an assistant who can answer from memory, open a calculator, search records, or ask another expert. More checking can improve the answer, but each check takes time, costs money, and can introduce a new mistake. A plain accuracy score hides whether the assistant solved the task wisely or just spent heavily.

State Space

The state is everything the agent has already done and paid for: its private thinking, each tool call, what the tools returned, the final answer, the time spent waiting, the money spent calling tools, and the extra risk from involving those tools.

Object

The object is the menu of choices that are not clearly wasteful. One agent may be cheap and usually right; another may be slower and slightly more accurate. A choice is wasteful if another choice gives at least the same correctness for less cost, or gives better correctness for the same cost.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful task performance after the cost is counted. What may change is how often the agent uses tools and how much extra accuracy it buys with them.

Allowed Movement

The agent may spend more calls to get harder answers right, or spend fewer calls when memory is enough. The important rule is that the tradeoff is shown before anyone hides it inside one combined score.

Why It Works

This works because "better" has more than one side. A hospital, a classroom, and a phone app may choose different agents because delay, money, privacy exposure, and mistakes have different prices in each setting. The method first throws away choices that are plainly wasteful. What remains is the set of real tradeoffs: to get more correctness, you must knowingly give up time, money, simplicity, or risk. Name used later: Pareto frontier.

keep pi only when no pi' is at least as accurate and no more costly, with one side strictly better

Evidence Check

Show the cost-versus-correctness curve, per-task tool budgets, the value of one more call, and examples where fewer tool calls give the same right answer. Include the cost of checking the tools, not only the tool price.

Failure Boundary

The curve misleads if it leaves out human review time, security exposure, bad tool results, or the damage caused when the agent confidently uses the wrong tool.

Connection

VALUEFLOW and SafeDPO ask how usefulness trades against safety. LiftQuant and TetraJet ask how accuracy trades against speed and machine limits. ParetoPO applies the same plain idea to agents: better means better for a visible price.

EXEMPLAR 16

WebDevJudge, PaperBench, And VERINA

Evaluation As Checking The Thing Itself

Problem

A builder can write a convincing note saying a website works, while the real page has broken buttons. A paper can summarize a result smoothly while the cited evidence does not support it. A proof can sound plausible while a proof checker rejects it. Text about an artifact is not the artifact.

State Space

The state is the real object and the trail behind it: files, tests, browser behavior, claims, citations, proof steps, dependencies, execution logs, and the exact rule used to decide whether the object passed.

Object

The object is a claim tied to evidence that can be inspected. The claim says what should be true; the evidence is the website, paper, code, proof, or run log that must make it true. The important link is not "the text sounds right," but "the thing being judged can be checked where it can actually fail."

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is whether the actual artifact works. What may change is how the evaluator breaks the artifact into smaller checks.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may split a website into links, forms, layouts, and tests, or split a paper into claims and citations. It cannot drop the links between those pieces, because many failures happen only when pieces are used together.

Why It Works

This works because correctness lives in different places for different artifacts. A web app must be opened and used because layout, clicks, and state changes can fail even when the description is perfect. Code must run because syntax and real inputs expose mistakes prose can hide. A proof must satisfy its checker because one invalid step breaks the whole chain. A paper claim must lead back to evidence because summaries can skip the weak spot. The formal handle is a verifier relation: a rule that accepts a claim only when the right supporting object, checked in the right medium, supports it.

accept claim c only if checker V can follow the needed path through artifact a and witness w

Evidence Check

Report false passes, false failures, execution logs, broken dependencies, human disagreement, and cases where a text-only judge approves something the artifact-level check rejects.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the checker sees only an easy stand-in, skips interactions among parts, or rewards artifacts that are written to fool the checker instead of work for users.

Connection

CyberGym makes security tasks executable. PaperBench and RefineStat tie scientific claims to checkable work. The shared lesson is simple: judge the object that can fail, not the explanation around it.

EXEMPLAR 17

ThinKV, STAR-KV, And EntroKV

Context Compression As Keeping The Needed Notes

Problem

Imagine taking notes during a long meeting. You cannot write down every word, but if you drop the one decision made early in the meeting, your later summary may be wrong. Long-context models face the same problem when memory is expensive.

State Space

The state is the conversation so far, the model's stored memory entries, which parts later steps can still look at, which facts are repeated, which facts are rare, and the future questions the model may need to answer.

Object

The object is a shorter memory that still carries the facts needed later. It is not a pleasant summary for a reader. It is more like the notes a person keeps on the desk while solving the next part of the problem: if a note can change a later answer, dropping it changes the work.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the answer the model would give when the dropped text matters. What may change is the exact wording, order, or storage form of repeated and unused details.

Allowed Movement

The system may remove, merge, route, or store entries with fewer bits. It should do that based on whether an entry can change future behavior, not only whether it looks important right now.

Why It Works

This works when the shortened memory leaves the model in almost the same position as the full history for the tasks that matter. Repeated filler can be dropped if it would not change any later decision. A quiet fact must be kept if a later question depends on it. The test is not whether the memory looks compact; it is whether future behavior stays the same when the full history is replaced by the shorter record. The formal handle is a sufficient state or sufficient statistic.

good cache C(H) makes future answers from C(H) close to future answers from full history H

Evidence Check

Compare with full context on delayed facts, rare clues, multi-step references, distracting text, long video memory, and cases where early tokens matter only near the end.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when it keeps only loud or recent text, drops rare evidence, or is tested on future tasks that do not match the tasks it was tuned for.

Connection

Skill-Pro compresses agent experience into reusable procedures. VideoKR keeps the video evidence needed later. Mamba Markov ICL asks when a running memory is enough. They all ask the same question: what can be forgotten without changing the next decision?

EXEMPLAR 18

CSPO, SafeDPO, And SecFid

Safety As Staying Inside A Guardrail

Problem

A driver should not first speed toward a cliff and then rely on a last-second fine to stop. Some safety training has that shape: make the model more helpful, then punish unsafe answers afterward. That can still let learning pass through places it should avoid.

State Space

The state is how likely the model is to give each kind of answer, which answers are known to be safe or unsafe, which cases sit near the line, how much useful detail the answer gives, and how close a training update would move the model toward a violation.

Object

The object is the set of changes the model is allowed to make. The model may become more useful, more direct, or less overcautious, but the path of change must stay inside the safety region instead of crossing into unsafe behavior and hoping a later penalty pulls it back.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is respect for the safety rule, especially near hard cases. What may change is wording, helpful detail, refusal behavior, and ranking among safe answers.

Allowed Movement

Training may make safe answers more likely and unnecessary refusals less likely. It may not buy usefulness by making unsafe acceptance more likely or by ignoring uncertain boundary cases.

Why It Works

This works because it controls the move before the model takes it. A penalty says, "that bad result was expensive after it happened." A guardrail says, "do not choose an update that makes that bad result too likely." This matters most near hard boundary cases, where a small push toward helpfulness can also make unsafe acceptance more likely. The formal handle is constrained optimization: improving the useful score while satisfying an explicit safety condition.

maximize usefulness(pi) while safety_risk(pi) stays below epsilon

Evidence Check

Audit both sides of the line: unsafe acceptance, needless refusal, adaptive prompts near the boundary, subgroup behavior, and how much usefulness is lost as the safety rule gets stricter.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the safety rule measures the wrong thing, sees only easy examples, or changes once adversarial users start pressing on it.

Connection

PACT constrains robot action. Copyright-Bench constrains legal agent behavior. VALUEFLOW shows value-safety tradeoffs. These all replace "maximize one score" with "improve only through allowed moves."

EXEMPLAR 19

LiftQuant, TetraJet, And QAT Scaling

Low-Bit Deployment As Using A Smaller Measuring Cup

Problem

Imagine replacing a precise kitchen scale with one that rounds to the nearest large mark. For many recipes nothing changes, but a delicate recipe can fail. Low-bit models are similar: they save memory and run faster, but rounding can change reasoning, repetition, confidence, or behavior on specific hardware.

State Space

The state is the real deployed computation: stored weights, intermediate numbers created while answering, rounding rules, scale factors, fused operations, memory layout, and the exact arithmetic supported by the serving hardware.

Object

The object is the recipe that turns the original model into the smaller-number model. It says which numbers get rounded, which numbers get rescaled first, which operations are merged, and whether the model is trained while already seeing the smaller measuring cup it will use in production.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the behavior users rely on: decisions, confidence, long-output stability, and speed targets. What may change is the exact internal number used to get there.

Allowed Movement

The system may round numbers, change scales, fuse operations, or train with low precision. Those changes must match the arithmetic that will actually run at serving time.

Why It Works

This works when the lost numeric detail is detail the task did not need. Some internal numbers are like rough ingredient amounts: a little rounding does not change the recipe. Others are like a medicine dose: a small change can matter. Scaling tries to place the important numbers where the smaller numeric grid can still express them. Training with low precision lets the model learn around the rounding it will face later. Name used later: quantization, approximating a model with fewer numeric levels.

need deployed low-bit model f_q(x) to behave like full model f(x) on real serving inputs x

Evidence Check

Report real hardware traces, precision comparisons, long-output behavior, confidence drift, latency-memory curves, and tasks where small numeric errors grow into large behavior changes.

Failure Boundary

The guarantee fails when the test uses fake rounding that differs from real kernels, when rare tokens or long sequences magnify errors, or when average accuracy hides drift in important cases.

Connection

Quantized diffusion studies how small rounding errors build along a generation path. FOCUS and RePAIR study behavior changes after pruning. TileLang makes machine layout explicit. The shared rule is that deployment math is the math the machine actually runs.

EXEMPLAR 20

Safety-Guided Flow And DMPO

Reward And Safety As Steering A Crowd

Problem

Imagine guiding a crowd through a city. You want people to avoid unsafe streets and reach better places, but if you push everyone through the same narrow road, you lose many valid routes. Guided generation has the same risk: stronger reward or safety pressure can improve samples while erasing diversity or chasing a flawed score.

State Space

The state is the generation path: the starting noise or base model, the half-formed outputs along the way, the reward or safety signal, the strength of each steering step, and the final image, molecule, plan, or text.

Object

The object is the crowd of possible outputs and how that crowd is steered while it forms. The method does not only pick a final output. It changes which half-formed outputs become more likely and which ones fade away before the final sample appears.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is coverage of valid outputs. What may change is how much probability is shifted away from unsafe or low-reward outputs and toward safer or better ones.

Allowed Movement

Guidance may bend the path toward safer or more useful regions. It should not erase rare valid outputs, create only reward-hacking patterns, or use a steering rule that does not match the actual step-by-step sample-making procedure. Papers often call that procedure the sampler.

Why It Works

This works when each small steering step nudges the crowd toward better outputs without trapping everyone in the same narrow corner. A reward signal is useful only if high reward still means the output is really better. A safety signal is useful only if it removes dangerous routes without deleting rare but valid routes. The formal handle is distribution transport: moving probability mass from one shape to another while preserving the parts of the target that still matter.

guided path changes probability over time, but should keep the valid target region covered

Evidence Check

Audit diversity, rare valid outputs, reward-target disagreement, safety boundary cases, coverage under stronger guidance, and examples where the reward looks high but the real output is worse.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the reward is only a stand-in for the true goal, when guidance deletes important kinds of output, or when the step-by-step generator is treated as if it could make moves it does not actually make.

Connection

RLVepsR studies what happens when reward labels become training pressure. Rare Event Analysis asks whether rare cases are still produced. Quantized diffusion studies path error. Safety-Guided Flow and DMPO put the steering question inside the step-by-step generator itself.

EXEMPLAR 21

IRNO And RealPDEBench

Physical Surrogates As Stable Step-By-Step Predictors

Problem

A weather shortcut can be close for the next hour and badly wrong after a day if each small error feeds the next prediction. Cheap replacement physics models face that problem. Papers often call them surrogates: they stand in for a slower simulator. One good step is not enough if repeated steps create impossible waves, leaks, or boundary failures.

State Space

The state is the physical field being predicted, the grid it lives on, the boundary rules at the edges, the leftover mismatch after a prediction, the correction the model applies next, and the whole rollout over time.

Object

The object is a learned rule that maps one physical state to the next physical state. It is a replacement for an expensive simulator, so it must behave like a stable process. A one-step guess can look good while repeated use slowly creates impossible heat, pressure, waves, or mass.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the physical relationship that makes the future follow from the present. What may change is the cheaper internal representation, grid size, or correction step used to compute it.

Allowed Movement

The model may correct its own leftover error again and again. Each correction should reduce the important error or keep it bounded, not add new small patterns that grow over time.

Why It Works

This works when repeated correction acts like steady cleanup. After each step, the model looks at what is still mismatched and pushes the field closer to one that obeys the physical rule. The key is that small leftovers must not grow when the rule is applied again. A useful replacement simulator is like a shortcut map that still sends the river downhill, keeps heat from appearing from nowhere, and respects the boundary of the room. Names used later: a learned operator is the rule that transforms a whole field, a fixed point is a state that stays consistent when the rule is applied, and a residual is the leftover mismatch between the prediction and the physical rule.

seek state u where learned rule F_theta(u, conditions) and physical residual R(u) stay small over rollout

Evidence Check

Measure long-rollout error, high-frequency noise, new grid sizes, changed boundary conditions, conservation checks, and domain-specific physical metrics rather than one-step average error alone.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when one-step tests hide errors that grow later, when the model cannot represent the needed correction, or when the benchmark avoids the physical situations where the cheap replacement model will be used.

Connection

RealPDEBench evaluates whether physical structure survives. Long video work studies whether a trajectory stays coherent. Diffusion transport papers control path error. The shared rule is that local mistakes matter because they accumulate.

EXEMPLAR 22

Local Covariate Selection And TRECA

Local Effects As Comparing Like With Like

Problem

Suppose you want to know whether a tutoring program helped one kind of student. Comparing every tutored student with every untutored student may be unfair if the groups differ in preparation, school, or family support. But adding every possible variable can also wash out the very effect you wanted to measure.

State Space

The state is the treatment, the outcome, the candidate background facts, the local group being studied, the policy question, the cases that can fairly be compared, and any hidden factor that could still make the comparison unfair.

Object

The object is the set of background facts needed for this one comparison. It is not a full map of every cause in the world. It is the smaller checklist needed so "treated" and "untreated" mean a real difference in treatment, not a hidden difference in who ended up in each group.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the change being studied: what would happen if this treatment were applied here. Papers often call that kind of deliberate change an intervention. What may change is which background facts are included, excluded, or weighted for the local group.

Allowed Movement

The method may choose background facts that make treated and untreated cases comparable. It should not match on facts that are consequences of the treatment, erase the effect being studied, or leave the groups with no real overlap.

Why It Works

This works when the selected background facts make the comparison fair enough that treatment status is no longer secretly standing in for preparation, access, health, income, or some other difference. Then the remaining outcome gap is more plausibly about the treatment question. The method also needs overlap: if no untreated case looks like the treated cases, there is no honest comparison to make. Names used later: covariate selection and causal identification.

estimate local effect only when treated and untreated cases are comparable after selected variables Z

Evidence Check

Report balance checks, overlap, sensitivity to hidden factors, local versus global estimates, placebo outcomes, and whether policy or fairness conclusions change under nearby variable choices.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the selected background facts miss an important hidden cause, when the local treated and untreated groups barely overlap, or when adjusting changes the treatment question itself.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence warns that data can fit more than one cause story. Fair Causal Bandits use estimates of what an action would change to choose actions. Missing-covariate work keeps uncertainty about unseen background facts. All ask the same first question: what does the evidence really let us claim?

EXEMPLAR 23

Neural Effect Search And HypoSpace

Discovery As Making Claims That Can Be Checked

Problem

Imagine reading thousands of lab notes and asking a model to suggest what might be true. It can produce many smart-sounding ideas. The danger is that the same notes used to invent the idea can also make the idea look supported, even when it is only a coincidence found by searching hard enough.

State Space

The state is the whole discovery situation: all the claims the system could have made, the evidence already used to invent them, the new claim being considered, the rule for searching, the evidence held back for checking, and the rule for saying "this claim survived." The pile of claims matters because searching a larger pile gives the system more chances to find a lucky accident. Name used later: hypothesis space.

Object

The object is a claim that can lose. A useful scientific claim is not just a fluent sentence. It must point to evidence that could prove it wrong, and it must say what observation would count as failure. That is the plain meaning of a rejectable hypothesis.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is testability. The system may look through many possible ideas, but the final accepted idea must still face evidence that was not already spent inventing it.

Allowed Movement

The search may propose, combine, narrow, or rank ideas. What it cannot do is pretend that looking at many ideas is the same as looking at one. The more ideas it tries, the easier it is to find a lucky pattern, so the final check must remember how wide the search was.

Why It Works

This works by separating invention from checking. Search is allowed to be creative and broad because its job is to find possible claims. Acceptance must be stricter because the winner has already benefited from the search. If the system looks through many claims, some will fit by luck. The final check asks whether the claim still holds on evidence that did not help create it, and it counts how many chances the search had to find a lucky pattern. Names used later: validation for checking on separate evidence, and false discovery control for limiting how often searched-for coincidences are mistaken for real findings.

accept claim h only if separate checking evidence supports h after accounting for how many claims were searched

Evidence Check

Report held-back evidence, repeated checks by another method, failed examples, how many claims were searched, whether the claim can be reproduced, and whether it survives independent data or execution.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the same evidence invents and approves the claim, when the claim is too vague to be wrong, or when the system searches thousands of ideas but reports the winner as if it were chosen in advance.

Connection

Bitween and RefineStat make scientific artifacts checkable. PaperBench breaks paper claims into evidence trails. Neural Effect Search connects effect estimates to new candidate claims. The shared point is that discovery only counts when the evidence can push back.

EXEMPLAR 24

Mind-Omni And PRISM

Brain Signals As A Careful Translation Layer

Problem

Brain and body signals are messy measurements, not words or pictures. A scanner can pick up machine patterns, timing quirks, subject differences, and task shortcuts. If a model turns those signals into ordinary tokens too quickly, it may learn the noise around the measurement instead of the biological state inside it.

State Space

The state is the measured signal, where and when it was recorded, the task the person was doing, the person or session it came from, the compact internal code the model builds, and the final decoded output. The compact code is not meant for people to read directly. It is the model's internal translation of the signal: a smaller working version that keeps the useful biological pattern and throws away details the later model should not need. Name used later: latent representation.

Object

The object is a translation layer between biological measurements and a general AI model. It should carry the part of the signal that changes with brain or body state, while leaving behind scanner quirks, subject identity, timing artifacts, and easy task clues. The layer is an interface in the everyday sense: one side speaks in raw body measurements, the other side speaks in model tokens, and the bridge decides what is allowed to cross. Name used later: structured latent interface.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the biological information needed for the task. What may change is the format: raw voltages, time traces, region-level summaries, or internal tokens can be rearranged if they still track the real signal.

Allowed Movement

The method may group the signal into chunks, mask parts of it, line it up with text or images, or translate it into model tokens. It must keep proving that the output depends on the biological signal, not on the prompt, scanner, subject identity, or a memorized reconstruction pattern.

Why It Works

This works when the translation layer acts like a narrow, disciplined bridge. It is narrow enough that the model cannot ignore the signal and solve the task from context alone. It is disciplined enough that changing the real signal changes the internal code in the expected way. Only the information that crosses this bridge can affect the later model, so the bridge forces the system to choose what matters. Name used later: bottleneck.

learn code z from signal so output changes when the real signal changes, not when only scanner noise changes

Evidence Check

Swap signal pieces while holding the prompt fixed, test new subjects and sessions, remove the signal to see if performance collapses, control for scanner artifacts, and use tasks where the model cannot answer from familiar output patterns alone.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the internal tokens store scanner or subject artifacts, when pretty reconstructions hide weak biological tracking, or when the model can solve the task without using the measured signal.

Connection

Tell-Tale Norm treats hidden model state as a signal. VISUALSWAP asks which input channel actually carried evidence. Causal Route Gating asks which route produced the answer. Mind-Omni and PRISM ask the same question for brain and body measurements: did the biological signal really do the work?

EXEMPLAR 25

HDFlow, PACT, And BehaviorVLA

Embodied Action As Moves The World Allows

Problem

A robot can say "pick up the cup, place it on the shelf" and still fail. The gripper may not fit, the cup may slip, the shelf may be too high, or the robot may need to move around an obstacle first. In physical tasks, a plan is only real if every step can actually happen.

State Space

The state is what the robot can see, where objects are, what is touching what, what the goal is, which small actions are available, how the world changes after an action, and what feedback comes back during execution.

Object

The object is a map of moves the world actually allows. Each point is a physical situation. Each link is an action that can really move the world from one situation to the next. If there is no link, the robot cannot simply declare the move done. Name used later: feasible transition graph.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is executable progress toward the goal. What may change is the level of description: the robot can reason in words, subgoals, or motor commands, as long as each described move maps back to a possible physical move.

Allowed Movement

The policy may break a task into subgoals, reuse learned skills, or describe actions in language-like tokens. It cannot treat a label such as "grasp" as successful unless the hand, object, timing, and contact make that grasp possible.

Why It Works

This works by making a huge control problem smaller without lying about physics. Instead of planning every tiny motor value from scratch, the system plans through local moves that have already been checked or learned. The plain rule is that every chosen move must be a move the world can actually make. Names used later: dynamics for how the world changes after an action, and constraints for rules that make some changes impossible.

a plan is valid only if every step from state s_t through action a_t to s_{t+1} is physically allowed

Evidence Check

Report failed grasps, invalid subgoals, contact mistakes, transfer to new rooms or objects, recovery after bumps, and logs of actual state changes instead of only the written plan.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the abstract state leaves out contact, timing, weight, reach, or friction, when subgoal labels hide impossible actions, or when benchmarks grade descriptions instead of execution.

Connection

PACT makes allowed movement explicit. ReaSyn treats molecule building as a route that must be possible. Skill-Pro stores reusable procedures. Embodied models bring the same idea into the physical world: intelligence has to move through states the world permits.

EXEMPLAR 26

Protein Autoregressive Modeling And DeCoDe

Biological Generation As Respecting How Proteins Work

Problem

A protein is not useful just because its amino-acid string looks common. The string has to fold into a shape, the shape has to put the right chemical parts near each other, and the final molecule has to bind, stay stable, or perform a biological role. A next-token model can produce a likely-looking string that fails those deeper checks.

State Space

The state is the protein as a connected object: the sequence letters, the identity of each amino acid, the folded shape, nearby contacts, binding pocket or partner molecule, target property, and the experiment or simulation used to judge it.

Object

The object is the set of protein candidates that remain biologically plausible. The letter sequence is only one description of the candidate. Shape, chemistry, binding context, and test setting are also part of what makes it valid. Changing the string is allowed only if those deeper checks still make sense.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is functional plausibility: the candidate should still fold, bind, stay stable, or satisfy the intended role. What may change is the exact sequence or local structure, as long as those deeper biological requirements survive.

Allowed Movement

The model may mutate residues, fill in missing pieces, decode a structure into a sequence, or condition on a target. It should not wander freely through strings when that freedom breaks folding or chemistry.

Why It Works

This works by reducing a hopeless search. All possible protein strings are too many, and most are useless. Biology gives layers of rules: nearby chemicals have to fit, the chain has to fold, the folded shape has to expose the right parts, and the final candidate has to work in a real test. Using those layers narrows the search to candidates that have a reason to work. The model is still making new candidates, but it is making them inside a fenced-off region where biology has not already ruled them out. Name used later: constrained generative distribution.

generate sequence while fold, contacts, target property, and biological checks stay plausible

Evidence Check

Report fold consistency, preserved contacts, independent property predictions, binding or stability checks, novelty against known families, and lab or strong simulation evidence when claiming real biological function.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when likely-looking sequences are treated as valid proteins, when the constraint measures the wrong biological test, or when candidates fool a structure predictor without improving real function.

Connection

ReaSyn and MORetro* require molecule-building routes to be possible. Quotient-Space Diffusion removes irrelevant coordinate choices. IRNO preserves physical field structure. The shared move is to generate in the space where validity actually lives, not the space that is easiest to tokenize.

EXEMPLAR 27

Adam Degeneracy And Beyond Muon

Optimization As Choosing How The Model Is Allowed To Move

Problem

Training a model is like adjusting thousands of knobs at once. Two training methods can lower the error by the same amount while turning very different knobs. One may move gently in sensitive places; another may damage a useful internal feature while still making the average loss look good.

State Space

The state is the current knobs of the model, the direction suggested by the error signal, the optimizer's memory of past directions, which directions are sensitive, the next update, and how the model's useful internal features change. The immediate training push says which small move would lower the current error fastest. Name used later: gradient.

Object

The object is the training movement rule. It decides which directions are treated as cheap, which are treated as expensive, which pushes get amplified, and which pushes get suppressed. In plain terms, it is the optimizer's ruler for deciding how big a move really is. Name used later: optimizer-induced metric.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful progress: the model should reduce error without breaking important internal behavior. What may change is the size, angle, or scaling of each update.

Allowed Movement

The optimizer may shrink, stretch, rotate, normalize, or project the update. It should do that to move more in directions that help and less in directions where a small step can cause large damage. A sharp direction is a direction where the error changes quickly, so a tiny move can have a large effect.

Why It Works

This works when the movement rule makes an uneven training problem easier to walk through. If one direction is steep and another is flat, using the same step size everywhere is crude: the steep direction can overshoot while the flat direction barely moves. A better rule measures the ground first, then scales the step so useful movement is not blocked and fragile movement is not too large. Name used later: preconditioning, reshaping the update before applying it.

choose update delta so training error goes down while sensitive directions are not moved too far

Evidence Check

Measure error decrease per amount moved, whether updates point in helpful directions, sensitivity to sharp directions, what happens when optimizer memory is removed, and whether gains remain after matching compute, batch size, and learning-rate schedules.

Failure Boundary

The argument fails when the movement rule is fitting noise, when gains come only from better tuning, or when low training error hides drift in internal features needed for later tasks.

Connection

DiReCT chooses data by how it moves the model. LoRA theory asks whether a task can be learned by moving in a small set of directions. Trust-region RL limits policy movement. All say learning is not just "lower the loss"; it is controlled movement through the model.

EXEMPLAR 28

Transformer Circuits And Single-Head Attention

Architecture As Showing The Actual Computation

Problem

It is weak to say, "this model could solve the task." A calculator could in principle do many things, but we still need to know which buttons and operations produce the answer. For neural networks, the same issue appears: the architecture may be capable of a behavior, but we need to show the computation that actually creates it.

State Space

The state is how tokens are represented, which tokens look at which other tokens, what information gets copied or combined, how each layer changes the information, and what training data pushes those pieces into place. Attention scores are simply the model's learned "which previous pieces matter for this piece" weights.

Object

The object is a small working machine inside the larger network. It may assign items to groups, add evidence, compare two values, retrieve a memory, or repeat a simple update. Papers often call this an algorithmic circuit: a set of model parts that together carry out a recognizable procedure.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the input-to-output procedure. The exact weights can differ, but the model must still move information through the same kind of steps: read, combine, update, compare, and answer.

Allowed Movement

The analysis may ignore irrelevant weight details. It cannot ignore the structure needed to show how attention and other layers implement the claimed procedure with the actual model size, data, and numeric precision.

Why It Works

This works when each layer can be matched to a real step in the procedure. For clustering, one step can assign points to centers, another can combine points into summaries, and another can refine the assignment. For single-head attention, the question is whether one attention head can pick out the useful repeated pattern from random variation. The plain idea is like hearing a steady beat in a noisy room: repeated structure stands out because it keeps lining up, while accidental noise cancels or fades. Name used later: spectral filtering means keeping the repeated pattern that lines up across the data while letting accidental variation cancel out.

mechanism claim: the network's layer-by-layer map behaves like the claimed algorithm on the tested inputs
