Evidence Check

Show hand-built settings or probes of learned mechanisms, remove the proposed circuit pieces, test inputs that keep the same rule but change surface details, and separate "the network could do this" from "training actually made it do this."

Failure Boundary

The claim fails when the architecture could implement the algorithm but the trained model uses a shortcut, or when the demonstrated mechanism only works at a scale, precision, or data setting unlike the real model.

Connection

Language Generation in the Limit separates what is possible from what can be learned from finite data. Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates asks when the target signal is visible. Finite certification asks what evidence proves the claim. Together they connect capability, learning, and proof.

EXEMPLAR 29

Finite Test Certification And Micro-Benchmark Reliability

Benchmarks As A Limited Amount Of Evidence

Problem

A benchmark score can look like a fact about a model: "Model A is better than Model B." But the score came from a limited set of questions, graders, prompts, and random model outputs. A small score gap may be a real difference, or it may be luck from the sampled questions.

State Space

The state is the public claim, the larger task world the claim is about, the finite questions actually sampled, the model answers, the grading rule, the score calculation, and the remaining uncertainty. The benchmark questions are only a sample of the broader task world, the way a handful of exam questions stands in for a whole subject. Name used later: task distribution.

Object

The object is a measured claim with a doubt range. It should say not only the score, but how much evidence that score provides for the broader claim. A certificate here is not a badge of truth; it is a receipt saying what was checked, how much was checked, and how much uncertainty remains. Name used later: statistical certificate.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the comparison. If the leaderboard says A is better, that should reflect a real expected difference on the stated task world, not a lucky draw of items or graders.

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may sample fewer items, choose items adaptively, combine subtasks, or split the score into parts. It must still report what population the claim covers and how uncertain the measurement is.

Why It Works

This works by replacing a naked score with a score plus a doubt range. More items, cleaner graders, and more stable prompts shrink that range. A small benchmark does not become universal; it only tells us exactly how far the evidence reaches. Name used later: confidence interval, one way to state the remaining uncertainty around the measured score.

claim A beats B only when the measured gap is larger than the uncertainty from items, graders, prompts, and model randomness

Evidence Check

Report uncertainty ranges, rank stability after resampling, item-level variation, grader agreement, prompt sensitivity, model randomness, and whether the sampled tasks match the deployment claim.

Failure Boundary

The certificate fails when the benchmark is narrower than the public claim, when leaked items or biased grading distort the score, or when the uncertainty report ignores randomness from the model or evaluator.

Connection

ATLAS chooses questions that teach the most. FRABench separates score components. WebDevJudge and VERINA check artifacts directly. They all make evaluation more honest by saying what was actually measured and how much evidence it provides.

EXEMPLAR 30

Common Corpus And Hubble

Data As Training Evidence With Receipts

Problem

A model can improve after adding data, but the improvement may come from copied benchmark items, duplicated text, disallowed sources, or private material. If we do not know where the data came from, the score does not tell us what kind of evidence trained the behavior.

State Space

The state is the dataset with its source labels, licenses, collection dates, filters, duplicates, task labels, memorized spans, and downstream tests. A corpus is just the collected body of training text or examples.

Object

The object is not an anonymous pile of examples. It is a mix of sources, where each example carries both learning value and a record of where it came from. The source history matters because it lets later reviewers ask whether an example was allowed, duplicated, private, or copied from a test. Name used later: provenance.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is auditable training evidence. The pipeline may clean, filter, rebalance, or remove examples, but it should not erase the ability to answer: what did the model learn from, and was that source allowed?

Allowed Movement

The pipeline may remove duplicates, keep a subset, reweight sources, document sources, or exclude risky material. Those moves must preserve enough source history to support later claims about copied test items, privacy, rights, and model behavior.

Why It Works

This works because source identity turns a hidden training mixture into something inspectable. Once we know which source each example came from, we can ask whether that source helped, whether it copied test items, whether it caused memorization, and whether it was allowed. Name used later: source distribution, the recipe of source types and weights that make up the dataset.

train on a documented source mix, with checks for license, copied tests, duplication, and memorization risk

Evidence Check

Report source weights, filtering rules, duplicate rates, memorization tests, source-removal tests, contamination checks, license accounting, and score changes after removing high-risk sources.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when source records are incomplete, when metadata is trusted without audit, or when score gains come from copied or disallowed material that the benchmark mistakes for general ability.

Connection

Sequential Data Values and Accuracy Auctions ask how much each data source contributes. Copyright-Bench turns legal rules into action boundaries. Privacy and unlearning papers ask whether a source can be hidden or removed. All require data to have receipts.

EXEMPLAR 31

TileLang, DHSA, And einx

Systems Work As Doing The Same Math Where The Machine Can Reach It

Problem

A recipe can have few steps and still be slow if every step makes you walk across the kitchen for one spoon. Neural-network code has the same problem. Counting arithmetic operations misses the cost of moving numbers through memory, arranging them for the chip, and launching the small machine programs that do the work.

State Space

The state is the computation the model wants to do, the shapes of the number tables, how those tables are laid out in memory, which chunks are loaded together, which small hardware routine runs, what numeric precision is used, and which chip executes it. A tensor is just a table of numbers, and a kernel is a small low-level routine that runs one repeated piece of the computation.

Object

The object is the same calculation placed onto a real machine. Some numbers must be computed before others because later steps depend on earlier ones. That dependency map says which pieces can be moved around safely and which pieces must keep their order. Name used later: computation graph. The systems problem is to place the map into memory and hardware without changing the answer.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended function: for the same inputs, the model should produce the same output up to acceptable rounding. What may change is the order, grouping, layout, or low-level expression of the calculation.

Allowed Movement

The implementation may split tables into tiles, combine kernels, reorder independent work, specialize for common shapes, or write the same equation in another form. It cannot break dependencies, silently change rounding too much, or assume shapes that users will not actually run.

Why It Works

This works because modern chips are often limited by moving data, not by doing arithmetic. Tiling means keeping a small chunk of numbers close to the processor long enough to reuse it. Fusion means doing several operations while the numbers are already nearby. The machine has close, small, fast places to keep numbers and farther, larger, slower places; speed comes from using the close places well. Name used later: memory hierarchy.

keep the same tensor function while reducing expensive memory movement and unused hardware time

Evidence Check

Report low-level traces, memory bandwidth, hardware occupancy, shape sweeps, backend comparisons, numerical drift, full-model latency, and whether the speedup holds for the model sizes users actually serve.

Failure Boundary

The claim fails when it is fast only for cherry-picked shapes, when compilation overhead eats the win, when small numeric changes alter model behavior, or when one faster routine does not speed up the full pipeline.

Connection

LiftQuant and TetraJet change the numbers the machine stores. ThinKV changes what memory the model keeps. Finite-precision theory asks what computation survives machine limits. TileLang, DHSA, and einx make the machine part of the claim, not an afterthought.

EXEMPLAR 32

FOCUS And RePAIR

Pruning As Removing Parts Without Changing The Conversation Loop

Problem

Cutting parts out of a model can make it smaller while keeping short benchmark answers mostly right. But long generation is a loop: the model writes a token, then reads its own token as part of the next input. A tiny change can grow into repetition, topic collapse, or brittle answers after many steps.

State Space

The state is the full model, the smaller model, the weights or channels removed, the next-token choices over time, the generated context that feeds back into the model, and behavior measures such as repetition and drift.

Object

The object is a process that changes as it runs. The next state depends on the current state. In language generation, the next word becomes part of the future input, so the model is not just answering once; it is steering a sequence. Name used later: dynamical system, a self-feeding process where each step changes what the next step sees.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is behavior across a whole generated path, not only the first next-word score. What may change is the number of model parts, as long as repeated generation still stays stable.

Allowed Movement

The method may remove parameters, repair internal numbers, or adjust the smaller model. It must keep the next-step rule from creating traps such as repetition loops. That next-step rule turns the current context into chances for the next token, and then the chosen token becomes part of the next context. Name used later: transition kernel.

Why It Works

Repair works when it finds which removed parts used to keep the sequence stable and restores that effect. The issue is not one small error at one input. The issue is whether that error keeps feeding itself until the model falls into a bad loop.

audit whether small next-token changes in the pruned model accumulate into repeated or collapsed states

Evidence Check

Measure repetition rates, long-generation drift, diversity over time, topic collapse, recovery after repair, layer-removal tests, and cases where the smaller model matches short scores but behaves differently in long outputs.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when repair fixes obvious repetition but misses other drift, when tests are too short to reveal the loop, or when pruning removes rare abilities that matter for safety or reliability.

Connection

Quantized diffusion studies small errors that accumulate along a sampling path. LiftQuant studies machine-number errors. RAGEN-2 studies reasoning traces collapsing during training. FOCUS and RePAIR make the same point for compressed language models: preserve the running process, not just the static score.

EXEMPLAR 33

VALUEFLOW And Accuracy Auctions

Governance As Rules That Change How People Act

Problem

If a system pays people for data, ranks people by a score, or turns preferences into policy, people will react to the rule. They may report honestly, exaggerate, hide information, coordinate with others, or change behavior to get a better outcome. An average reward can hide who benefits and who pays.

State Space

The state is the people or groups involved, what each knows privately, what they report, what data they contribute, how the system allocates benefits or payments, what fairness or welfare goal is claimed, and what constraints must be respected.

Object

The object is the rule connecting reports to outcomes. It says what happens when people report data, make claims, bid, rank, or reveal preferences. Name used later: mechanism, a rule that turns people's reports or contributions into payments, rankings, allocations, or policy choices.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimacy after people respond to incentives. A result is not meaningful if it works only when everyone behaves in a way the rule itself discourages.

Allowed Movement

The designer may average, weight, price, restrict, or redistribute outcomes. The page has to say which property is being protected: honest reporting, fair treatment, efficient allocation, total welfare, or protection for a subgroup.

Why It Works

This works when the rule makes useful behavior the sensible behavior. If honesty gives people worse outcomes than manipulation, the system will not get honest information. The point is to design the rule while accounting for how participants will try to improve their own outcomes under that rule. Name used later: mechanism design.

choose a rule where the useful or truthful action is the best response under the stated assumptions

Evidence Check

Report simulations where participants game the rule, subgroup benefit and harm curves, collusion checks, sensitivity to strong preferences, payment robustness, and examples where improving one group hurts another.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when real incentives are outside the model, when one welfare number hides who is harmed, or when the assumed private information does not match what people actually know or control.

Connection

ParetoPO exposes cost tradeoffs. SafeDPO exposes safety-usefulness tradeoffs. Data markets price evidence. Fair Optimal Transport constrains allocation. Governance papers add the human layer: the measurement rule changes the system being measured.

EXEMPLAR 34

LIMSSR And Robust Missing-Covariate Optimization

Decision Making When Important Facts Are Missing

Problem

A doctor, lender, or robot often has to decide with incomplete information. The missing fact may be exactly the fact that matters. If a model silently fills it in and acts confident, the decision can look precise while resting on a guess.

State Space

The state is the full set of facts that would matter if we had them, the subset we actually see, the pattern of what is missing, any guessed value, the decision rule, the score, and the uncertainty about unseen possibilities. A background fact may change what decision is fair or accurate, even if that fact is not the main thing being predicted. Name used later: covariate.

Object

The object is a decision made from partial evidence. It should use what is visible while keeping track of what could still be true in the hidden part. The decision is tied to the exact slice of facts the system can see, so two people with different missing facts may need different uncertainty, not the same confident score. Name used later: observed-subset decision problem.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is honest uncertainty about the missing part. The model may infer likely hidden facts, but it should not turn a guess into unsupported certainty.

Allowed Movement

The method may encode what is visible, average over possible missing values, protect against bad hidden cases, or produce a conditional score. The final decision must remain valid for the missingness pattern it claims to handle. Imputation means filling in a missing value; robust means the decision is tested against more than one possible fill-in.

Why It Works

This works by separating evidence from completion. The observed facts say what is known. The missing facts define a range of possible worlds. A careful decision asks what action is still reasonable across that range, rather than pretending the most convenient completion is true.

choose an action that performs acceptably over the plausible hidden states consistent with the observed facts

Evidence Check

Report tests with structured missing facts, calibration when a whole input type is absent, fill-in ablations, worst-case loss, shifts in missingness patterns, and cases where the model abstains or widens uncertainty instead of inventing certainty.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when missingness is treated as harmless random absence but is actually tied to the outcome, when the uncertainty range leaves out realistic hidden states, or when the model learns shortcuts from dataset-specific missing markers.

Connection

VISUALSWAP asks which input channel carries evidence. Local Covariate Selection asks which background facts make a comparison fair. Mind-Omni asks whether a biological signal channel is real. Missingness work asks the same question when the channel is absent.

EXEMPLAR 35

Copyright-Bench And Legal Agents

Law As Knowing Which Helpful Moves Are Allowed

Problem

An agent can be useful in a way that is not allowed. It may quote too much, transform protected material in the wrong setting, give legal-sounding advice without enough context, or refuse everything near the boundary. The hard part is not "be helpful" or "be safe" in general; it is choosing the right move for the specific content and rule.

State Space

The state is the user's request, the source material, the relevant rule or policy, the action the agent wants to take, the final output, whether it refuses or complies, and the risk label for that situation.

Object

The object is the set of responses the rule still permits. Each possible answer is allowed, forbidden, or close enough to the line that it needs extra care. The rule works like a boundary around the menu of possible actions: after forbidden actions are removed, the model should choose the best remaining useful action. Name used later: constrained action set.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is usefulness within the rule. The system should not become helpful by crossing the legal boundary, and it should not become "safe" by refusing every hard but allowed case.

Allowed Movement

The agent may summarize, transform, cite, ask for clarification, refuse, or offer an allowed alternative. The right choice depends on the source, the user's request, the jurisdiction or policy, and the kind of transformation.

Why It Works

This works when evaluation turns vague legal pressure into concrete boundary examples. The model has to learn two things: first, whether a response is allowed for this source, request, and rule; second, what useful action remains if the first choice is not allowed. The checker is really asking whether a candidate answer sits inside or outside the allowed region. Name used later: feasible-region classifier.

choose the most useful response among the responses allowed for this source, request, and rule

Evidence Check

Report false refusals, false allowances, near-boundary examples, sensitivity to source length, transformation type, citation behavior, and prompts that ask for the same content in different wording.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when labels are too coarse, when legal context is missing, when generic refusal is rewarded too much, or when the model handles obvious examples but fails nearby variants.

Connection

CSPO and SafeDPO also restrict model movement. Common Corpus makes source rights part of data state. WebDevJudge checks real artifacts. Legal-agent work adds a social rule layer: some outputs are unavailable even if the model can produce them.

EXEMPLAR 36

VideoKR And Visual Attribution Streaming

Long Video As Remembering The Frame That Matters

Problem

A long video may answer a question in one brief moment: someone picks up a key, a car changes lanes, a patient shows a symptom, or an object moves behind a door. A model can sound right by using captions or nearby frames while missing the actual moment that supports the answer.

State Space

The state is the stream of frames, events, tracked objects, the user's question, the memory kept by the model, the evidence retrieved later, the path from evidence to answer, and the answer itself.

Object

The object is a memory of visual evidence across time. It should keep the frame or event that can change the answer. The memory is not a scrapbook of every frame; it is a compressed record of what happened, in what order, and which moments may matter later. Name used later: temporal evidence state.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is answer-relevant visual evidence. The model may discard most frames, but not the rare event that decides the question.

Allowed Movement

The method may split the video into events, retrieve old frames, mark which frames mattered, summarize, or update memory as frames arrive. It must keep the time links that matter and show where the answer came from.

Why It Works

This works when memory keeps enough evidence for future questions and the attribution points to frames that actually affect the answer. Attribution means assigning credit to the input parts that caused the output. A good test removes or swaps the alleged supporting event and checks whether the answer changes.

keep video memory that changes the answer when the supporting event is removed or swapped

Evidence Check

Use delayed-evidence questions, frame swaps, event deletion, checks that attribution is faithful, memory-budget curves, question-conditioned retrieval audits, and cases where text priors conflict with what the video shows.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when it highlights plausible-looking frames rather than answer-causing frames, keeps common events while dropping rare decisive events, or answers benchmark questions without needing the video.

Connection

ThinKV compresses text context. VISUALSWAP tests which input channel carried evidence. DLMR tracks memory sources. VideoKR and streaming attribution combine all three: long memory, visual grounding, and evidence route.

EXEMPLAR 37

SAW-Bench And RoboMME

Embodied Benchmarks As Measuring Whether The World Changed

Problem

A benchmark can ask a robot question without testing robotic ability. If the score rewards a written plan but never checks perception, contact, motion, or the final world state, it can make a model look embodied while measuring language only.

State Space

The state is the environment, what the agent perceives, the instruction, the action sequence, the intermediate world states, the success condition, and the trace of what failed.

Object

The object is a task attempt over time, not a static answer. The score should watch what the agent saw, what it tried, how the world changed, and where the attempt ended. The important evidence is the path itself: situation, action, next situation, and so on until success or failure. Name used later: trajectory.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is physical task success: did perception support the right action, did the action change the world, and did the final state satisfy the goal?

Allowed Movement

The evaluator may split the task into parts, use simulation, or include images and language. The score must still depend on actual state changes, not only whether the plan sounds plausible.

Why It Works

This works because physical failures become visible only when the attempt is followed through time. The agent may choose an object it cannot reach, lose contact, treat a closed drawer like an open one, or fail to recover after a bump. Affordance means an action the environment makes possible, such as a handle being graspable.

score the full state-action path, including whether each action caused the needed world change

Evidence Check

Report state-transition logs, contact or affordance failures, simulator-to-real sensitivity, input ablations, recovery after perturbations, and disagreement between written-plan score and executed success.

Failure Boundary

The benchmark fails when environments are too scripted, when answers can be inferred from text alone, when perception artifacts leak labels, or when physical limits are absent from the score.

Connection

HDFlow and PACT constrain action steps. BehaviorVLA learns reusable behavior. WebDevJudge checks web artifacts in their native environment. Embodied benchmarks apply the same principle to physical action: check the thing in the medium where it can fail.

EXEMPLAR 38

Language Generation In The Limit

Formal Possibility Versus Evidence That Can Find It

Problem

A lock may have a correct key, but that does not mean you can find it by trying a few keys. A model may be capable of representing a language rule, but realistic training data may not contain enough evidence to identify that rule.

State Space

The state is the class of possible languages, the process generating examples, the learner, the candidate rule the learner believes, the error standard, and the amount of data available. A formal language is a precisely defined set of allowed strings.

Object

The object is the amount of evidence needed before learning is possible. If many different rules fit the examples seen so far, the learner has not been given enough evidence to know which rule is right. The question is not just whether a rule exists; it is how many examples are needed before the wrong rules can be ruled out. Name used later: sample complexity.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the distinction between "there exists a rule" and "the learner can find the rule from the available data." Possible in principle is not the same as learnable from evidence.

Allowed Movement

The theorem may choose the language family, the learner's access to data, the success condition, and the kind of convergence. It must state which evidence setting it is talking about and what generation guarantee is being claimed.

Why It Works

The argument works by showing that many different rules can agree on the examples seen so far while disagreeing later. If the data cannot separate those rules, no learner can know which one is right within that budget. Expressivity means the model can represent a rule; learnability means evidence can select it.

finite samples cannot identify the target if many possible languages match the samples but differ elsewhere

Evidence Check

State the language family, what the learner observes, the success criterion, sampling assumptions, lower-bound example, and whether the impossibility is about worst-case formal classes or practical data with extra structure.

Failure Boundary

The warning is overused when a worst-case barrier is applied to narrower real settings where structure makes learning easier. It is underused when architecture capability is advertised as if it already solved the evidence problem.

Connection

Transformer Circuits shows possible computations. Finite Test Certification asks what finite evidence proves. ATLAS treats evaluation as measurement. Language Generation in the Limit supplies the missing caution: a representable rule is not necessarily a learnable rule.

EXEMPLAR 39

The Tell-Tale Norm

Internal Magnitude As A Sign Of When To Think More

Problem

Some questions are easy and some need extra work. Spending extra computation on every question wastes time, but never spending it misses hard cases. The question is whether the model has an internal sign that says, "this is one of the cases where more thinking may help."

State Space

The state is the prompt, the model's internal numbers at each layer, the size of those internal numbers, the rule for trying more computation, the candidate answers, and final answer quality. Activations are the internal numbers a layer produces; a norm is a single size measure for a group of numbers.

Object

The object is a measured internal sign used for a decision. It is not the reasoning itself. It is more like a dashboard light that may tell us when the model is uncertain, effortful, or in a state where extra computation is valuable. The number comes from hidden model state, so it must earn trust by predicting real improvement, not by sounding psychologically meaningful. Name used later: latent-state statistic.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful information about effort or uncertainty. If the internal size changes, it should track real reasoning demand or confidence, not answer length, topic, style, or refusal wording.

Allowed Movement

The system may run another reasoning pass, steer the model, sample another answer, or choose among answers based on that internal sign. Those interventions must improve real tasks at the same compute budget, not merely make the model look more thoughtful.

Why It Works

This works only if the internal sign predicts the value of extra computation. If high norm means "extra work is likely to reduce error," then compute can be spent selectively. The signal must still help after ordinary clues like prompt length, topic, and answer style are already considered. In plain terms, the hidden sign must tell us something we did not already know from the outside of the prompt. Name used later: conditional information.

spend extra compute when the internal signal predicts enough error reduction to justify the cost

Evidence Check

Measure which layers carry the signal, whether norm predicts errors, improvement per extra compute, off-target effects, stability under prompt changes, and whether norm-guided extra work beats giving every question the same extra work.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the norm reflects verbosity, domain, fake uncertainty, or prompt artifacts instead of useful reasoning state, or when the intervention pushes the model into internal states it was not trained to handle.

Connection

Steer Like the LLM changes internal directions. RAGEN-2 checks whether traces depend on the input. Mind-Omni asks whether biological internal signals carry evidence. All turn hidden state into something measurable, then require proof that the measurement actually matters.

EXEMPLAR 40

h1 And OpenThoughts

Long Reasoning As Teaching Short Links Before Long Chains

Problem

Long reasoning is hard because the final answer may depend on many earlier steps. If the model only hears "right" or "wrong" at the end, it may not know which earlier step helped or failed. The learning signal becomes weak by the time it reaches the beginning of the chain.

State Space

The state is the set of smaller subtasks, the intermediate conclusions, training examples, reasoning traces, how many steps are chained together, the feedback signal, and the updates made to the model. A dependency means one step must be correct because a later step uses it.

Object

The object is a chain of dependent steps. Each intermediate piece of work becomes an input to something later, so a small early mistake can poison the rest of the chain. The useful map shows the pieces and which later pieces depend on them. Name used later: task graph.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is dependency correctness. A short-step skill must remain useful when it becomes part of a longer problem; otherwise the long trace is just more text, not more reasoning.

Allowed Movement

The curriculum may start with short chains, increase the length, combine examples, or use reasoning traces. It must preserve the real dependencies that make the task longer, not just add verbose filler.

Why It Works

This works like teaching arithmetic before algebra word problems. The model first learns local moves where feedback is close to the action. Then the curriculum composes those moves into longer chains. The hard part is knowing which earlier step helped or hurt the final result once many steps are chained together. Name used later: credit assignment.

learn short dependable steps first, then increase chain length while feedback still identifies which step mattered

Evidence Check

Run tests by chain length, scramble dependencies, perturb traces, test transfer from short to long compositions, compare sparse versus step-level feedback, and check that long traces actually use earlier intermediate results.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when long tasks share shallow templates, when the model memorizes trace style, or when short-step skill breaks once later steps interact with it.

Connection

Midtraining Bridges chooses when data should enter training. Skill-Pro turns successful traces into reusable procedures. DiReCT chooses examples by how they move the model. h1 and OpenThoughts make the reasoning-side lesson explicit: make the learning path navigable.

EXEMPLAR 41

Omni-Reward And JudgeBench

Reward Models As Scores That Must Still Mean Quality

Problem

Quality is messy. A judge model turns it into a number so another model can train against it. The danger is that the model learns how to get a high number without improving the thing people actually wanted, such as truth, usefulness, safety, or visual correctness.

State Space

The state is the answer or artifact, the real quality we care about, what the judge can observe, the reward score, the training process that optimizes the score, the evidence in each input type, and behavior after optimization.

Object

The object is a noisy measurement of quality. The real target may be truth, usefulness, safety, or visual correctness, but the training loop sees an easier stand-in score. That score is like a thermometer: it is useful only while it still rises when the real thing rises. If the score goes up while real quality goes down, training is now improving the measuring device's blind spot instead of the target. Names used later: proxy is the stand-in measurement, and reward channel is the path from real quality, through judge observations, into the number used for training.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the link between higher reward and real improvement. What may change is the output style, ranking, or training path, as long as the reward still tracks the target under pressure.

Allowed Movement

The system may train, rank, filter, or guide outputs using reward scores. But the score must be audited after optimization, because that is when the model has the strongest incentive to exploit judge blind spots.

Why It Works

Reward modeling works when the score keeps the same order as real quality in the region the trained model visits. If better answers usually get higher scores, the reward is useful. If high-scoring answers become glossy but false, the reward has broken. The danger appears after training pushes the model into answer styles the judge was not originally tested on. Name used later: distribution shift means the model has moved into cases that differ from the cases where the measurement was known to work.

use reward only while higher reward still means higher target quality on optimized outputs

Evidence Check

Measure reward-target disagreement, judge calibration, reward hacking, audits after optimization, dependence on visual or other non-text evidence, pairwise consistency, and human review of high-reward boundary cases.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the reward sees surface fluency instead of target quality, ignores important input channels, or drives the model into cases where the judge is confidently wrong.

Connection

RLVepsR studies verifier labels as noisy training pressure. WebDevJudge checks real artifacts. Safety-Guided Flow uses reward while sampling. Omni-Reward and JudgeBench name the central risk: the training objective is only as real as the measurement behind it.

EXEMPLAR 42

OPUS And HOBIT

Data Selection As Choosing Examples By What They Do To The Model

Problem

An example is not valuable by itself. It is valuable because training on it changes the model. The same example can teach something new early, become redundant later, or push the model in a harmful direction after the model has already learned related material.

State Space

The state is the current model, candidate examples, the update each example would cause, the optimizer's memory, the validation target, sensitive directions in the model, and downstream performance. Each example creates a training push: a direction it wants to move the model so the model handles that example better. Name used later: a gradient is that training push written as a direction for changing the model.

Object

The object is the extra value of training on one example now. An example matters if its update teaches something the current model still needs and does not cause too much damage elsewhere. The value is not a permanent label on the example; it depends on what the model already knows and what goal the next update is supposed to improve. Name used later: marginal update value means the extra improvement caused by adding this example at this moment.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful model movement per unit of data or compute. Selected examples should push the model toward the target without causing too much damage elsewhere.

Allowed Movement

The method may rank, filter, weight, or order examples. The decision should be based on the actual update the training rule will make, not just whether the example looks similar, hard, rare, or semantically relevant.

Why It Works

This works because training depends on the path already taken. A lesson helps only relative to what the student currently knows. Optimizer-aware data selection estimates how an example will move the current model, then keeps examples whose movement helps the validation goal. Some parts of the model are fragile: a tiny push there can change behavior a lot, while the same-sized push elsewhere barely matters. Useful examples must therefore be judged by both benefit and damage risk. Name used later: curvature or sensitivity means how strongly behavior changes when the model is nudged in a particular direction.

select examples whose predicted update improves the validation target while limiting off-target movement

Evidence Check

Report whether predicted updates match actual validation gains, phase sensitivity, redundancy tests, comparison to static filters, off-target loss, and whether selected examples remain useful under optimizer or learning-rate changes.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when update estimates are too noisy, when validation targets are too narrow, when selected examples overfit a temporary training phase, or when the optimizer used for scoring differs from the optimizer used for training.

Connection

DiReCT chooses data by how it moves the model. Midtraining Bridges chooses when data should enter. Common Corpus tracks where data came from. OPUS and HOBIT complete the data story by asking what each example does to the model right now.

EXEMPLAR 43

Gaussian Certified Unlearning

Deletion As Making Two Histories Look The Same

Problem

When someone asks for their data to be removed, we cannot open a trained model and see a labeled drawer for that example. The model may look changed for unrelated reasons, or it may look unchanged while still leaking something about the removed data.

State Space

The state is the original training data, the deletion request, the original training method, the unlearning method, a reference model retrained without the deleted data, the released model, what an auditor or attacker can see, and the test used to compare the two worlds.

Object

The object is a comparison between two histories: one where the data was never used, and one where it was used and then removed. Each history can lead to many possible model outputs because training has randomness and the model can answer in different ways. The question is whether those two clouds of possible outputs overlap so much that an observer cannot tell which history happened. Name used later: distribution.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of deletion. After unlearning, an observer should not be able to reliably tell which history happened, within the access rules the guarantee claims.

Allowed Movement

The unlearning method may update, perturb, or randomize the model. It may add noise. It may lose some usefulness. But the released model must behave close to a model that was properly trained without the deleted data.

Why It Works

This works when noise and updates limit what an observer can learn about the deleted example. The guarantee does not prove the model has human-like forgetting. It proves the two histories are hard to tell apart: the observer should not be able to tell whether the data was never used or was used and then removed. The proof is about tests, not feelings of forgetting: no allowed test should reliably separate the two output clouds. Name used later: statistical indistinguishability.

unlearning is certified when the released model is hard to distinguish from retraining without the deleted data

Evidence Check

Report what the attacker can observe, how close the two histories are, utility loss, deletion batch size, dimension effects, repeated deletion behavior, and membership or reconstruction attacks.

Failure Boundary

The deletion guarantee fails when attackers see more than the guarantee assumed, when the added noise is too small for large models with many internal numbers, or when preserving usefulness forces the method to keep data-specific traces.

Connection

Common Corpus tracks where data came from. Watermarking wants selected traces to remain detectable. Privacy wants private traces to be hard to detect. Unlearning is the deletion version: prove a source no longer leaves an auditable difference.

EXEMPLAR 44

Catch-22 And Watermarking

Watermarking As Hiding A Mark That Still Survives Editing

Problem

A watermark has to do three things at once: let the owner detect it, survive edits like paraphrasing or cropping, and stay hard for an attacker to find and remove. Those goals conflict. A stronger mark is easier to detect, but also easier to notice.

State Space

The state is the generated text, image, or audio; the secret key; the rule that places the mark; the detector; the edits or attacks applied later; the unmarked case; the marked case; and the cutoff for saying "mark found."

Object

The object is a test between two explanations: this artifact came from the marked generator, or it did not. The detector looks at evidence in the artifact and chooses the explanation that better fits. The key question is how often the detector falsely accuses clean artifacts and how often it misses marked ones after normal edits. Name used later: hypothesis test.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is reliable origin evidence. The mark must leave enough signal to be found after ordinary edits, while not damaging quality or making removal easy.

Allowed Movement

The system may slightly change word choices, image statistics, internal features, or signatures. The artifact should still look and work normally, and the test must match the attacks it claims to survive.

Why It Works

This works when the detector measures a pattern that appears much more often in marked artifacts than unmarked ones, even after edits. It breaks when editing blurs that pattern or when the pattern is visible enough for attackers to erase. The detector finally reduces the pattern to a number and compares that number with a cutoff. Name used later: detection statistic.

detect a mark when the measured pattern is common after marked generation and rare without it

Evidence Check

Report false alarms, missed marks, survival under attacks, quality loss, risk that the secret key is exposed, adaptive removal tests, shifts to new data, and detection after paraphrase, crop, compression, or regeneration.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when robustness makes the mark visible to attackers, when stealth makes detection too weak, or when evaluation uses easier edits than realistic laundering.

Connection

Gaussian unlearning tries to make two histories hard to distinguish. Watermarking tries to make selected origins distinguishable. SecFid studies security versus normal-use quality. Common Corpus asks whether origin can be audited before generation. All are evidence tests with different desired mistakes.

EXEMPLAR 45

Fair Optimal Transport

Fairness As Choosing Allowed Matches Before Minimizing Cost

Problem

Suppose a system assigns people to schools, jobs, loans, or services. The cheapest matching can still place most burden on one group. If fairness is checked only after the cheapest match is chosen, the unfair choices were already allowed during the search.

State Space

The state is the people or items being assigned, the destinations or outcomes, the cost of each possible match, group labels or protected structure, the full matching table, fairness rules, and final allocation.

Object

The object is a matching table: how much of each source goes to each target. In allocation, that might mean which people get which opportunities or burdens. The table is the real decision object because it decides who is paired with what, how much cost they carry, and whether the fairness rule is obeyed before cost is minimized. Names used later: transport plan for the full movement table, and coupling for the pairing table that says which source is linked to which target and how much.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful allocation with fairness meaning. The plan should still match well, but it must not concentrate cost, harm, or benefit in a forbidden pattern.

Allowed Movement

The method may change pairings, move more or less amount through different matches, or smooth the plan. It may only choose among matching tables that satisfy the stated fairness rule and still allocate the required total amounts.

Why It Works

This works because the fairness rule is placed on the object that creates outcomes: the matching table itself. Instead of choosing an unfair plan and trying to patch it later, the method removes unfair plans from the candidate set before optimizing cost.

choose the lowest-cost matching among matchings that satisfy the fairness rule

Evidence Check

Report cost-versus-fairness curves, subgroup outcomes, sensitivity to the fairness rule, feasibility under data shifts, comparison to after-the-fact repair, and which harm the rule actually prevents.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when group labels are incomplete, when the fairness rule misses the real harm, or when small data shifts make every fair plan impossible or too costly.

Connection

CSPO restricts model movement. VALUEFLOW shows welfare tradeoffs. ParetoPO shows cost-accuracy tradeoffs. Fair Optimal Transport applies the same first principle to allocation: decide which moves are allowed before choosing the cheapest one.

EXEMPLAR 46

Mamba Markov In-Context Learning

In-Context Learning As Keeping A Running Summary Of The Task

Problem

A model that reads examples one after another cannot keep an unlimited prompt word for word. To learn from the examples in the prompt, it needs to keep a compact running summary of what task seems to be happening.

State Space

The state is the sequence of examples, the model's running internal memory, the rule for updating that memory, the hidden task the examples reveal, noise in the examples, and the next prediction.

Object

The object is the running summary that is enough for the next prediction. It should keep the task pattern revealed by earlier examples without storing every example word for word. Enough means the next prediction from the summary should match the prediction the model would make if it reread the useful parts of the full context. Step-by-step means the summary is revised after each new example. Name used later: a sufficient recurrent statistic is a running memory that keeps exactly the task information needed for future predictions.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is task information from the context. The model may forget exact examples, but it should keep the pattern that determines how to answer the next one. After each example, the model's belief about the hidden task should become better informed. Name used later: posterior means the revised belief after seeing the examples so far.

Allowed Movement

The model may update its compact memory after each example. The update must keep evidence useful for future predictions and avoid confusing irrelevant order or surface features with the real task pattern.

Why It Works

This works when the hidden memory behaves like a running estimate of the task. Each new example slightly changes the model's belief about what rule the prompt is showing. The summary is good only if it keeps everything the future prediction needs; then the raw past can be compressed without losing the task. Name used later: Markov structure means the current running state carries the needed past, so the next prediction does not need the full old sequence again.

a running state is good when predictions from that state match predictions from the full context

Evidence Check

Test delayed evidence, order sensitivity, memory-size limits, tasks where the current state should or should not be enough, longer prompts, update-rule ablations, and whether hidden memory recovers the task pattern.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the task cannot be compressed into the assumed summary, when early irrelevant examples overwrite later evidence, or when long contexts require direct retrieval instead of a running memory.

Connection

ThinKV keeps sufficient context tokens. VideoKR stores visual evidence over time. Skill-Pro compresses agent traces into procedures. Mamba Markov ICL gives the theory-side version of the same question: what state is enough?

EXEMPLAR 47

ThunderAgent, Speculative Actions, And ADP

Agent Latency As Doing Safe Future Work Early

Problem

Agents are often slow because the time is not only in writing text. They call tools, wait for websites, retrieve files, check results, and update external state. Making token generation faster does not fix a workflow where the slow parts are outside the model.

State Space

The state is the workflow: model calls, likely next branches, tool state, side effects, checker gates, cached context, and reusable traces. An execution graph is a map of which work depends on which earlier work.

Object

The object is a workflow with some steps that can happen early and some that must wait. If two steps do not depend on each other, they can run in parallel. Partially ordered means not every step has one strict order; only the true dependencies have to be respected.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the correct task state. The system may prepare likely future work, but it must not commit an irreversible tool action or reuse stale information that changes the final result.

Allowed Movement

The system may predict future actions, prefetch data, run branches in parallel, cache results, or normalize traces. It should do this only when a checker can accept or reject the early work before it affects the official state.

Why It Works

This works when the time saved on accepted early work is larger than the time wasted on rejected work plus checking overhead. Speculative execution means preparing a likely branch before knowing for sure it will be needed, then keeping it only if later evidence agrees.

speculate only when likely saved time exceeds wrong-branch waste plus checker cost

Evidence Check

Report accepted speculation rate, wrong-branch cost, side-effect safety, stale-state failures, end-to-end latency, task success under concurrency, and whether trace reuse preserves debugging information.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when speculative actions have irreversible side effects, branch prediction is weak, checker costs are too high, or normalized traces remove state needed for correctness later.

Connection

ParetoPO measures tool cost. Skill-Pro reuses verified procedures. WebDevJudge checks native execution. ThunderAgent and speculative-action work make agent speed a workflow scheduling problem, not just a UI latency problem.

EXEMPLAR 48

RAIN-Merging, FlexRank, And LoRA Geometry

Adaptation As Adding A Skill Without Moving Through Fragile Parts

Problem

A model can learn a new task and accidentally damage an old ability, a safety habit, or a reasoning style. The damage is often not random. It happens because the update moved through parts of the model that were carrying something important.

State Space

The state is the base model, update directions from different tasks, adapter pieces, the budget for how many directions can change, fragile feature directions, target behavior, and tests for retained behavior.

Object

The object is the allowed set of ways the model may change. Instead of letting every internal knob move freely, the method limits the update to a smaller family of directions. The hope is that the new skill can be added through those directions while fragile old behavior is left mostly untouched. Names used later: subspace for the smaller family of allowed directions, and low rank for building the change from a limited number of direction patterns.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful old behavior while new behavior is added. The model may change on target tasks, but it should avoid directions that carry general reasoning, safety, or other retained capabilities.

Allowed Movement

The method may merge task updates, project away risky movement, choose rank, or factor adapters. Movement through fragile directions must be measured, limited, or justified.

Why It Works

This works when useful new-task movement can be separated from movement that damages retained behavior. Rank controls how many direction patterns the update can use. Projection removes or reduces movement in directions believed to be risky. Together they decide how much learning capacity to spend and where the update is allowed to point.

choose an update that improves the target task while keeping movement in protected directions small

Evidence Check

Report how performance changes with rank, tests that remove fragile directions, retained reasoning behavior, task-vector projections, interference between merged models, and comparisons against unconstrained tuning at matched capacity.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when fragile directions are identified incorrectly, when the new task also needs those protected directions, or when low-rank limits hide necessary learning rather than making it safe.

Connection

DiReCT avoids sensitive data-induced movement. Adam-style optimizer work chooses how updates are measured. CSPO restricts safety movement. RAIN-Merging, FlexRank, and LoRA put the same idea inside adaptation and merging: change only through acceptable directions.

EXEMPLAR 49

Continual VLA, Skill Neologisms, GEPA, And DTO-KD

Continual Updates As Learning New Skills Without Erasing Old Ones

Problem

A deployed system may need to keep learning. But every new lesson can overwrite an old one. For agents and robots, that can look like a lost skill, an unsafe action, or a broken workflow, not just a worse score.

State Space

The state is old behavior, new task data, saved examples for rehearsal, learned control tokens for skills, prompt variants, teacher signals, task signals, and tests on both old and new tasks.

Object

The object is the path of behavior change over time. The system has to remember old skills and still make room for new ones. Stability means old abilities stay available. Plasticity means the system can still learn new abilities. The stability-plasticity tradeoff is the tension between those two needs.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is prior capability while new behavior is added. The model may change, but it should not cross into forgetting, unsafe drift, or blind imitation of a flawed teacher.

Allowed Movement

The method may replay old examples, add soft control tokens, search prompt variants, or rebalance teacher training. Replay means rehearsing old cases so they remain represented. Distillation means learning from a teacher model's outputs or signals.

Why It Works

This works when old evidence remains in the training loop and new updates are kept local. Replay reminds the model what not to forget. Skill tokens limit when a new behavior turns on. Searching across prompts preserves alternatives. Rebalancing teacher and task signals prevents one source from taking over.

learn the new task while bounding the loss of old-task behavior and limiting conflict between update signals

Evidence Check

Measure forgetting curves, replay coverage, whether skill tokens stay local, new-task gain, old-task retention, update-signal agreement, prompt diversity, and embodied failures after updates.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when replay misses rare old skills, soft tokens activate outside their valid setting, prompt search overfits a small rollout set, or distillation preserves teacher errors instead of useful knowledge.

Connection

Skill-Pro creates reusable procedures. HDFlow and PACT define physically allowed action steps. RAIN-Merging restricts update directions. Continual VLA, Skill Neologisms, GEPA, and DTO-KD ask how a deployed system can keep changing without losing its identity.

EXEMPLAR 50

Latent Hawkes, OU Identifiability, And Power-Law Discovery

Temporal Data As Asking What Process Made The Pattern

Problem

If one event often comes before another, it is tempting to say the first caused the second. But time order alone can mislead. Both events may be driven by a hidden process, the sampling rate may miss events, or the system may change its rules over time.

State Space

The state is the observed time series or event stream, unseen subprocesses, the rule by which events trigger later events, background drift, measurement noise, possible deliberate changes, and the time scale of observation.

Object

The object is the process rule that created the time pattern. The question is whether the observations narrow the story enough to say which rule made the pattern. Identifiable means the available observations are strong enough to pin down that rule, rather than leaving several different process stories equally possible.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the process rule. The inferred relation should describe a stable process, not just a correlation that happened under one sampling schedule.

Allowed Movement

The method may use event paths, averages of time patterns, unseen factors, repeated patterns at different scales, or deliberate changes to the system. It must say which process stories can actually be separated by the observations. Names used later: a latent variable is an unseen factor, a spectral feature is a repeated pattern pulled out of a signal, and an intervention is a deliberate change used to test what follows.

Why It Works

This works when the process model adds structure that plain correlation lacks. Hawkes-style models treat one event as temporarily increasing the chance of later events. OU-style models describe noisy movement that tends to drift back toward a stable level. Power-law discovery looks for patterns that keep the same shape across small and large scales. The common point is to use time structure to narrow which process rule could have produced the data.

a mechanism is identifiable when the observed time patterns rule out competing process rules under stated assumptions

Evidence Check

Report sensitivity to time scale, hidden-process stress tests, checks on time-pattern averages, intervention validation, diagnostics for changing rules over time, and whether the inferred process predicts under shifted timing regimes.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the assumed process family is wrong, hidden drivers make several stories indistinguishable, sampling frequency merges separate events, or the process is treated as stable when it changes over time.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence warns when several causal stories fit the same data. IRNO treats physical dynamics as stable rules. Mamba Markov ICL compresses temporal evidence into state. These temporal-discovery papers ask when time reveals a mechanism rather than only a pattern.

EXEMPLAR 51

FlashWorld And VectorWorld

World Models As Worlds That Change Because Of Actions

Problem

A generated driving scene or game world can look believable one frame at a time and still ignore the action that was taken. If the car turns left, the next state should reflect that turn. For simulation and robotics, pretty video is not enough; the world has to respond lawfully.

State Space

The state is the current world: actors, roads or objects, layout, which places connect to which other places, facts the camera cannot directly see, what the camera sees now, the action taken, the next world state, and the rendered image. The model needs both visible facts and hidden facts, because the camera may not show every car, lane, object, or connection that affects what can happen next. Names used later: topology for how places connect, and latent variable for a hidden fact the model uses even when it is not directly shown.

Object

The object is the rule for how the world changes after an action. The same starting scene should not have one generic next frame; it should have futures that depend on the action taken. If the action changes, the allowed next world changes too. Name used later: action-conditioned transition.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is causal continuity. From the same starting world, changing the action should change the future in the way the world's rules allow.

Allowed Movement

The generator may represent the world as pixels, vectors, graphs, or hidden variables. It must keep the variables that actions can change and the constraints that rule out impossible futures.

Why It Works

This works when the model separates two jobs: first update the world state, then render what that state looks like. Vector or graph state helps because it names cars, lanes, objects, and relationships before turning them into pixels. The update rule says which next worlds are possible, and how likely they are, after this state and this action. Name used later: transition kernel.

update world state from current state plus action, then render the new state

Evidence Check

Run tests where the same state gets different actions, edit road or object connections, check actor interactions, run long rollouts, inspect hidden-state consistency, and compare against video continuation that ignores actions.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when hidden state leaves out variables the action changes, when realistic rendering hides impossible physics, or when the checker cannot inspect the state where the error happens.

Connection

HDFlow and PACT restrict physical action steps. Stable Video Infinity repairs long generated paths. VideoKR asks whether visual evidence is retained. FlashWorld and VectorWorld put those ideas inside an interactive simulator.

EXEMPLAR 52

Depth Anything 3

Vision As Recovering How Far Things Are

Problem

A model can name a chair, a door, or a person and still misunderstand where they are in space. For walking, grasping, reconstructing a room, or changing a camera view, the missing fact is often distance, not object category.

State Space

The state is the image, the camera's internal settings, the line from the camera through each pixel, the estimated distance at each pixel, the 3D point in the scene, ambiguity about scale, hidden surfaces, and the downstream spatial task. Camera intrinsics are the camera settings needed to turn pixels into viewing rays.

Object

The object is a map from flat pixels to 3D space. A ray is the line of possible locations behind one pixel. Depth picks how far along that line the real surface is.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is spatial layout: what is near, what is far, what blocks what, and what space is reachable. What may change is the internal representation used to store that layout.

Allowed Movement

The model may infer depth from shadows, perspective, texture, and learned regularities. It must stay consistent with camera geometry and cannot replace measured spatial structure with guesses based only on familiar object sizes.

Why It Works

This works because depth adds the coordinate lost when the 3D world was projected into a 2D image. A pixel gives a direction. Depth gives a distance. Together they locate a scene point, making occlusion, relative position, and reachable space computable.

pixel plus camera ray plus depth gives a 3D scene point

Evidence Check

Measure real distance, near-far ordering, scale transfer, camera changes, object boundaries, cross-scene generalization, and cases where object recognition succeeds but spatial layout fails.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when it guesses common object size instead of scene geometry, when camera settings are wrong, or when a task needs uncertainty over several possible depths rather than one confident estimate.

Connection

Quotient diffusion chooses the right geometric space. FlashWorld maintains world state. Embodied benchmarks measure real state changes. Depth Anything 3 supplies a missing coordinate that many physical and multimodal systems need.

EXEMPLAR 53

VibeVoice, TTSDS2, And Hibiki-Zero

Speech As Sound Over Time, Not Just Text

Problem

Speech carries words, but also timing, speaker identity, emotion, rhythm, pauses, and audio quality. A transcript can be correct while the spoken result is late, flat, unnatural, or impossible to understand.

State Space

The state is the meaning, the sound pattern, the speaker information, tone and rhythm, the partial audio heard so far, the allowed delay, the generated waveform, and the speech-quality score. Prosody means tone, stress, rhythm, and timing. A waveform is the actual audio signal.

Object

The object is an internal speech state that keeps meaning tied to sound over time. It has to carry words, voice, timing, and partial audio together, because a listener hears all of those at once. The hidden state is doing more than holding text: it is holding what should be said, how it should sound, and what has already been spoken. Name used later: acoustic-temporal latent state.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is spoken intent: what was said, who or how it was said, when it was said, and whether a listener can understand it.

Allowed Movement

The system may translate, synthesize, stream, or score speech. It should not collapse speech into finished text too early, because text drops timing, voice, and sound quality.

Why It Works

This works when the model keeps the variables that matter to listeners. Streaming translation also needs a good partial state: enough meaning and tone to speak now, without waiting for the whole sentence and causing unusable delay.

speech output should depend on meaning, voice, prosody, and latency, not transcript text alone

Evidence Check

Measure intelligibility, speaker match, prosody, naturalness, delay, turn timing, artifact-level speech ratings, and cases where transcript accuracy disagrees with audio quality.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when speech is text-correct but late, flat, unstable, speaker-inconsistent, or judged through text-only proxies that cannot hear the error.

Connection

Mind-Omni creates an interface for biological signals. VideoKR preserves visual evidence over time. WebDevJudge checks artifacts in their native medium. VibeVoice, TTSDS2, and Hibiki-Zero say speech must also be generated and judged in its own medium.

EXEMPLAR 54

CausalGame

Causal Learning As Choosing The Test That Breaks The Tie

Problem

Watching the world can leave several cause-and-effect stories equally plausible. If a system can run an experiment, it should not merely describe causality. It should choose the action that separates the competing stories.

State Space

The state is the set of possible cause stories, current observations, available deliberate changes or measurements, their costs, current uncertainty, and the cause-and-effect question being answered. After each observation, some stories become less likely and some remain plausible; the remaining doubt is what the next experiment should attack. Name used later: posterior uncertainty is the remaining doubt after updating the possible stories with the evidence already seen.

Object

The object is a rule for choosing experiments. The rule should pick the next test because it can separate stories that still look possible. The action is not an answer in words; it is the next deliberate change or measurement that should make the rival stories disagree. Name used later: an experiment policy is the rule for choosing that next test.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is cause-and-effect knowledge that the evidence can actually support. Each chosen action should either rule out some possible stories or improve the estimate of the effect we care about.

Allowed Movement

The agent may deliberately change something, observe, query, or stop. It should choose actions because they distinguish possible worlds, not because they lead to fluent explanations. Name used later: an intervention is a deliberate change made to test what follows.

Why It Works

This works when different cause stories predict different outcomes under the same deliberate change. The best experiment is the one where the stories disagree the most for the least cost. The value of the experiment is how much doubt it is expected to remove once the result is seen. Name used later: information gain is the amount of remaining doubt a test is expected to remove.

choose the experiment expected to separate competing causal stories most for its cost

Evidence Check

Measure uncertainty reduction, experiment-choice quality, hidden-cause traps, cost-sensitive choices, recovery of the real cause rule on held-out cases, and whether the agent separates correlation from controllable effect.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the deliberate changes are unrealistic, hidden causes are missing from the environment, costs are ignored, or the agent memorizes benchmark-specific experiments instead of learning which evidence separates cause stories.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence reports what passive data cannot distinguish. Local Covariate Selection identifies target effects under assumptions. Neural Effect Search proposes claims. CausalGame adds the active step: choose evidence that can break the tie.

EXEMPLAR 55

Softmax Turing And HATSolver

Expressivity As Showing What Computation The Architecture Can Carry

Problem

Listing a model's parts does not tell us what it can compute. A pile of calculator buttons matters only if the buttons can be arranged into the needed procedure. Neural architectures need the same test: can their operations actually carry the target computation?

State Space

The state is the tokens or symbols, attention weights, internal memory-like numbers, smaller subproblems, update rules, numeric precision limits, and final decisions. Attention weights are the model's learned "which pieces should this piece read" scores.

Object

The object is a procedure implemented by model operations: storing information, updating memory, solving smaller expressions, simulating steps, or combining algebraic pieces. The question is not whether the model has a fancy component; it is whether those components can be arranged into the needed procedure. Name used later: expressivity, what a model architecture can represent or compute in principle.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the algorithmic relation: the right sequence of internal transformations from input to output. Different weights can implement it, but the computation being claimed must survive.

Allowed Movement

The proof may simplify depth, width, numeric precision, or input type. It must clearly say which resources are needed, because a computation that works with impossible precision or huge size may not explain real models.

Why It Works

This works when each architecture operation can be matched to a defined step in the needed procedure. Softmax attention can be used to read from memory or choose next states under a construction. Hierarchical attention helps when the task itself breaks into smaller expressions that can be solved and recombined. The key is a step-by-step map, not the label "expressive."

the architecture expresses a task when its layers can implement the task's needed steps

Evidence Check

Separate "can be constructed" from "can be learned," state how resources scale, test finite precision, remove hierarchy or attention parts, and check whether trained models use the constructed mechanism.

Failure Boundary

The claim fails when possible computation is mistaken for learnable computation, required resources are unrealistic, or the task does not actually have the hierarchy the architecture assumes.

Connection

Transformer Circuits gives concrete algorithm maps. Language Generation in the Limit warns about evidence needed to learn them. Finite Test Certification asks what evidence supports a deployed claim. Softmax Turing and HATSolver fill the capability-in-principle side of that chain.

EXEMPLAR 56

Structure Learning CI And DiCoLa

Graph Discovery As Asking Which Links The Data Can Actually Support

Problem

Finding a causal graph can look like drawing arrows. The harder question is whether the data really supports each arrow or missing arrow. Finite, noisy data can make weak links look absent, and hidden causes can make unrelated variables move together.

State Space

The state is the observed facts, possible hidden facts, candidate graph family, facts held fixed during tests, statistical tests, divide-and-conquer strategy, sample size, and the returned graph or set of graphs. CI means conditional independence: two variables stop being related after we hold a chosen set of other variables fixed.

Object

The object is evidence about which facts remain linked after other facts are held fixed. That evidence identifies a graph only under assumptions. When several graphs fit the same held-fixed relationship evidence, the data cannot choose one uniquely, so the method should return the remaining set instead of one forced graph.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is honesty about what the evidence proves. Scaling the algorithm should not turn weak tests into false certainty about arrows.

Allowed Movement

The method may split the graph, restrict possible graph shapes, or handle hidden facts. Each claimed edge or missing edge must still be tied to checked evidence about whether relationships remain after other facts are held fixed.

Why It Works

This works because causal structure leaves shadows in the data. If one variable affects another only through a third, the first and second can stop being related after the third is held fixed. Divide-and-conquer helps only if the split keeps the held-fixed relationship facts needed to rebuild the graph family. The key test asks whether two facts still move together after the relevant background facts are fixed. Name used later: conditional independence.

use supported held-fixed relationship tests to narrow the set of cause graphs still consistent with the data

Evidence Check

Report whether held-fixed relationship tests are calibrated, sensitivity to sample size, power on weak links, false and missing edges, hidden-variable stress, split-and-recombine error, and remaining doubt when one graph is not identified.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when independence tests are too weak, hidden variables break assumptions, decomposition cuts needed links, or a single graph is reported even though several graphs fit the same evidence.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence protects against overclaiming. Local Covariate Selection uses graph assumptions for target effects. CausalGame adds active interventions. Structure Learning CI and DiCoLa define the passive evidence layer underneath.

EXEMPLAR 57

RefineStat, MedAgentGym, WZ-LLM, And VERINA

Scientific Work As Claims That Must Run Or Check

Problem

A generated paper section, code analysis, or proof can sound convincing while being wrong. Scientific work becomes useful only when the claim enters an environment that can expose bad statistics, failed code, invalid proof steps, or an unfinished workflow.

State Space

The state is the generated artifact, dataset or proof context, execution environment, checker, diagnostics, repair action, task goal, and final accepted or rejected claim.

Object

The object is the set of artifacts that pass the relevant check. Admissible means allowed or accepted. An admissible artifact is one that satisfies the execution rules, statistical meaning, proof rules, or workflow completion requirements for the claim being made.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is checkable scientific validity. The model may propose code, analyses, priors, or proof steps, but acceptance must depend on evidence that can reject them.

Allowed Movement

The system may repair priors, rerun code, change analysis steps, or search proof states. The move is valid only when the checker observes the failure mode relevant to the scientific claim.

Why It Works

This works by splitting proposal from acceptance. The generator can explore possible artifacts. The environment decides which artifacts satisfy the rules that matter for the domain: code must run, statistics must match the data question, proof steps must be accepted, and workflows must finish. That prevents persuasive language from replacing evidence. Name used later: domain constraints.

accept a scientific artifact only when the relevant checker passes the claim it is supposed to support

Evidence Check

Report execution success, statistical diagnostic repair, proof-kernel acceptance, biomedical workflow completion, false-positive checks, repair paths, and places where the checker is narrower than the final scientific conclusion.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the checker validates syntax but not meaning, sandbox tasks are toy versions of real practice, or a verified component is treated as proof of an unsupported end-to-end claim.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench evaluate native artifacts. Neural Effect Search proposes claims that can be rejected. CausalGame chooses evidence. RefineStat, MedAgentGym, WZ-LLM, and VERINA make scientific generation accountable to a failure channel.

EXEMPLAR 58

MAPF Via MMOT And Schrodinger Bridges

Multi-Agent Planning As Moving Everyone Together Without Collisions

Problem

If every robot, vehicle, or character plans its own shortest path, the group can collide, block hallways, swap identities, or waste space. The real problem is not one route. It is coordinated movement through shared space and time.

State Space

The state is where agents start, where they need to end, whether identities matter, positions at each time, obstacles, motion rules, collision rules, path costs, and any smoothing used in the solver.

Object
