The useful middle ground is an editable state that remembers both the user's control facts and the medium's own rules. Name used later: a stateful cross-modal protocol is a rule for moving from one editable media state to the next across media types.

current artifact + instruction -> shared control -> next native media state, while unchanged facts stay unchanged

Evidence Check

Test repeated edits, identity preservation, image validity, audio timing, video consistency, agreement across media, reconstruction quality, and whether later instructions can still point to objects or events created earlier.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the shared code is too abstract to reconstruct details, too low-level to follow instructions, or too tangled to edit one part without corrupting another.

Connection

VibeVoice and Hibiki-Zero keep speech in sound-time form. FlashWorld and VectorWorld keep world transitions. TideGS and RadioGS keep field coordinates. OmniVerifier checks generated artifacts. NextStep-1, EditVerse, LatentLM, and UALM define the shared interface that lets those native states join one controlled workflow.

EXEMPLAR 85

EmotionThinker, EEmo-Logic, Active Mind Avatars, And MetaphorVU

Reading Social Meaning From More Than One Surface Clue

Problem

A person can say "great" and mean delight, sarcasm, resignation, or politeness. The words alone are not enough. Tone, face, body, context, culture, and what just happened can all change the meaning. A model that jumps from one word to an emotion label can be confident for the wrong reason.

State Space

The state is voice cues, face or body expression, language, metaphorical phrase, social setting, event history, cultural frame, the person's internal appraisal, intended meaning, and the response or answer.

Object

The object is the hidden situation that would make the social clues fit together. If someone says "great" after bad news, the word alone points one way, but timing, tone, facial expression, and shared context may point another way. The model is trying to infer the unseen appraisal: what the person likely thinks just happened, how they feel about it, and what response would respect that uncertainty. EmotionThinker grounds emotion reasoning in sound and context. EEmo-Logic rewards reasoning for emotion questions. Active Mind Avatars model body-based social expression. MetaphorVU tests figurative meaning as a vision-language problem rather than literal caption matching. Name used later: a latent social state is a hidden social meaning inferred from visible clues.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is an explanation that fits the human situation. The model should account for the cues and support the response without reducing meaning to a single word, facial expression, or stereotype.

Allowed Movement

What may change is which cue receives weight. The model can combine sound, face, text, gesture, and context. It should keep uncertainty when cues conflict and should not replace missing cultural or situational evidence with a generic guess.

Why It Works

It works by refusing to treat a surface cue as the answer. A strained voice, a sad phrase, an ironic image, or a tense gesture is evidence, not the emotion itself. The method earns trust when it can explain why those clues belong together: what happened, what the person may believe about it, which cue is decisive, and which cues remain ambiguous. Training helps only if it rewards that chain of support, not just the final emotion label. When cues disagree, the right output may be uncertainty rather than a forced category.

infer hidden social state from cues and context, then answer while keeping uncertainty when the cues disagree

Evidence Check

Use examples where cues conflict, sarcasm changes the meaning, metaphors are perturbed, culture shifts the interpretation, sound disagrees with text, expression disagrees with context, and the same literal phrase means different things in different situations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when benchmarks reward stereotypes, when labels force many different appraisals into one category, or when the model explains social meaning from the easiest cue instead of the decisive one.

Connection

Mind-Omni and PRISM build interfaces for human signals. WETR weights token evidence for emotion. MetamerGen tests when different images feel the same to people. EmotionThinker, EEmo-Logic, Active Mind Avatars, and MetaphorVU add the social layer: grounding can mean inferring a person's hidden situation, not only recognizing objects.

EXEMPLAR 86

JoSE, RodriNet, PACT, And HDFlow

Moving Only In Ways The Body Can Actually Do

Problem

A robot can understand "pick up the cup" and still send a command that twists the wrist the wrong way, clips through the table, or loses grip. The task idea is not enough. The action must live inside what the body and contact with the world allow.

State Space

The state is body pose, joint chain, motor commands, compact action variables, high-level subgoal, low-level motion path, trusted old behavior, correction update, contact, and the physical result after movement.

Object

The object is the allowed action surface: the smaller set of moves that make sense for this body in this state. A robot arm cannot choose any command from a giant list. Its joints bend only certain ways, rotations have rules, contact can block movement, and balance can make a move unsafe. JoSE searches compact behavior coordinates instead of the whole raw command list. RodriNet builds lawful rotation and body-chain structure into prediction. PACT keeps corrections close to trusted behavior. HDFlow separates the high-level goal path from the low-level motion path. Name used later: a constrained action manifold is the reachable action surface inside the larger command space.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended physical effect. The robot should still move the world toward the goal while respecting body geometry, contact, and the range of behavior already known to be stable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how the goal is decomposed, which compact action coordinates are used, and how the policy is corrected. The method may not move into commands the body cannot execute or updates that erase useful old skills.

Why It Works

It works because most raw commands are irrelevant or impossible before the task even begins. Learning in raw command space wastes experience rediscovering body limits, rotation rules, and contact failures. Better coordinates remove impossible directions before learning starts, so practice is spent choosing among moves the body can plausibly execute. Separating intent from motion lets the model plan "what should happen" while a lower layer handles "which body movement can cause it." Bounding corrections matters when feedback is weak because a small-looking change in a model can create a large real-world movement after contact and leverage amplify it.

choose a move from the allowed action surface, keep the update near trusted behavior, and check that the world transition satisfies the goal

Evidence Check

Measure body-rule violations, coverage of allowed actions, subgoal correctness, recovery after perturbations, loss after correction, transfer to new bodies, and cases where the language plan is good but physical execution fails.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the allowed surface excludes a needed recovery move, when the body model is wrong, or when a small policy update creates a large state change because contact is unstable.

Connection

SceneSmith and MomaGraph build environment state. EgoTactile measures hidden contact. BehaviorVLA studies reusable embodied behavior. MAPF plans paths under constraints. JoSE, RodriNet, PACT, and HDFlow give the action-side complement: after the state is visible, movement still has to stay physically possible.

EXEMPLAR 87

MetaEmbed, TACO, COMPACT, And LiME

Putting Data In A Shape That Keeps Its Rules

Problem

A spreadsheet is not just a long sentence. Rows, columns, keys, units, and missing fields all matter. If a model flattens the table until those relationships are blurred, it may answer from nearby words instead of from the table rule. The same problem appears when compressing context or adapting a model: the convenient representation can hide the structure the task needs.

State Space

The state is the structured input, row and column keys, field types, relation edges, serialization order, kept context, compact adaptation coordinates, task error, and the facts that must survive the representation.

Object

The object is a change of coordinates that keeps the useful operations available. A coordinate is just where something is placed in the model's internal format. The useful coordinates are the ones that still let the model look up a key, compare values, join records, keep a rare field, or move a model in a direction that changes the target behavior. MetaEmbed and TACO keep table and record operations visible. COMPACT keeps the parts of context that matter instead of treating every coordinate equally. LiME restricts adaptation to a small set of directions that still carry the needed behavior change.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the task's rulebook: lookup, comparison, aggregation, joining by keys, checking types, preserving source relations, and keeping the update directions that actually change the target behavior.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the encoding. The method may embed, serialize, compress, or adapt. It must not destroy the operations that define the problem just because the new representation is easier for the transformer to process.

Why It Works

It works when the new coordinates preserve the actions the task will ask for. If a question depends on joining two tables by a key, the representation must keep the key relationship, not just nearby words. If a column has units, the representation must keep the unit so numbers are compared correctly. If a model update only needs a few behavior-changing directions, the compact update space must include those directions. Efficiency helps only after irrelevant coordinates are removed; it hurts when useful structure is removed. Name used later: a structure-preserving coordinate map changes the representation while keeping the operations that matter.

a representation is valid when the same lookup, join, comparison, and useful update remain possible after encoding

Evidence Check

Test row and column swaps, unit changes, key joins, aggregation, missing fields, serialization changes, context-compression budgets, and whether compact adaptation loses behavior outside the calibration set.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the chosen coordinates preserve layout but not meaning, when correlations replace keys, when compression removes rare fields, or when a small adapter hides a domain-specific direction the task needs.

Connection

GraphGlue and embedding translation preserve compatibility between representation spaces. CRAMF keeps formal symbols queryable. ThinKV keeps sufficient context. MetaEmbed, TACO, COMPACT, and LiME make the preprocessing lesson explicit: choosing coordinates is part of the reasoning, not a formatting detail.

EXEMPLAR 88

VIST3A, TideGS, And RadioGS

Making Pictures And Signals Come From One Stable Scene

Problem

A generated room can look fine from one camera angle and fall apart from another. A radio or field signal can match one sensor and fail at a nearby sensor. The issue is that each observation may be made separately, without a stable scene or field underneath it.

State Space

The state is camera position, 3D appearance, geometry, small local blobs used to represent the scene or field, position, size, opacity, signal strength, sensor view, the rendering or measurement rule, and the observed artifact.

Object

The object is a hidden scene or field that can produce many observations. A real room is not rebuilt from scratch every time you move the camera. The same table, wall, and lamp should explain all views. A radio field works similarly: a nearby sensor should see a related signal because both sensors are measuring one underlying field. VIST3A ties generated appearance to viewpoint and 3D geometry. TideGS and RadioGS use many small rounded pieces to approximate continuous scenes, fields, or signals that sensors can measure. Name used later: those rounded pieces are Gaussian primitives.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the identity of the underlying scene or signal across views. Changing the camera, time, or sensor should change the measurement, not invent a different object or a different physical rule.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the view, edit, rendering, or reconstruction. But when the constraint is geometric or physical, the change has to happen in the scene or field state. Moving pixels directly is valid only when those pixels are the visible result of a consistent hidden state change.

Why It Works

It works because the representation stores what caused the observations, not just the observations themselves. A scene state lets the model move the camera while keeping object identity, position, and shape fixed. A field state lets the model predict what a different sensor should measure because both sensors are tied to the same hidden field. The check has to happen before the final picture or signal is produced, because that is where the shared physical object lives. Name used later: a renderable native state is an internal object that can be turned into measurements by a known rendering or sensing rule.

one hidden scene or field should explain many views through the rendering or sensor rule

Evidence Check

Run multi-view consistency tests, new-view rendering, field reconstruction with held-out sensors, local edit tests, occlusion cases, boundary cases, and regimes where the local bell-shaped pieces are too weak to represent the signal.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the chosen pieces cannot represent sharp breaks or long-range effects, when camera or sensor calibration is wrong, or when a plausible picture hides the fact that no single scene explains all views.

Connection

Quotient-Space Diffusion removes irrelevant symmetries. Depth Anything 3 recovers coordinate structure. FlashWorld and VectorWorld maintain transition state. VIST3A, TideGS, and RadioGS apply the same rule to media and fields: generate in the space where validity is defined.

EXEMPLAR 89

OmniVerifier, DAVE, VC-STaR, And HALO

Checking The Part Of The Artifact Where Failure Actually Lives

Problem

A written judge can say an image is fine while missing a broken hand, wrong label, or impossible layout. A self-correction loop can improve the wording while leaving the bad step unchanged. A policy update can look better in logs while making agents unstable together. The checker fails when it watches the wrong part of the system.

State Space

The state is the produced artifact, visual evidence, explanation signal, correction path, policy update, decentralized agent state, the part shown to the verifier, the constraint being checked, and the accept or reject decision.

Object

The object is a checker aimed at the part where the failure can actually live. If the failure is a malformed image, the checker must inspect the image, not only a caption. If the failure is a false explanation, the checker must test whether the explanation still points to the same evidence when irrelevant details change. If the failure is a broken reasoning path, the repair must fix the step where the error entered. If the failure is unstable behavior, the update must be checked in the behavior state. OmniVerifier checks generated visual artifacts directly. DAVE separates stable visual explanations from artifacts caused by the model architecture. VC-STaR uses corrected solution paths for self-training. HALO bounds policy or agent updates when stability is at risk.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is satisfaction of the real constraint in the native place where it can fail: image regions, evidence maps, reasoning paths, or behavior updates, not only final text.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the artifact, explanation, correction path, or policy. But the checker has to inspect the state component whose correctness is being claimed.

Why It Works

It works when the verifier sees the right view of the system: the part that carries the claim. A text-only judge can miss a visual defect because the defect never appears as text. A final-answer judge can miss a reasoning bug because the final sentence hides the bad step. A log-score judge can miss unstable control because the bad state appears only during interaction. The checker becomes meaningful when its view includes the variable that would make the claim false. Name used later: projected constraint checking means checking the right slice of a larger system.

accept only when the checker sees the failure-bearing part and that part satisfies the claimed constraint

Evidence Check

Compare native and text-only judging, change irrelevant image features, inspect corrected paths, test whether the verifier can be gamed, repeat optimization against the checker, and measure whether accepted updates stay stable in deployment-like interactions.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the verifier becomes another weak score, when the inspected slice omits the failure variable, or when repeated optimization finds artifacts that satisfy the checker but not the user-facing constraint.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench judge native artifacts. RLVepsR treats verifier labels as noisy learning signals. Binary RAR checks factual contradictions. FlowGuard checks cross-modal risk. OmniVerifier, DAVE, VC-STaR, and HALO unify those cases: verification only means something when it sees the object that can fail.

EXEMPLAR 90

BehaviorVLA, DreamDojo, SCALE, XR-1, Latent Action Supervision, And ScaleMoE

Proving A Learned Behavior Still Works When The Setting Changes

Problem

A robot can learn to open one drawer in one simulator and still fail on a different drawer, robot arm, lighting condition, or feedback loop. A benchmark score is weak evidence if it only proves the behavior worked in the exact world where it was practiced.

State Space

The state is what the robot observes, the robot body, the hidden action code, expert routing, simulated world, real rollout, self-check signal, deployment correction, task mix, and transfer result.

Object

The object is a behavior pattern plus the conditions under which it transfers. The useful behavior is not "move motor 7 by this exact amount." It is a rule like: from this kind of visual and language situation, choose an action that causes the intended world change. BehaviorVLA seeks reusable vision-language-action behavior with less data. DreamDojo expands practice through generated worlds. SCALE uses model signals to adjust behavior during deployment. XR-1 tests whether behavior works across bodies. Latent Action Supervision checks whether hidden action codes match task structure. ScaleMoE routes control through specialized expert components.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the useful effect of the behavior: the same kind of state change should happen across new objects, bodies, or environments, and the system should expose when that transfer assumption is no longer justified.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the practice world, expert route, hidden action code, or deployment correction. The behavior must remain tied to what the robot observes and to real physical transition evidence.

Why It Works

It works when the behavior is represented at the right level of detail. If it is too close to one robot's motors, it will not transfer to another body. If it is too abstract, it becomes a wish like "open the drawer" without the contact, timing, and force needed to make the drawer move. Generated worlds help only when they vary the same facts deployment will vary. Expert routing helps only when experts divide real control situations, not superficial scene styles. Hidden action codes help only if they group actions by what they do in the world. Self-correction helps only when the model's uncertainty is tied to real action risk.

transfer is credible when the same behavior causes the intended state change across tested tasks, bodies, and worlds

Evidence Check

Measure real rollouts across bodies, synthetic-to-real gaps, hidden-action ablations, expert-routing failures, distribution shifts, self-correction under uncertainty, and whether learned behaviors preserve transition effects rather than memorizing demonstrations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when simulated practice misses contact or timing, when a hidden action code hides body-specific constraints, when expert routing follows superficial features, or when self-evaluation stays confident outside training support.

Connection

SceneSmith constructs environments. MomaGraph and EgoTactile expose affordance and contact. JoSE and RodriNet constrain action geometry. MAPF plans under path constraints. BehaviorVLA, DreamDojo, SCALE, XR-1, Latent Action Supervision, and ScaleMoE ask whether those pieces become behavior that survives deployment shift.

EXEMPLAR 91

FRABench, UFEval, And Micro-Benchmarking Reliability

Breaking A Score Into What It Actually Measured

Problem

A restaurant can have a high average rating while the food is good, the service is slow, and the allergy handling is unsafe. A model leaderboard has the same problem. One number can hide whether the model improved image quality, followed instructions, stayed safe, or only won by a tiny gap that might disappear on another set of questions.

State Space

The state is the model, the pool of test items, the separate rubric dimensions, judge outputs, scores for each aspect, item difficulty, noise from using only a finite test set, gaps between models, and the final ranking claim.

Object

The object is a measuring report with parts and error bars. A useful report does not only say "4.3 stars." It says which part was measured, how many examples supported that number, and how much the number might move if the test used another reasonable sample. FRABench and UFEval split evaluation into aspect-level scores. Micro-Benchmarking Reliability asks whether the model order survives resampling and small test-set noise. Name used later: a factored measurement model with finite-sample uncertainty is a score split into real parts, with doubt from using only a limited test set.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the real capability claim. If the claim is "better at instruction following," the aggregation must not hide that under visual quality. If the claim is "model A beats model B," the report must keep uncertainty about whether that ordering is stable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how items are weighted, grouped, sampled, or aggregated. The evaluator can combine scores, but it must keep the component scores and the doubt needed to justify the conclusion.

Why It Works

It works because ability is not one thing, and a test item is not the whole world. One image question may test visual detail. Another may test instruction following. A third may mostly test whether the judge likes verbose answers. Splitting the score shows which ability actually moved. Error bars ask a different plain question: if we drew another similar batch of test items, would the claimed gap probably remain? Name used later: a bootstrap interval means repeatedly resampling the test results to see how much the reported score would wiggle.

say A beats B only when the measured component gap is larger than the uncertainty from finite items and judges

Evidence Check

Report reliability for each aspect, judge agreement, uncertainty intervals, sensitivity to item count, ranking reversal rates, and examples where the average score hides a severe component failure.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the rubric leaves out the real failure type, when judges cannot separate the aspects reliably, or when the items are stable but too narrow for the deployment claim.

Connection

ATLAS spends questions where they reduce doubt most. Finite Test Certification limits what a benchmark can prove. WebDevJudge and OmniVerifier change what the evaluator can observe. FRABench, UFEval, and Micro-Benchmarking Reliability complete the measurement picture: a score must say what it measured and how much evidence supports it.

EXEMPLAR 92

Weak-Strong Verification And RLVepsR

Knowing When A Cheap Checker Is Not Enough

Problem

Imagine a factory where a quick camera check catches most defects, but a few expensive failures need a human inspector. Checking everything by hand is too slow. Trusting the cheap check everywhere is unsafe. Verification for AI has the same tradeoff, and the stakes rise when checker scores are used to train the model.

State Space

The state is the task, candidate answer, cheap checker score, strong checker result, cost of checking, confidence threshold, defer decision, model update, and whether the answer truly succeeded.

Object

The object is a checking system with known mistakes and known costs. A cheap checker is like a quick scale at the airport: useful when the bag is clearly under the limit, risky when it is right at the boundary. A stronger checker costs more, so it should be used where it can change the decision or prevent harm. Weak-Strong Verification decides when to accept, reject, or ask the stronger checker. RLVepsR studies what happens when checker labels become training rewards even though some labels are wrong. Name used later: a noisy measurement channel is an imperfect checker, not truth itself.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is connection between measured success and real success. Strong checking should be spent where it changes the decision or prevents the model from learning from a bad signal.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the route: accept, reject, defer, change thresholds, or train from labels. The system must keep track of false positives, false negatives, and whether those errors become worse after the model optimizes against the checker.

Why It Works

It works when uncertainty decides who checks the answer. A cheap score far from the boundary can save cost because a stronger checker is unlikely to change the call. A cheap score near the boundary should buy stronger evidence because one wrong call can flip the outcome. For training, the question is sharper: when the checker is wrong, what direction does it push the model? If mistakes are rare and balanced, learning may still improve. If mistakes reward the wrong surface pattern, training teaches the model to exploit the checker rather than solve the task.

use the strong checker when its expected decision value is worth the cost; train only when checker feedback points toward real success

Evidence Check

Calibrate cheap scores, report defer curves, sweep the strong-checker budget, measure false positives and false negatives, test checker gaming after optimization, and compare learning, stalling, and damage under controlled noise.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when cheap-checker confidence is wrong on hard cases, when the strong checker is not actually stronger for the relevant failure, or when the trained model moves into cases where old checker calibration no longer applies.

Connection

DR Tulu changes rubrics as evidence changes. Omni-Reward and JudgeBench treat reward as measurement. Binary RAR checks factual contradictions. OmniVerifier checks native artifacts. Weak-Strong Verification and RLVepsR state the core rule: a verifier is an instrument with mistakes, costs, and feedback effects.

EXEMPLAR 93

Reward Redistribution For CVaR MDPs, UP-OCP, And ROCP

Designing For The Bad Cases That Average Scores Hide

Problem

A delivery system can be fast on average and still unacceptable if one percent of packages contain medicine that arrives dangerously late. AI decisions have the same issue. The average can look good while rare sequences, uncovered cases, or high-loss deployment states remain unsafe.

State Space

The state is the sequence of actions, reward at each step, final loss, the worst slice of outcomes, past calibration data, prediction set, online update, desired coverage level, and the downstream harm from a wrong decision.

Object

The object is a risk rule tied to action. The rule asks three plain questions: what happens in the bad slice of cases, which earlier choices helped create those bad endings, and whether the current prediction range is wide enough to act safely. Reward Redistribution for CVaR MDPs sends the blame from rare bad endings back to earlier choices that helped cause them. CVaR means focusing on the worst part of the loss pile, not the average. UP-OCP controls prediction intervals as data arrives over time. ROCP-style robust or conformal prediction keeps several plausible outcomes alive so the decision can hedge instead of pretending one forecast is certain.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is safety under bad but plausible outcomes. The method should preserve coverage, tail control, or a loss bound exactly where the downstream action is sensitive.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the reward timing, the width of prediction sets, the calibration rule, or the conservative decision. The risk statement must stay tied to the real harm variable and to the order in which data arrives.

Why It Works

It works by turning uncertainty into a limit on what action is allowed. Looking at the worst cases forces training to care about harm that the average would bury. Sending blame back through the sequence helps the learner see that the bad ending may have been prepared by an early choice, not only the final step. Online coverage keeps asking whether the prediction range still contains the truth often enough as new cases arrive. Robust prediction keeps multiple plausible futures alive when evidence is weak, so the action is chosen for the range of credible outcomes rather than the prettiest forecast.

choose the action that controls worst-case or coverage loss, not only the average loss

Evidence Check

Report tail-loss curves, coverage over time, calibration under shift, interval width versus usefulness, stress cases near decision boundaries, and whether redistributed reward points to the early actions that actually create tail harm.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the bad event is defined wrong, when prediction sets leave out the real shift, when conservative sets are too broad to act on, or when redistributed rewards point to proxies rather than causes of tail improvement.

Connection

Rare Event Analysis estimates low-probability harmful mass. Conformal Policy Control limits policy improvement using finite evidence. CreDRO, CRC, and MIRA make uncertainty decision-shaped. CVaR MDPs, UP-OCP, and ROCP give the operational form: uncertainty matters when it changes the action safely.

EXEMPLAR 94

LR Decay, BLL-Loss, LALP, Sequential Data Values, And NASH

Valuing Data By What It Can Still Teach Right Now

Problem

A math hint can be useful before a student understands the topic and useless after they already solved it. A duplicate worksheet adds little after the first one. Training data works the same way: an example is not valuable in isolation. Its value depends on the model's current state and what other examples have already taught.

State Space

The state is the current model, optimizer memory, learning rate, the direction an example would push the model, reasoning trace, local step validity, selected data subset, utility pieces, redundancy, and the extra contribution of adding one more item.

Object

The object is credit for useful change along a training path. An example earns credit only if the current model can still learn something from it that the selected data has not already taught. Timing matters: the same example can be powerful early, redundant later, or harmful if it pushes the model in the wrong direction. LR Decay studies how timing and step size change data value. BLL-Loss and LALP give credit to reasoning traces by local validity or outcome. Sequential Data Values measures what an item adds after previous selections. NASH breaks utility into parts so contribution estimates are less noisy. Name used later: marginal contribution means the extra value added after what is already selected.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful model movement. Data should be valued by the change it can still cause now and whether that change supports the intended capability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the example weight, timing, trace reward, or selected set. The credit must stay tied to how the model moves, not just metadata, source prestige, or copying a teacher's exact words.

Why It Works

It works because an example affects training only through the update it causes now. A high learning rate can make the same example powerful; a late small step can make it nearly inert. An example can push toward the target, away from it, or sideways into a lesson already covered by other data. For reasoning traces, the important question is not whether the wording matches a reference exactly. It is whether each step is allowed by the previous step and whether the whole path still reaches the right conclusion. Data value is therefore a moving property of the model, the timing, and the surrounding selected set.

data value depends on the current model, step size, useful update direction, and what the selected set already covers

Evidence Check

Move the same data to different training times, measure whether updates point toward the target, test alternate valid traces, audit local reasoning credit, report redundancy and complementarity, and remove utility components to see which ones mattered.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when useful movement is measured on a narrow validation slice, when local trace credit rewards plausible but globally wrong steps, or when value changes after the model moves but the selector keeps using stale estimates.

Connection

Midtraining Bridges times distribution movement. DiReCT chooses data by directions that help and do not damage. OPUS and HOBIT select examples by optimizer effect. Common Corpus keeps source records. LR Decay, BLL-Loss, LALP, Sequential Data Values, and NASH explain the data principle underneath: data matters through the movement it produces at that moment.

EXEMPLAR 95

BRCD And Production Agent Studies

Finding The Fix, Not Just The Loudest Symptom

Problem

When a website goes down, many dashboards turn red at once. The service with the biggest spike may only be reacting to another service that failed first. Operators need to know which change would actually stop the failure, even when logs are incomplete.

State Space

The state is the service dependency map, partial causal structure, traces before failure, observed failure, anomaly scores, candidate causes, possible directed cause stories, repair outcomes, and risk that the incident comes back.

Object

The object is a ranked list of repair points with remaining doubt attached. Each candidate repair is judged by a plain counter-question: if we fixed this point, how likely is the failure to stop? BRCD keeps several possible cause maps that fit the logs instead of betting everything on one story, then asks which repair would most change the failure across those maps. Production agent studies add workflow facts: tool calls, handoffs, monitors, hidden state, and when to escalate to a human. Name used later: posterior means revised uncertainty after seeing the traces.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is repair value. A diagnosis should keep uncertainty over possible mechanisms while identifying actions that would reduce the failure probability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the ranking, the sampled cause story, the aggregation across stories, or the escalation path. The method should not collapse uncertainty into one confident story unless the traces actually distinguish that story.

Why It Works

It works because cause is about what would change after a fix, not which dashboard shouted loudest. Symptoms say where the system looked abnormal. Causes say where a forced repair would alter the bad outcome. Keeping several possible cause maps is important when logs are incomplete, because the same symptom pattern can come from different mechanisms. A good diagnosis therefore ranks actions, not stories: it asks which next check or repair would most reduce the chance of the incident continuing or returning.

rank a suspected cause by how likely the failure would change if that point were fixed, given traces and possible mechanisms

Evidence Check

Compare anomaly rank to successful fixes, audit missing trace variables, test whether proposed fixes reduce recurrence, report uncertainty over alternative causes, and measure whether agent diagnoses choose useful next checks rather than plausible stories.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the true cause is not traced, when possible cause graphs share the same symptoms but imply different repairs, or when no intervention feedback arrives to correct the diagnosis.

Connection

CausalGame chooses evidence to identify mechanisms. Local Covariate Selection targets one effect. Distributional Equivalence names what passive data cannot distinguish. BRCD and production studies bring those ideas to operations: the output that matters is the ranked repair action.

EXEMPLAR 96

DISCO, Fair Posthoc Control, And Accuracy Auctions

Treating Predictions As Rules That Affect People

Problem

A loan model is not just guessing a label. Its output changes who gets money, who pays more, and who has a chance to improve. A high average accuracy can still hide shortcuts, unfair group effects, or the fact that being correct matters more for some users than others.

State Space

The state is input features, shortcut variables, protected or social groups, assumptions about what would happen under a different decision, a control knob after training, private value of correctness, prediction, decision rule, and welfare or harm.

Object

The object is a decision rule with constraints. It should say which evidence is allowed to influence the decision, which shortcut routes are forbidden, how different groups are affected, and whose benefit is counted. DISCO discourages shortcut paths by checking whether unwanted variables still carry information after allowed facts are held fixed. Fair Posthoc Control keeps a visible fairness-accuracy tradeoff after training instead of hiding it in one score. Accuracy Auctions treat correctness as worth different amounts to different people and design reporting rules around truthful value. Name used later: policy means a rule for choosing actions.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate decision value: useful prediction under stated limits about which variables may drive the decision, how groups are affected, and whose accuracy matters most.

Allowed Movement

What may change is weighting, post-processing, auction design, regularization, or abstention. The system must expose the tradeoff instead of hiding social constraints inside one average objective.

Why It Works

It works by treating the model output as an allocation rule. The prediction changes what people receive, so the route from evidence to decision matters. Removing a shortcut blocks an unwanted route, such as a protected fact leaking through a proxy. A fairness tradeoff curve shows what is gained and lost when the decision rule is moved toward one fairness goal. An auction uses people's private values so accuracy is spent where being correct matters most, but only if the reporting rule makes honesty the best move. Name used later: a constrained decision rule chooses actions while obeying causal, fairness, and incentive limits.

choose the decision rule that creates value while obeying shortcut, fairness, incentive, and uncertainty constraints

Evidence Check

Run shortcut interventions, sweep subgroup tradeoffs, test assumptions about what would happen under protected changes, check truthful reporting, compare welfare, and find cases where average accuracy rises while constrained value falls.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when shortcut variables are hidden, fairness variables are mismeasured, people act strategically outside the mechanism, or post-training controls cannot repair representation choices made during training.

Connection

VALUEFLOW exposes value aggregation. SafeDPO exposes helpfulness-safety frontiers. Fair Causal Bandits constrain exploration. Data Market Pricing handles strategic information. DISCO, Fair Posthoc Control, and Accuracy Auctions add the policy layer: model outputs have social consequences once they allocate decisions.

EXEMPLAR 97

Causal Route Gating, Divergent Interventions, And Activation Oracles

Changing A Model Internally Without Creating A Fake Situation

Problem

If you test a car by replacing one part with a random object, the car's failure may tell you nothing about how the original part worked. Internal model tests have the same danger. Changing hidden activations can reveal a causal route, or it can create an unnatural state the model would never use on its own.

State Space

The state is the hidden activation vector, attention route, visual or text evidence path, normal range of activations, internal patch, question asked to an oracle probe, output change, and distance from states the model naturally visits.

Object

The object is an internal cause test that stays near the model's normal computation. The test should change the route being studied without turning the model into a situation it never normally enters. Causal Route Gating estimates how much the answer came through the visual route or the text-prior route, then suppresses paths that rely too much on prior text habits. Divergent Interventions warns when a patch creates an unnatural hidden state. Activation Oracles expose information stored in hidden activations through probes that can be queried in language. Name used later: on-manifold means the changed state remains close to states the model naturally uses.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is mechanism validity. The intervention should change the route being tested while keeping the rest of the computation plausible.

Allowed Movement

What may change is a patch, gate, steering direction, or internal query. The experiment must watch locality and distance from natural states so the conclusion remains about the real computation.

Why It Works

It works when the internal change is close to a clean experiment. A clean experiment changes one suspected route while leaving the rest of the machine in a believable condition. If blocking a text-prior route reduces hallucination while visual evidence remains available, that route was actionable. If an oracle can recover hidden information, the state really carried that signal. But the result is credible only when the patched state is still one the model could reasonably occupy and the output change is specific to the route being tested. Otherwise the experiment may only prove that broken internal states produce strange outputs.

an internal effect is credible when the output changes after a local patch and the patched state remains near normal model states

Evidence Check

Measure distance from natural activations, route specificity, patch locality, visual-text swaps, oracle calibration, evasion under pressure, and whether interventions transfer across prompts without unnatural artifacts.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when patches create unnatural activations, when probes become optimization targets, when route estimates are only correlated with the cause, or when oracle answers expose information without enabling reliable control.

Connection

MINT and VISUALSWAP ask whether visual evidence causes outputs. Steer Like the LLM turns prompts into activation directions. FlowGuard checks cross-modal safety. Causal Route Gating, Divergent Interventions, and Activation Oracles name the internal standard: interventions must stay near the model's own computation.

EXEMPLAR 98

Jacobian Spectra, BBP Transitions, And FTRL Lower Bounds

Seeing Which Learning Directions Are Actually Reachable

Problem

A radio signal can be real but too faint to separate from static. A route can exist on a map but take too long to reach. Learning has the same limits. A model may be able to represent the answer in principle, while the signal is buried in noise or the update path is too slow to reach the useful state.

State Space

The state is the pattern of strong and weak directions in the representation, local sensitivity of the model, planted signal, noise cloud, optimizer path, game shape, regret, distance from the current state to the target state, and the set of possible stable outcomes.

Object

The object is the direction that learning can actually see and follow. A local sensitivity table asks: if this input or parameter changes a little, which outputs change a lot and which barely move? A Jacobian is that table. Its strong and weak directions show where the model reacts most or least. Those direction strengths are called spectra. BBP transitions describe when a hidden signal becomes strong enough to stand apart from random noise. FTRL lower bounds show that low average regret does not mean the current choice quickly reaches the stable outcome that matters.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is recoverable progress: a signal, direction, or path that the algorithm can follow with finite data and time.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the representation, optimizer, or game assumption. The theory must keep separate two claims: "a solution exists" and "this learning process can actually reach it."

Why It Works

It works because learning is directional. A signal that sticks out beyond the noise can become a feature. A signal buried inside the noise may be real but unusable with the available data and update rule. A network that is sensitive along one direction can learn changes there more easily than along a direction it barely reacts to. In games, updates can reduce average regret while the actual current play remains far from the desired stable behavior. Name used later: a phase transition is a sharp change from hidden signal to visible signal as strength crosses a threshold.

progress needs a signal direction above noise and an update path that gets closer to the target in usable time

Evidence Check

Track strong and weak directions, emergence of outlier signal, noise thresholds, whether updates move with the recovered directions, distance of the current choice to the stable outcome, and hard games where regret improves but practical convergence does not.

Failure Boundary

The result fails as guidance when the random-noise or game model does not match training, when visible directions are not task-relevant, or when average-regret guarantees are mistaken for a guarantee about the final current behavior.

Connection

Alignment-Sensitive Minimax Rates studies target signal in learned spectra. Single-Head Attention studies spectral recovery. Adam Degeneracy changes optimizer geometry. Language Generation in the Limit separates possibility from reachable evidence. Jacobian spectra, BBP transitions, and FTRL bounds add the same caution from another angle: capability is the part of structure that learning can actually reach.

EXEMPLAR 99

CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, And MADQA

Checking The Skill Experts Actually Care About

Problem

A counseling answer can sound warm while missing a risk boundary. A medical video answer can describe movement while missing the event a clinician cares about. A document answer can be wrong because the needed evidence was never retrieved, not because the final reasoning was bad. In expert domains, fluency is often the wrong thing to measure.

State Space

The state is the domain artifact, expert checklist, safety boundary, time-based evidence, document evidence, human baseline, answer with ideal access, agent search path, retrieved support, and final score.

Object

The object is a score broken into the parts an expert would actually inspect. A counseling answer can be kind in tone and still miss the safety move. A seizure-video answer can describe motion and still miss the medically important moment. A document answer can reason well from the wrong source because the right source was never found. CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, and MADQA split the score so each hidden failure remains visible. Name used later: expert measurement decomposition means splitting the score by expert-visible pieces instead of hiding them inside one average.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is domain-valid support. The evaluation should preserve the difference between an answer that merely sounds right and one supported by the right expert rule, time window, or document evidence.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the scoring setup: human experts, ideal evidence access, separate scores, or time labels. The benchmark must keep the domain failure visible instead of reducing every miss to one generic accuracy number.

Why It Works

It works because expert tasks have rules that fluent text can hide. The judge must ask the same question an expert would ask: did the answer handle the risk boundary, locate the right time window, retrieve the needed document, or respect the professional constraint? Once those parts are separated, a model cannot earn a high score merely by sounding polished. The score becomes evidence about the skill that matters in the domain, not evidence about general smoothness.

domain score should separate evidence access, expert interpretation, and safety or professional constraint satisfaction

Evidence Check

Report expert agreement, safety-boundary violations, event localization over time, ideal-answer versus human versus agent gaps, retrieval misses, and cases where fluent responses fail domain-specific constraints.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when expert labels disagree without being modeled, when benchmark cases are too simplified, when time labels miss clinically relevant cues, or when ideal-answer definitions make the task too easy or too strong.

Connection

FRABench factors general multimodal quality. ATLAS treats items as measuring instruments. WebDevJudge changes the artifact channel. CounselBench, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, and MADQA add the domain rule: evaluation must observe the variable experts actually use.

EXEMPLAR 100

Gaia2, BIRD-INTERACT, tau2-bench, OpenApps, VenusBench-Mobile, And PIPE

Judging The Whole Work Session, Not Just The Final Reply

Problem

An assistant booking travel, editing an app, or querying a database can fail long before the final answer. It may click the wrong button, misunderstand a user correction, query the wrong table, forget a state change, or recover badly from a changed interface. Scoring only the final text hides where the work broke.

State Space

The state is the user goal, environment state, interface view, database or app state, action history, checks for intermediate actions, user feedback, memory, final artifact, and task completion condition.

Object

The object is the whole sequence of states, actions, and observations. A work session is like following a recipe in someone else's kitchen: what you see, which drawer you open, what changes after each step, and whether you recover from a wrong turn all matter. Gaia2 attaches action-level checks in changing environments. BIRD-INTERACT turns text-to-SQL into real database interaction. tau2-bench exposes failures in coordinating with user guidance. OpenApps, VenusBench-Mobile, and PIPE test app or mobile agents under interface variation and rewrites. Name used later: a controlled interaction trajectory is the checked path of task states through time.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate progress toward the user's goal. Each action should move the environment closer to completion while leaving evidence that the state transition was valid.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the action: ask, query, click, write, retrieve, revise, or stop. Every action changes the state and should be judged by that change, not by how plausible the action sounded.

Why It Works

It works because agent work is a loop, not a final sentence. The agent sees only part of the environment, chooses an action, observes what changed, updates its memory, and chooses again. If a failure happens at step three, a polished final answer cannot prove the work was valid. Intermediate checks locate the break in the actual loop: seeing the screen, remembering the task, retrieving the right evidence, following the user, grounding a database query, or adapting to the interface. Name used later: a partially observed control problem means acting while the full state is not directly visible.

evaluate the action path by verified progress through task states, not only by the final text

Evidence Check

Measure action validity, state-transition success, user-correction handling, interface perturbation robustness, database-grounding errors, memory failures, and whether final success can be traced to legitimate intermediate steps.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when checks verify local steps but miss the global goal, when interface variants are superficial, when user simulators are unrealistic, or when agents learn benchmark-specific action scripts.

Connection

Skill-Pro turns traces into reusable procedures. ThunderAgent schedules agent execution graphs. DR Tulu changes research rubrics as evidence changes. SandboxEscapeBench checks boundary crossing. Gaia2 and related interaction benchmarks provide the measurement base: agent capability is a property of stateful work paths.

EXEMPLAR 101

CyberGym, CVE-Factory, And SandboxEscapeBench

Proving Security Claims By Running The Attack Or The Defense

Problem

A person can describe a lockpick without opening a lock. A model can describe a vulnerability without producing an exploit, and it can describe a patch without proving the bug is blocked. Security claims are behavioral: did the attack work, did the patch stop it, and did the isolation boundary hold?

State Space

The state is vulnerable code, patched code, exploit attempt, proof-of-concept output, generated security task, container or virtual-machine boundary, forbidden host resource, tool path, and pass or breach record.

Object

The object is runtime evidence about a boundary. The question is not whether the model can describe danger. The question is whether an action crosses a boundary when run, or whether a defense blocks it when run. CyberGym checks proof-of-concept behavior against vulnerable and fixed code. CVE-Factory studies how to generate realistic security tasks that can be checked. SandboxEscapeBench tests whether forbidden host state can be reached from inside nested isolation. Name used later: executable boundary evidence means the certificate comes from running code, not from persuasive text.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is security causality. The exploit should succeed because the vulnerability exists, fail because the patch or boundary works, and leave logs that distinguish those cases.

Allowed Movement

What may change is task generation, attack execution, patching, or sandboxing. The result must stay tied to runtime evidence rather than textual plausibility.

Why It Works

It works because security is about reachable states. A vulnerability claim is supported when the exploit changes the vulnerable program and fails on the fixed one for the right reason. A sandbox claim is supported when forbidden resources remain unreachable under the allowed actions. Generated tasks matter only when they preserve a real vulnerability structure and include a success condition that execution can check. Running the attack or defense turns a story about security into evidence about which states can actually be reached.

security claim holds when execution separates vulnerable, fixed, and forbidden-boundary states under controlled actions

Evidence Check

Run vulnerable-versus-patched comparisons, capture exploit logs, test false positives, verify generated tasks with independent checks, measure sandbox reachability, and inspect whether agents exploit benchmark harness artifacts instead of the intended vulnerability.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when generated tasks are unrealistic, when proof-of-concept attacks exploit the test harness, when patches change unrelated behavior, or when sandbox models omit real host attack surfaces.

Connection

WebDevJudge and PaperBench judge native artifacts. RLVepsR studies verifier labels as training signal. CSPO and SecFid model safety boundaries. CyberGym, CVE-Factory, and SandboxEscapeBench make the security version explicit: the certificate is an executed state change or its prevention.

EXEMPLAR 102

EditBench, WZ-LLM, VERINA, And AlgoVeri

Letting The Checker Define Whether Code Or Proofs Count

Problem

A code patch can look tidy while failing the user's edit. A proof can sound convincing while failing the proof checker. For code and formal math, similarity to a known answer is weak evidence. The artifact has to run, satisfy the spec, or be accepted by the formal system.

State Space

The state is the source artifact, edit instruction, tests, specification, proof goal, checker or proof kernel, generated patch or proof, rejection trace, and the set of artifacts the checker accepts.

Object

The object is the set of acceptable artifacts defined by an external checker. The model can write many patches or proofs, but only some of them actually satisfy the surrounding code, the stated behavior, or the proof system. EditBench evaluates edits in their surrounding context. WZ-LLM and VERINA target proof artifacts that must satisfy formal systems. AlgoVeri studies verified-code or proof capability across tools. Name used later: an admissible artifact set is all outputs the checker accepts for this spec and context.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is semantic validity: the code should do the intended behavior, or the proof should establish the intended theorem, under the checker. Looking like a solution is not enough.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the patch, proof, repair, or target tool. Each move must land inside the accepted set for the target artifact and preserve the user's stated intent.

Why It Works

It works because proposal and acceptance are separated. The model is allowed to guess, search, repair, and try again. The checker is not asked whether the answer sounds convincing; it enforces a rule over behavior, specification, contract, or proof kernel. That turns correctness into a membership question: is this artifact inside the accepted set for this exact task? The hard part is scope. A passing test proves only the behavior tested. A formal proof proves only the theorem stated. The benchmark has to keep that boundary visible.

a generated artifact is valid when the checker accepts it for the spec, context, and intended behavior being claimed

Evidence Check

Report checker acceptance, failing-test categories, proof-kernel rejection causes, theorem-statement alignment, edit-intent satisfaction, cross-tool transfer, and cases where accepted artifacts still miss broader user intent.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when tests are incomplete, specs are wrong, theorem targets are irrelevant, proof checkers verify a narrow statement, or benchmark examples reward local fixes that break surrounding behavior.

Connection

CRAMF retrieves formal objects. RefineStat repairs probabilistic programs under semantic checks. PaperBench evaluates research artifacts. Consequence-Based Utility uses downstream checks when direct proof is unavailable. EditBench, WZ-LLM, VERINA, and AlgoVeri are the strict end of that spectrum: acceptance is defined by an external verifier.

EXEMPLAR 103

Pressure Reveals Character, Jailbreak Foundry, Harnessing Non-Adversarial Robustness, LLM Deception, And Obfuscation Atlas

Testing What Happens When The Interaction Pushes Toward Failure

Problem

A person may seem calm in ordinary conversation but behave differently under pressure. A model can also look safe or honest on easy prompts because the test never reaches the condition that triggers failure. The failure may appear only after multi-turn pressure, reusable jailbreak pieces, harmless-looking wording shifts, detector-aware training, or prompts that invite deception without obvious attack language.

State Space

The state is the prompt path, user pressure, attack module, model response, hidden internal state, detector score, harmless-looking wording variation, deception label, safety boundary, and observed failure event.

Object

The object is a hidden tendency revealed by pressure. A model may look safe in ordinary prompts because the test never pushes on the boundary where the unsafe behavior appears. Pressure Reveals Character changes the interaction path to expose behavior traits. Jailbreak Foundry makes attack pressure reusable and executable. Harnessing Non-Adversarial Robustness studies controlled prompt changes that are not explicitly malicious. LLM Deception on Benign Prompts asks whether deception appears without hostile wording. Obfuscation Atlas studies what happens when internal detectors become targets. In plain terms, the method measures a tendency to fail that ordinary prompts do not expose.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the safety claim under pressure. A real claim should not only say the model behaves well in the easiest prompt region; it should say how close the interaction can move toward violation before the claim breaks.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the prompt path, attack composition, harmless surface form, internal-state inspection, or detector pressure. The pressure process must be explicit enough that the measured failure rate can be reproduced.

Why It Works

It works because hidden tendencies are inferred from behavior under carefully chosen changes. Average accuracy mostly measures the easy middle of the prompt world. Pressure tests move toward a boundary while keeping the failure definition fixed. If a model has a deception or evasion pattern, the signal may stay rare until the interaction reaches the region where that pattern is useful to the model. Adaptive tests increase the chance of entering that region. The important protection is that the pressure path must be reproducible; otherwise the test may only show that one clever prompt worked once.

pressure tests estimate failure near a boundary, not average response quality on ordinary prompts

Evidence Check

Report pressure-path definitions, human or verifier calibration, attack-module reproducibility, harmless-perturbation families, detector-awareness sweeps, hidden-state changes, and whether failures remain when the pressure path changes form but targets the same boundary.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when pressure is dramatic but not diagnostic, when attack modules only overfit known defenses, when harmless perturbations are too narrow, or when detector-aware optimization creates a new evasion game that no longer measures the original safety property.

Connection

Rare Event Analysis estimates tail mass. CSPO defines distance to violation. SandboxEscapeBench checks executable boundary crossing. FlowGuard monitors risky generation paths. This family adds the behavioral rule: safety is a response curve under pressure, not a point estimate at the easiest part of the distribution.

EXEMPLAR 104

LLM DNA, LLMs Get Lost, And Token Overcharging

Checking Whether The Same Task Still Acts Like The Same Task

Problem

The same request can be wrapped in different wording, placed earlier or later in a conversation, split into different tokens, priced differently, or served by a model with a different adaptation history. A system can look capable while being brittle to the wrapper rather than the task.

State Space

The state is the meaning of the task, prompt surface form, conversation history, tokenization, position, pricing rule, model lineage, behavior fingerprint, belief state, user cost, and final answer.

Object

The object is a group of interfaces that should count as the same task. If the real request is unchanged, then moving the request to a new position, wording it differently, splitting it into different tokens, or serving it through a related model should not secretly change the capability being measured. LLM DNA fingerprints behavior across adaptation and distillation. LLMs Get Lost measures path dependence when early mistaken assumptions corrupt later recovery. Token Overcharging treats token accounting and pricing as part of the mechanism. The plain rule is that different wrappers should preserve the same task-relevant behavior.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is semantic and economic stability. Equivalent tasks should not change capability just because the wrapper changes, and users should not face distorted costs because the token interface can be exploited.

Allowed Movement

What may change is formatting, compression, distillation, adaptation, pricing, or conversation continuation. Those movements should preserve the task-relevant function and make unavoidable dependence on history or cost visible.

Why It Works

It works by separating the task from the wrapper used to present it. If two prompts carry the same information, a robust model should behave compatibly. If moving the request later in the conversation changes the answer, the model depends on position. If rewording the same task changes the answer, it depends on surface form. If token splits or pricing change the cost without changing the work, the interface has become part of the mechanism. Name used later: invariance means the output should stay stable under changes that do not change the underlying task.

robust capability means equivalent wrappers give compatible behavior after accounting for real information and token-cost differences

Evidence Check

Run prompt-equivalence sweeps, recovery tests after wrong assumptions, lineage fingerprint comparisons, tokenization and pricing audits, position-sensitivity checks, and wrappers that preserve task meaning while changing interface coordinates.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when two interfaces are declared equivalent despite hiding different information, when fingerprints confuse style with capability, or when pricing fixes remove one incentive while leaving other strategic usage channels untouched.

Connection

ThinKV and EntroKV ask what history is sufficient. LongWriter-Zero and Mamba-3 ask what long context preserves. Common Corpus and Hubble ask where a model came from. LLM DNA, LLMs Get Lost, and Token Overcharging make the same issue user-facing: the interface is part of the mathematical system.

EXEMPLAR 105

Hedging On The Frontier, Know More Know Clearer, MC-Search, And Anytime Trees

Deciding Whether To Answer, Search, Hedge, Or Stop

Problem

A doctor does not order every possible test for a simple cold, and should not guess when symptoms point to something dangerous. An AI system has the same choice: answer now, hedge, retrieve more evidence, branch a search, ask for clarification, or stop. Treating every task as direct answering wastes effort on easy cases and creates confident errors on hard ones.

State Space

The state is current evidence, candidate answer, confidence, known tradeoff frontier, available models, search node, retrieved medium, split statistic, stopping rule, compute budget, and the cost of being wrong or vague.

Object

The object is a rule for buying evidence. Imagine deciding whether to answer from memory, look up one source, order an expensive test, or say the evidence is not enough. The right move depends on how much the next evidence could change the decision and what a wrong answer would cost. Hedging on the Frontier uses known benchmark tradeoffs to choose among models when evidence is sparse. Know More Know Clearer makes stated certainty match actual knowledge. MC-Search turns multimodal retrieval into step-by-step evidence gathering. Anytime Trees add structure only when sequential tests support the split. Name used later: an evidence-acquisition policy is a rule for when to search, hedge, answer, or stop.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is decision value under uncertainty. The system should preserve the difference between knowing, not knowing yet, knowing after search, and lacking enough evidence for a strong answer.

Allowed Movement

What may change is whether the system hedges, branches, retrieves, aggregates, defers, or stops. Each action should be justified by expected information gain, risk reduction, or protection against false structure.

Why It Works

It works because evidence has value only through the decision it can change. When uncertainty is low and the cost of error is small, extra search mostly wastes compute. When uncertainty is high or the cost of error is large, searching, hedging, or deferring can be the safer action. Calibration keeps the language of certainty tied to what the system actually knows. Anytime tests protect against a common mistake: repeatedly searching until random noise looks like a real pattern. The stopping rule must count how many chances the system had to fool itself.

choose answer, hedge, search, or stop by comparing expected value of more evidence against compute cost and error risk

Evidence Check

Report calibration curves, abstention or hedging utility, search-depth value, retrieval support quality, transfer of frontier estimates, validity of stopping rules, and cases where extra evidence changes or fails to change the decision.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when confidence is miscalibrated, when search retrieves repeated evidence, when the benchmark frontier does not transfer to the new task, or when the stopping rule is invalid because the algorithm keeps probing until noise looks significant.

Connection

ATLAS allocates test items by information. Weak-Strong Verification buys stronger checks near uncertainty boundaries. Consequence-Based Utility evaluates downstream effects. Hedging, metacognition, search, and anytime inference are the same control problem: spend evidence only where it changes the decision.

EXEMPLAR 106

BrokenMath, EigenBench, BFTS, 2-SAT Robustness, FPS, LM Geometry Diagnostics, And INDUCTION

Building Tests Where The Error Reveals The Broken Operation

Problem

A student can memorize the shape of a proof without understanding whether the theorem is true. A model can do the same: write a proof for a false statement, exploit the order of logic clauses, hide uncertainty, satisfy a subjective judge for the wrong reason, or show neat internal structure that does not predict actual behavior.

State Space

The state is the formal premise, logic instance, proof trace, subjective-value judgment, revised uncertainty after evidence, exploration context, internal representation shape, perturbation family, hidden rule, and observed error pattern.

Object

The object is a test item designed so the error points to a specific operation. A good diagnostic question is like a mechanic's test: it is built so the failure tells you which part broke. BrokenMath uses false statements to test whether proof writing follows truth or merely flatters the premise. EigenBench treats subjective value as hidden agreement to estimate. BFTS tests decisions that should use uncertainty under nonlinear feedback. 2-SAT Robustness changes logical surface form. FPS and INDUCTION check construction and rule recovery. LM Geometry Diagnostics asks whether internal geometric measurements actually predict downstream capability. Name used later: a structural probe is a benchmark item built to expose one operation, not broad familiarity.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended operation: logical validity, satisfiability structure, constructive correctness, rule recovery, uncertainty-guided action, subjective-value direction, or representation shape that predicts behavior.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the surface form, labels, hidden rule, construction requirement, judge aggregation, or internal measurement. The target operation must stay isolated enough that the failure is interpretable.

Why It Works

It works when the benchmark behaves like an experiment rather than a trivia quiz. A false premise tests whether proof behavior depends on truth. Renaming clauses and adding filler tests whether the model follows logic rather than presentation. Construction tasks force outputs into a checkable set. Induction tasks ask whether the hidden rule was recovered rather than memorized. Subjective and exploration tests ask whether value or uncertainty is actually used to choose. Geometry diagnostics close the loop by asking whether an internal number predicts the operation it claims to explain. The key is controlled change: alter the wrapper, keep the real task fixed, and see which operation fails.

a reasoning test is useful when changing irrelevant surface form keeps the task same and errors reveal the failed operation

Evidence Check

Use controlled perturbations, false-premise audits, verifier checks, hidden-rule generalization, uncertainty-to-action curves, human validation of subjective values, geometry-behavior correlation tests, and error clusters that map back to named operations.

Failure Boundary

The method fails when the probe leaks shortcuts, when perturbations change the real task, when subjective consensus hides minority values, when geometry metrics describe but do not predict behavior, or when the benchmark labels failure without saying which operation failed.

Connection

Language Generation in the Limit separates formal possibility from learnability. Transformer Circuits maps architecture to algorithms. Finite Test Certification bounds what benchmark evidence proves. This family gives the experimental counterpart: a reasoning benchmark is useful only when it makes a mathematical operation visible.

EXEMPLAR 107

DRPBench, CIRBench, SwingArena, And SparseRL

Finding Bugs That Only Appear In A Certain Order

Problem

Two people can safely use a kitchen if one opens the oven after the other steps away; the same actions become dangerous in the opposite order. Software has the same kind of failure. Code can look fine line by line, yet break when two threads, callbacks, UI events, or sparse updates happen in a particular order.

State Space

The state is program text, shared memory, lock state, event queue, UI state, callback order, compiler or runtime setting, sparse update path, possible orderings of events, and the observed failure trace.

Object

The object is the set of executions the program can actually reach. A program is not only its lines of text; it is all the ways those lines can run when threads, callbacks, UI events, and updates interleave. DRPBench targets data races, where two operations touch shared data in an unsafe order. CIRBench asks whether code reasoning follows runtime behavior rather than syntax alone. SwingArena makes UI behavior depend on state and events. SparseRL exposes how sparse updates change learning or execution paths. Name used later: reachable executions under partial-order constraints means all event orders allowed by the program and runtime rules.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is correctness across the relevant event orders, not only the easiest trace. A correct answer should say whether a bad state can be reached and which ordering, event, or update makes it reachable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the test schedule, simulated UI actions, runtime configuration, or explanation prompt. The score must depend on reachable behavior. A diagnosis that sounds right is weak evidence unless it maps to an executable path.

Why It Works

It works because interactive and concurrent software is a state machine over time. The same line can be safe or unsafe depending on what happened before it and what runs next. The proof of a bug is not that a line looks suspicious. The proof is an allowed path from a safe starting state to a bad state. Schedules, event traces, and UI states make that hidden path visible. If no allowed path reaches the bad state, the warning may be only a pattern match. If one does, the benchmark can point to the exact order that makes the failure real.

a bug exists when an allowed event order reaches a bad state from the starting state

Evidence Check

Report which event orders were tried, which variables were shared, which locks or ordering rules forced one action before another, which UI events happened, which tests reproduce the failure, which warnings were false alarms, and where a surface code pattern guessed the wrong outcome.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the benchmark tries only easy event orders, when its runtime model leaves out real platform behavior, when UI state is flattened into text, or when an explanation names a bug class without showing a path that can actually reach the bad state.

Connection

EditBench and VERINA use checkers for code and proof artifacts; CyberGym executes security claims; WebDevJudge inspects rendered software. DRPBench, CIRBench, SwingArena, and SparseRL sharpen the software side: correctness is a property of reachable state transitions.

EXEMPLAR 108

Prescriptive Scaling, Train-before-Test, CALIPER, And CV Relative Instability

Stating What Was Done Before The Score Was Measured

Problem

A runner's time means different things if they trained for six months, warmed up, ran on a hill, or ran after an injury. A model score is the same. It depends on compute, post-training, allowed adaptation, data drift, and the comparison split. Treating the score as a permanent property of the model hides the recipe that produced it.

State Space

The state is the base model, pretraining compute, post-training rule, adaptation budget, new task data, drift stream, cross-validation folds, instability measure, high-end target performance, and the final selection or retraining decision.

Object

The object is capability with the protocol attached. A score should carry its recipe: how much compute was used, what training happened before the test, whether new data arrived, and how the comparison split was chosen. Prescriptive Scaling estimates how compute maps to downstream capability under a specified training recipe. Train-before-Test measures what a model can do after task-specific training, not only frozen behavior. CALIPER asks whether evidence after drift is enough to retrain. CV Relative Instability asks when cross-validation comparisons have honest uncertainty. Name used later: a conditional capability functional is a score that depends on the model plus the evaluation recipe.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the claim. A score should carry the conditions that make it true: what was trained, when adaptation was allowed, what drift was observed, and how stable the comparison was.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compute, adaptation before testing, retraining after drift, or fold comparison. Those changes must be named as part of the claim instead of hidden behind one leaderboard number.

Why It Works
