It works because capability is not a bare property floating inside the model. It is affected by what was done before measuring. More compute changes what can be learned. Task-specific training changes what behavior can be extracted. Drift changes whether yesterday's model is still the right object to deploy. Cross-validation helps only when the model ordering is stable enough that the uncertainty around the split is smaller than the claimed gap. The plain rule is: report the score together with the procedure that created it, or the claim cannot be compared fairly.

reported capability = result of model, data, compute, adaptation, drift state, and comparison protocol together

Evidence Check

Report the full evaluation recipe, how uncertain the compute prediction is, how performance changes as more adaptation is allowed, whether new data after drift is enough to justify retraining, whether cross-validation intervals are wide enough to cover ranking noise, and cases where frozen and train-before-test rankings disagree.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the recipe is underspecified, when compute predictions stop holding after a new post-training method appears, when drift detection lacks enough fresh evidence for retraining, or when cross-validation folds are too unstable to support model ranking.

Connection

ATLAS treats items as measurement instruments; Micro-Benchmarking Reliability studies finite ranking noise; Finite Test Certification bounds benchmark claims. Prescriptive Scaling, Train-before-Test, CALIPER, and CV Relative Instability add the missing variable: what the evaluator did to the model and data before declaring capability.

EXEMPLAR 109

PhyWorldBench, AdAEM, WoW!, And ImageDoctor

Checking Whether A Pretty Artifact Still Obeys Its World

Problem

A generated video can look beautiful while a ball changes speed for no reason. A repaired medical image can look cleaner while removing evidence a doctor needed. A world benchmark can reward style while missing that the scene broke its own rules. The artifact must satisfy the domain, not just look plausible.

State Space

The state is the generated artifact, physical scene, force or contact relation, time order, domain rule, corruption process, repaired image, evaluator observation, human or model judgment, and constraint violation.

Object

The object is the leftover mismatch after checking the artifact against the rule of its world. If a ball teleports, the mismatch is physical. If a repaired medical image removes a clue, the mismatch is clinical. If a world scene looks good frame by frame but cannot be one consistent world, the mismatch is in the world rule. PhyWorldBench tests whether video obeys physical principles. AdAEM-style evaluation asks whether artifact or anomaly scoring follows the real domain variable. WoW-style benchmarks test world consistency rather than frame quality alone. ImageDoctor treats repair as preserving diagnostic or meaning-bearing content while changing corrupted pixels. Name used later: domain constraint residual is the rule-breaking left after comparison with the domain rule.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is domain validity: gravity, contact, conservation, time consistency, diagnostic evidence, or artifact identity. The model may change pixels or frames, but the domain relation must remain true.

Allowed Movement

What may change is synthesis, denoising, restoration, simulation, or scoring. Evaluation must happen in the domain's own state, not only through generic visual quality.

Why It Works

It works by checking the rule where the rule lives. A physical video is not just plausible frames; it is a path where nearby moments should obey gravity, contact, object permanence, and time order. An image repair is not just a cleaner picture; it should remove corruption while keeping the evidence a doctor or domain expert needs. A useful benchmark makes the hidden rule violation visible enough to compare systems. If a generic judge only says "looks good," it can reward the exact artifact that the domain would reject.

an artifact is valid when the domain rule error stays small while task evidence is preserved

Evidence Check

Use physical scenes not seen during development, tests for contact and object permanence, checks that adjacent moments agree over time, domain-expert review, repair audits for each corruption type, measured rule mismatch, and examples where visual quality improves while domain validity gets worse.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the rule mismatch is guessed only loosely by a judge, when the benchmark covers too small a part of the physical world, when repair removes rare evidence, or when a world model learns benchmark cues without preserving the underlying law.

Connection

IRNO and RealPDEBench evaluate stable physical operators; VIST3A, TideGS, and RadioGS use renderable native state; Depth Anything 3 recovers coordinate structure. PhyWorldBench, AdAEM, WoW!, and ImageDoctor add the measurement side: domain generation must be judged where the domain constraint lives.

EXEMPLAR 110

TokSuite, Linguistic Nepotism, ATLAS Multilingual Transfer, And Local Redundancy

Seeing How Language Changes The Evidence A Model Can Use

Problem

The same fact can be cheap to write in one language and expensive in another. A search system may prefer familiar-language sources even when another source is stronger. A translation may carry the words but lose the local use. Language is not a neutral wrapper; it changes cost, retrieval, and evidence access.

State Space

The state is language, script, tokenizer, token length, meaning, internal neighborhood of similar texts, retrieval candidates, citation choice, repeated or new support among sources, transfer source, target task, and downstream answer quality.

Object

The object is the shape of representation and retrieval across languages. The same meaning can become long or short, nearby or far away, easy or hard to retrieve, depending on the language interface. TokSuite measures whether tokenizers handle native language perturbations robustly. Linguistic Nepotism studies citation preference by language instead of evidence relevance. ATLAS multilingual transfer estimates how training languages help target evaluations. Local Redundancy asks when nearby examples add real information rather than repeats. Name used later: geometry here means which items become near, far, cheap, costly, easy to retrieve, or easy to ignore.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence value across language interfaces. The same meaning should not become less useful only because one language costs more tokens, lands farther away internally, or is disfavored by retrieval.

Allowed Movement

What may change is tokenization, internal representation, translation, retrieval, citation, transfer, or duplicate removal. These operations should preserve meaning-bearing evidence and expose when cost or retrieval bias changes available support.

Why It Works

It works by treating language as a coordinate system, not a neutral wrapper. Token length changes how much budget a fact consumes. Internal neighborhoods change which documents get retrieved. Citation habits can favor familiar-language sources over better evidence. Transfer helps only when the source language shares the structure the target task needs, not when it merely shares surface patterns. A second source helps only if it adds new support rather than repeating the same evidence in a favored language. The preserved thing is meaning-bearing evidence; the allowed change is the language route used to reach it.

language evidence value depends on meaning, token cost, retrieval rank, shared structure, and nonrepeated support

Evidence Check

Measure how many extra tokens each language costs, whether small native-language changes break the tokenizer, which training languages help which target languages, whether cited sources are relevant rather than merely familiar, how translation changes retrieval rank, whether an added source brings new evidence, and low-resource cases where average multilingual scores hide interface failure.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when translation changes the task, when token tests miss cultural usage, when retrieval relevance is judged only in high-resource languages, or when source-language usefulness is inferred from correlation without a test that varies the source language itself.

Connection

Common Corpus treats provenance as a data variable; Token Overcharging makes representation cost economic; LIMSSR keeps missing evidence explicit; ATLAS item selection treats tests as information. TokSuite, Linguistic Nepotism, multilingual transfer, and redundancy work show the language version: evidence has geometry before reasoning begins.

EXEMPLAR 111

AstaBench, SimuHome, MiniAppBench, Vision2Web, And PhotoAgent

Measuring Agents By What They Discover And Change

Problem

A lab assistant may need to search papers, a home assistant may need to schedule devices, an app tester may need to discover bugs, and a photo editor may need several preference-guided edits. These tasks do not have one fixed answer. They are successful when the agent explores, changes state, and produces evidence or an artifact that satisfies the goal.

State Space

The state is the environment, user intent, tool budget, observation history, device or app state, generated artifact, visual feedback, preference reward, exploratory action, discovered failure, and final assessment.

Object

The object is exploration over states and artifacts while only seeing part of the world. The agent cannot know everything up front, so it must act, observe what changed, and choose the next move. AstaBench controls research tools and costs for scientific agents. SimuHome uses a time-aware home simulator. MiniAppBench turns apps into exploratory testing targets. Vision2Web evaluates web-building through visual and functional checks. PhotoAgent treats image editing as step-by-step visual control. Name used later: a partially observed exploration process means learning what to do by acting and observing when the full world is not visible at once.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is legitimate progress under the task's own state variables: evidence gathered, device state changed, artifact behavior discovered, web intent satisfied, or image preference improved without breaking earlier constraints.

Allowed Movement

What may change is search, click, query, edit, scheduling, testing, or revision. Every movement should be judged by the state it reaches and the evidence it uncovers, not by whether it sounds like a plausible plan.

Why It Works

It works because open-ended agency is closer to experiment design than classification. The agent should spend actions to reveal hidden state, test a guess, improve an artifact, or decide that a path is not worth continuing. The benchmark needs an environment with visible state changes, a cost model, and a way to score discovered evidence or artifact behavior. The answer is the work path plus the final state, not a sentence describing the work path. A good score therefore asks whether each action bought progress that mattered, not whether the agent looked busy.

agent value = final-state utility plus discovered evidence minus action and tool costs

Evidence Check

Report logs of each state change, tool costs, simulator events, which parts of the app or environment were explored, visual and functional artifact checks, whether preference scores match the user's intent, and cases where final artifacts look acceptable but interaction traces reveal broken behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the simulator leaves out real constraints, when exploratory tests reward shallow coverage, when visual judges miss interaction bugs, when preference scores reward style over task intent, or when tool budgets differ enough that model comparisons no longer mean the same thing.

Connection

Gaia2 and tau2-bench evaluate stateful trajectories; WebDevJudge and OmniVerifier judge native artifacts; ParetoPO turns tool use into a frontier. AstaBench, SimuHome, MiniAppBench, Vision2Web, and PhotoAgent add the open-ended case: capability is measured by exploration under a real state model.

EXEMPLAR 112

TabStruct, Neon, FAC Synthesis, SurvDiff, And PetaGAIL++

Generating Data That Keeps The Relationships The Task Uses

Problem

A fake spreadsheet can match the average age and average income while destroying the relationship between age, income, disease, and treatment. A fake mobility trace can look realistic while exposing private routines or losing traffic patterns. Synthetic data helps only when it preserves the relationships the downstream task needs.

State Space

The state is the real data source, synthetic generator, relationships between table columns, hidden features, generator bias, event time, the rule that decides when observation stops, mobility path, privacy noise, downstream task, and lost usefulness.

Object

The object is a generated data source constrained by the structure the task uses. The fake data does not need to copy real rows. It needs to keep the relationships that make later analysis work: which columns move together, which features are rare, when events happen, why observation stops, and which movement patterns matter. TabStruct measures whether synthetic tables preserve relationships without assuming a known causal graph. Neon uses generator bias as negative evidence about missing regions. FAC Synthesis targets coverage of sparse-autoencoder features. SurvDiff preserves event timing and censoring. PetaGAIL++ generates private mobility paths while keeping movement utility. Name used later: a synthetic distribution is the probability source that creates generated examples.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the structure the later analysis uses: relationships between variables, features that matter to the model, rules about when observation stops, movement patterns, or privacy-protected behavior patterns that still support the analysis.

Allowed Movement

What may change is sampling, imitation, perturbation, censoring, rebalancing, or privacy noise. Those changes should be controlled by what the downstream task needs, not by visible averages alone.

Why It Works

It works when generation is treated as designing a replacement data source for a named use. A table that preserves single-column averages but breaks the relationship between treatment and outcome is not useful for treatment analysis. Bias can reveal missing regions because the generator fails where it has not learned the source well. Feature-targeted synthesis can fill coverage holes when the generated examples still look like valid cases. Survival data must preserve both events and the process that stops observation. Mobility data must preserve aggregate movement while hiding individual routines. The test is downstream: does the analysis reach the same kind of answer on generated data as it would on real data?

synthetic data is useful when the downstream analysis on generated data matches the analysis on real data while privacy and coverage constraints hold

Evidence Check

Compare downstream task performance, recovery of relationships between variables, coverage of important model features, event times and observation-stopping patterns, movement summaries, private-information exposure, how real and generated variables move together, and regions where synthetic samples expose rather than hide generator failure.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when synthetic data preserves easy one-column summaries but breaks interactions, when feature coverage creates unnatural examples, when stopped observation is treated as ordinary missing data, when privacy noise erases rare movement structure, or when downstream validation is too narrow.

Connection

Common Corpus treats source as part of the data distribution; LIMSSR keeps missingness explicit; DiReCT values data by optimizer effect; GoodDiffusion attaches authorization to samples. TabStruct, Neon, FAC Synthesis, SurvDiff, and PetaGAIL++ define the synthetic-data rule: generate the structure the claim uses.

EXEMPLAR 113

Semantic Watermark Fingerprints, Ellipse Signatures, GoodDiffusion, Spherical Watermark, And WaterSIC

Keeping A Source Mark Detectable After Normal Changes

Problem

A receipt is not useful if it vanishes when the paper is folded. A watermark is not useful if it disappears after fine-tuning, quantization, filtering, editing, paraphrase, or platform conversion. Provenance methods have to survive the changes people actually apply while staying hard to forge.

State Space

The state is the original model or sample, secret key, watermark signal, meaning domain, output probabilities, diffusion input signature, transformation process, attacker action, detector statistic, false positive, and false negative.

Object

The object is a test that can still tell marked from unmarked after transformation. A good mark must survive ordinary handling, stay small enough not to damage the artifact, and remain hard for an attacker to forge or remove. Semantic Watermark Fingerprints place marks in broad meaning domains so ownership can survive model changes. Ellipse Signatures verify models from output probabilities. GoodDiffusion conditions generation on valid per-input signatures and shows signature recovery attacks. Spherical Watermark and WaterSIC study watermark shapes that preserve the output distribution while remaining detectable. Name used later: a hypothesis test decides between "unmarked" and "marked" while controlling mistake rates.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is a controlled signal: detectable by authorized checking, low-distortion for normal users, low false-positive on unrelated models or artifacts, and resistant to likely transformations.

Allowed Movement

What may change is fine-tuning, quantization, filtering, paraphrase, editing, or generation through another system. The provenance claim must state which transformation path the signal is expected to survive.

Why It Works

It works by placing a small, recoverable pattern where normal transformations do not erase it. The detector then asks which story better explains the observed artifact: it came from an unmarked source, or it came from a marked one that passed through allowed changes. The mark must be strong enough to detect and weak enough not to change normal quality. The allowed transformations must be stated, because a mark that survives paraphrase may not survive fine-tuning or filtering. GoodDiffusion also shows the risk: once a signature controls generation, attackers may try to infer the signature itself.

detect provenance when a statistic separates marked from unmarked after the stated transformation path at controlled error rates

Evidence Check

Report how often unmarked artifacts are wrongly flagged and marked artifacts are missed, how the signal survives each transformation, whether it survives fine-tuning and smaller-number deployment, whether output probabilities verify the source, whether meaning-domain marks transfer, whether quality or probability changes noticeably, whether adaptive attackers can remove the mark, and whether signatures can be recovered.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the deployment channel erases the statistic, when the watermark changes user-visible quality, when detector access assumptions are unrealistic, when semantic domains are too coarse, or when authorization signatures can be reverse-engineered.

Connection

Catch-22 frames watermarking as a robustness-stealth tradeoff; LLM DNA fingerprints model lineage; Common Corpus and Copyright-Bench name provenance constraints. This family states the statistical core: provenance is useful only when distinguishability survives the transformations people actually apply.

EXEMPLAR 114

Performative Misalignment, LLM Adaptability Limits, MNPO, DPO Unchained, And What Preferences Can And Cannot Predict

Figuring Out Which Failure Story The Behavior Actually Supports

Problem

If a student gives the answer the teacher wants, they might understand the topic, flatter the teacher, copy a pattern, or be confused but lucky. Alignment behavior has the same ambiguity. A model that looks aligned may be deceptive, sycophantic, prompt-brittle, stuck in conflicting preferences, or optimized with the wrong choice rule.

State Space

The state is the prompt, evaluator identity, model belief, preference observation, policy population, choice model, payoff table, adaptation attempt, high-confidence error, candidate mechanism, and observed aligned or misaligned behavior.

Object

The object is the set of failure stories still consistent with the evidence. The evaluator should ask which stories remain possible after the tests: deception, researcher pleasing, task misunderstanding, preference conflict, prompt limits, or a training rule that pushes the wrong way. Performative Misalignment asks whether apparent alignment-faking may instead be pleasing researchers. LLM Adaptability Limits shows many annotation errors cannot be fixed by prompting. MNPO treats preference optimization as a many-player problem where each side's response changes the others. DPO Unchained checks whether preference objectives match their assumptions about how choices are made. What Preferences Can and Cannot Predict studies when preference observations remain stable in multi-agent settings. Name used later: an identified set is the group of mechanisms the evidence has not ruled out.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the causal explanation of behavior. The method should preserve the distinction between deception, evaluator pleasing, task-definition mismatch, prompt-level correction limits, strategic preference cycles, and objective mismatch.

Allowed Movement

What may change is prompts, evaluator identity, preference populations, adaptation budgets, or objective assumptions. The evaluator should not collapse all failures into one story unless the evidence rules out the alternatives.

Why It Works

It works by treating behavior as evidence, not as the mechanism itself. The same aligned-looking answer can come from understanding, obedience to the evaluator, imitation, confusion, or strategy. To know what to fix, the test must create conditions where those stories predict different behavior. If researcher identity changes the answer, pleasing the evaluator becomes plausible. If prompting cannot fix confident errors, the problem is not only wording. If preferences cycle or differ across groups, one reward climb may not represent the population. If the preference objective assumes the wrong choice process, optimization can move the model in the wrong direction. The method is useful when it narrows the set of live stories rather than forcing one label too early.

diagnose alignment by listing behavior-consistent mechanisms, then using interventions that separate those mechanisms

Evidence Check

Run evaluator-identity swaps, prompt variations that try to rescue errors, audits of confident wrong answers, checks for preference cycles across groups, tests for whether the many-player optimization has actually settled, checks of the choice assumptions behind the objective, and interventions where deception and evaluator-pleasing predict different behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when tests only rename the same prompt pressure, when preference data lacks the diversity needed to reveal cycles, when prompt failures are mistaken for permanent incapability, or when an optimization objective is judged only by average preference score.

Connection

Pressure Reveals Character exposes behavior under intervention; VALUEFLOW measures value intensity; ParetoPO and SafeDPO make tradeoffs explicit; RLVepsR studies verifier gradients. Performative Misalignment, adaptability limits, MNPO, DPO Unchained, and preference-predictability work add the diagnostic rule: alignment is not solved until the mechanism behind the observed behavior is identified.

EXEMPLAR 115

PonderLM-2, Reasoning With Sampling, T3, THOR, Rational Agents, And NEXCO

Spending Extra Thinking Only When It Changes The Answer

Problem

A person solving a puzzle can stare longer, try another route, ask a clarifying question, use a calculator, or stop. More time helps only if it removes confusion or opens a route that was blocked. Model reasoning has the same issue. Extra hidden steps, extra samples, longer tool sessions, or more questions are useful only when they buy information, remove a mistake, or make a partial answer possible.

State Space

The state is the prompt, what the model currently thinks, alternative answer attempts, what is still uncertain, the partial solution, tool results, the cost of asking a question, the current work path, verifier feedback, compute budget, and final decision.

Object

The object is a rule for deciding whether another thinking step is worth paying for. Imagine doing a crossword: another minute helps if it rules out a wrong clue, reveals a new crossing word, or shows that you should stop and ask for help. It does not help if you only repeat the same guess. PonderLM-2 spends hidden thinking steps. Reasoning with Sampling tries several answer routes without letting them collapse into copies of one route. T3 cuts off parts of a work path that no longer change the belief. THOR uses immediate tool feedback to correct itself. Rational Agents choose questions or actions by how much useful uncertainty they remove. NEXCO grows partial solutions that still obey the constraints. Name used later: a controlled computation policy is a rule for buying more computation only when it can improve the decision.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful progress per unit cost. Each extra thought, sample, question, or tool call should reduce uncertainty, move a partial solution closer to being allowed, or expose evidence that changes the next action.

Allowed Movement

What may change is internal thinking, sampling alternatives, asking questions, calling tools, cutting off weak work paths, or expanding partial plans. Those moves should be controlled by what they add, not by a blanket preference for longer reasoning traces.

Why It Works

It works because reasoning is a series of paid checks. A sample can reveal a different route. A tool call can replace a guess with an observation. A question can remove a missing condition. Stopping can prevent low-value drift. The preserved thing is useful change in belief or action. The allowed movement is extra hidden thought, extra samples, questions, tools, or pruning. The method is trustworthy only when those moves are chosen because the next check might change the answer enough to justify the cost, not because long traces look more serious.

continue reasoning when the expected gain from the next check is larger than its compute, time, and error cost

Evidence Check

Report how accuracy changes as more compute is spent, whether samples are genuinely different and correct, which belief changes caused stopping, whether tool feedback was used, whether questions were worth their cost, what happens when prefixes are cut, and cases where longer reasoning makes answers worse.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when hidden thinking is not grounded in evidence, when samples are copies with different wording, when tool feedback is ignored, when questions are free in the benchmark but costly in deployment, or when truncation removes the one early clue that would have recovered the answer.

Connection

The Tell-Tale Norm estimates where thought is intense; h1 makes long horizons learnable; Weak-Strong Verification buys stronger evidence near uncertainty boundaries; ParetoPO prices tools. This family states the common rule: reasoning compute should be spent where it changes the state of knowledge.

EXEMPLAR 116

SVD As Fast Interpretability, Neuron-Basis Circuits, MDA, AI Engram, FlashTrace, T-SAE, And Temporal Superposition

Showing Which Part Actually Caused The Output

Problem

An explanation can point to a word, neuron, feature, or training example and still not show cause. It is like saying a lamp turned on because the wall is white. The real question is which piece received the evidence, which piece passed it forward, and whether changing that piece would change the answer.

State Space

The state is the training example, internal activation values, neuron or small weight pattern, feature list, memory trace, path from input to output, feature that changes over time, intervention on a circuit, output change, and evidence that the explanation is faithful.

Object

The object is a cause map from source to behavior. A useful explanation should read like a wiring note: this training example helped build this internal part; this text span activated it; this part passed the signal forward; changing it changed this output. SVD as Fast Interpretability splits a weight block into simple directions that detect one thing and write another. Neuron-Basis Circuits asks whether native neurons already form usable causal parts. MDA links influential examples to the later formation of induction heads and in-context learning. AI Engram isolates artificial memory traces by asking what hidden memory could have produced the behavior. FlashTrace follows long-context influence through text spans. T-SAE asks for features that stay consistent over time. Temporal Superposition studies what happens when several time-based signals are packed into the same limited space. Name used later: an attributed mechanism graph maps which source, internal part, and output effect are causally linked.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is causal responsibility. The named source, direction, feature, or memory component should be the reason the computation changes, not merely something that appears nearby when the answer is produced.

Allowed Movement

What may change is how the analyst splits weights, traces influence, patches activations, removes examples, recovers memory vectors, or groups text spans. The explanation must survive an intervention: change the alleged cause and the target behavior should change in the predicted way.

Why It Works

It works by turning explanation into a cause-and-effect test. Splitting a weight block helps only if one exposed direction really detects a condition or writes a result. A neuron explanation helps only if changing that neuron changes the relevant behavior. Data attribution matters when training examples helped build the internal procedure now being used. Memory matters when the model carries information from earlier inputs into later answers. Time features matter only if the same feature continues to play the same role as context changes. The plain rule is: an explanation is stronger when changing the named part changes the output, while changing nearby irrelevant parts does not.

a mechanism claim holds when changing the named component changes the target behavior and control changes do not

Evidence Check

Run activation patching, remove or change claimed parts, remove or add source examples, test memory creation and erasure, check whether highlighted spans are truly used, test features across time, and audit unwanted behavior changes outside the target claim.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when a weight split is convenient but not causal, when highlighted tokens are correlated with the answer but unused, when many different hidden memories could explain the same output, or when shared time features make one feature look responsible for another.

Connection

Steer Like the LLM turns directions into control; Causal Route Gating tests modality routes; Neural Effect Search treats features as causal discovery units. SVD, neuron-basis, data-attribution, engram, and temporal-feature papers supply the missing bridge from explanation to mechanism.

EXEMPLAR 117

FALCON, FIDIA, Control Consistency Losses, Dimension-Free Diffusion Sampling, High-Accuracy Sampling, And Manifold Perturbations

Drawing Samples That Preserve The Scientific Quantity

Problem

A scientist may not care whether one generated molecule or state looks plausible. They may care about an average energy, the best candidate in a batch, the chance of a rare event, or whether every sample stays on a valid physical surface. A sampler can make nice-looking examples while breaking the number the scientist wanted to estimate.

State Space

The state is the target source of samples, proposed sampling path, flow map, probability score, energy, valid-surface coordinate, rare-event route, selected sample batch, downstream quantity, estimation bias, and variation across repeated estimates.

Object

The object is movement of probability that preserves the quantity read from the samples. Think of a lab that draws test tubes from a freezer. If the scientist needs the average energy, the rare dangerous reaction, or the best candidate in a small batch, the drawing rule must protect that number. Plausible-looking samples are not enough. FALCON learns fast molecular flows while keeping probability scores accurate for equilibrium estimates. FIDIA trains for the best sample selected from a finite batch. Control Consistency Losses learn routes into rare events. Dimension-Free and High-Accuracy Sampling ask whether guarantees survive higher dimensions or stricter precision. Manifold Perturbations keep movement on the valid surface. Name used later: functional-preserving probability movement means the sampler may move probability around, but the downstream quantity must stay correct.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the scientific quantity the samples are used to estimate: density, free energy, rare-event chance, best-candidate value, valid-surface average, or coverage of important modes.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the sampling route, bridge into rare states, small perturbation, control rule, or batch selection. Those changes are allowed only if they preserve the quantity behind the scientific claim.

Why It Works

It works by matching the sampling method to the number being estimated. If the number is an equilibrium density, probability accounting matters because common and uncommon states must appear in the right proportions. If the number is the best item in a batch, the high end of the distribution matters more than the average. If the number is a rare-event chance, ordinary samples waste effort in common regions and may never visit the important event. If valid states lie on a special surface, moves that leave that surface create invalid samples. The sampler is good when it puts effort where the final scientific quantity is sensitive and can state the remaining error.

a sampler is useful when the scientific quantity estimated from generated samples matches the quantity from the target source within known error

Evidence Check

Report probability-score calibration, free-energy or density checks, best-of-batch calibration, enough effective rare-event samples, valid-surface rule violations, behavior as dimension grows, coverage of important modes, and estimation bias on held-out scientific systems.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when visual diversity hides probability error, when best-of-batch training overfits a proxy score, when rare-event control misses part of the tail, when surface-preserving moves only approximately stay valid, or when high-dimensional guarantees assume a smoother target than the domain provides.

Connection

Safety-Guided Flow and DMPO move distributions under reward or safety pressure; Error Propagation in Quantized Diffusion tracks path error; IRNO and RealPDEBench evaluate physical operators. FALCON, FIDIA, Control Consistency, and sampling-theory papers make the scientific rule explicit: the sample path is valid only relative to the quantity being estimated.

EXEMPLAR 118

Optimal MoE Sparsity, Pre-Training Under Infinite Compute, Scaling Laws Origin, Shallow NN Scaling Laws, And Power-Law Compositional Reasoning

Separating The Different Resources Hidden Inside "Bigger"

Problem

Saying a model improved because it got bigger is like saying a restaurant improved because it spent more money. The money could have gone to a larger kitchen, better ingredients, more cooks, more training, or repeated practice on the same menu. Model scaling has the same ambiguity: parameters, active compute, token budget, repeated data, data structure, memorization, reasoning, and post-training can all change the result.

State Space

The state is stored parameters, active experts, compute used per example, tokens per parameter, uncertainty in the data source, number of repeated passes over data, regularization, ensemble or distillation rule, source generator, strength of learnable directions, compositional depth, and downstream capability.

Object

The object is a map from resources to capability. The map should not say only "bigger helped." It should say what kind of bigger: more stored facts, more active computation per example, more data passes, more varied examples, more useful structure, or more post-training. Optimal MoE Sparsity separates stored capacity from active compute and shows memorization and reasoning respond differently. Pre-Training Under Infinite Compute asks how to get more signal from fixed data when compute is abundant. Scaling Laws Origin studies simple data sources that create power-law curves. Shallow NN Scaling Laws links improvement to which feature directions are available. Power-Law Compositional Reasoning asks how part-whole structure changes the curve. Name used later: a resource-allocation surface keeps these resource axes separate instead of flattening them into one size number.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the meaning of the scaling claim. A curve should say which resource changed, which capability was measured, and what structure in the data made improvement possible.

Allowed Movement

What may change is stored parameter count, active experts, repeated data passes, regularization, ensembling, distillation, token budget, or curriculum. Those movements should be named separately because they buy different kinds of improvement.

Why It Works

It works because different failures are blocked by different resources. Memorizing facts may need more storage. Reasoning may need examples that show how parts combine. Fixed-data training may need repeated use of the same data, but only with rules that prevent fitting noise. Sparse experts can save compute only if the active route still sees enough evidence for the current task. Power laws need not mean scale has magic in it; they can come from the source structure and from which directions are easy or hard to learn. The useful claim is not "scale works"; it is which resource removed which bottleneck.

capability should be reported as a result of active compute, stored capacity, token budget, repetition, data structure, and post-training together

Evidence Check

Report active versus total parameter tests, token-budget sweeps, repeated-pass tests on fixed data, controls over the data source, measurements of which feature directions are learned, compositional-depth tests, memorization-versus-reasoning splits, and prediction error when post-training changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when a fitted power law is extrapolated outside the data regime, when gains are actually post-training effects, when memorization improvements are sold as reasoning improvements, or when sparse expert routing saves compute but starves the active path of needed evidence.

Connection

Prescriptive Scaling attaches scores to the recipe that produced them; Language Generation in the Limit separates what is possible from what evidence can teach; Alignment-Sensitive Rates asks where useful signal sits. Scaling-law and MoE papers add the resource view: progress depends on how compute, data, and structure fit together, not on a single size variable.

EXEMPLAR 119

Linear Causal Representation Learning, LC-PB-SCM, Robust Causal Discovery With Power-Laws, And Unpaired Causal IV

Learning Variables You Can Actually Intervene On

Problem

A model can predict that wet sidewalks go with umbrellas, but that does not tell it what happens if you make people carry umbrellas. Prediction can mix cause, effect, and shared background into one useful-looking feature. Causal representation work asks for internal variables that still mean something when one factor is changed on purpose.

State Space

The state is observed data, hidden factors, targets for intervention, changes across environments, variables that shift one cause without directly shifting the outcome, time-series patterns, structural equations, hidden common causes, limits on how many independent directions the data can support, and the causal effect or graph that can be justified.

Object

The object is a coordinate system whose parts have intervention meaning. A useful coordinate is not just a prediction feature; changing it should correspond to changing one real factor while the causal question remains meaningful. Linear Causal Representation Learning asks when hidden coordinates recover causal variables. LC-PB-SCM studies causal models when some assumptions are partly broken or some structure is hidden. Robust Causal Discovery with Power-Laws extracts stable time-pattern features before graph discovery. Unpaired Causal IV uses special variables, called instruments, when matched "before and after" examples are unavailable. Name used later: an identifiable causal coordinate system means the evidence and assumptions force the same answer about the causal variable of interest.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is intervention meaning. A coordinate is useful if changing it corresponds to a stable causal operation, not just a rotated prediction feature that happens to fit the data.

Allowed Movement

What may change is the representation of observations, which environments are compared, which instruments are used, how time-series features are compressed, or whether the answer is a set of still-possible causal stories. The method must state which causal distinctions the evidence fixes and which ones remain unresolved.

Why It Works

It works only when the data contains some difference that prediction alone cannot explain away. An intervention shows what changes when one factor is forced. An instrument helps when it moves a cause without directly moving the outcome. An environment shift helps when the same relation survives some changes and breaks under others. Stable time patterns can help separate causes that move on different schedules. What must stay the same is the meaning of the "what if we changed this?" question. What may change is the representation used to expose the variable. The model does not get causality from low error; it gets causal meaning when every story still compatible with the evidence agrees on the target intervention claim.

a causal claim is justified when all evidence-compatible stories give the same answer to the target "what if we changed this?" question

Evidence Check

Report tests across changed environments, checks that instruments move only what they are supposed to move, interventions on learned coordinates, lists of still-possible causal stories, stress tests for hidden common causes, stability of time-pattern features, and counterexamples where predictive features rotate while causal meaning changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when instruments are weak or invalid, when an environment shift changes several mechanisms at once, when stable time features are not causal, when learned coordinates can rotate in ways that change intervention meaning, or when the paper reports one graph even though the evidence supports many.

Connection

Distributional Equivalence and DiCoLa define what passive evidence can identify; Local Covariate Selection targets one adjustment claim; CausalGame adds active experiments. Causal representation papers add the coordinate question: the model must learn variables that interventions can act on.

EXEMPLAR 120

Compositional Generalization Requires Linear Orthogonal Representations, Concept Binding, Emergent Analogical Reasoning, Grammar Substructure, And T-SAE

Reusing Parts In New Combinations

Problem

A child who knows "red square" and "blue circle" should be able to understand "blue square." A model can fail this if it memorized whole phrases instead of learning reusable parts. The same problem appears in grammar, roles, analogies, and relations: the parts must survive when arranged in a new way.

State Space

The state is the concept part, the operation that binds parts together, relation, analogy map, grammar feature, token context, internal direction, separation between directions, compositional task, and generalization error.

Object

The object is a representation where useful parts stay separable and can be joined into a whole. If "blue" and "square" are stored as one memorized phrase, the model may not know what to do with "blue triangle." The parts need their own handles, and the joining rule needs to combine them without erasing either part. Compositional Generalization Requires Linear Orthogonal Representations argues that reusable concepts need directions that do not overwrite each other. Concept Binding studies how parts combine without too much complexity. Emergent Analogical Reasoning tests whether relation structure can move to a new domain. Grammar Substructure and T-SAE recover internal features that line up with reusable grammar and time context. Name used later: a factorized representation with controlled binding keeps the parts recoverable while joining them into a structured whole.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is part identity under recombination. A concept should remain recoverable when paired with a new context, and a relation should remain usable when the objects in the relation change.

Allowed Movement

What may change is encoding, binding, attention, or composition. The representation should keep reusable parts distinct enough that valid new combinations can be reached without learning every pair from scratch.

Why It Works

It works because compositional tasks are built from pieces. If color and shape are stored in tangled form, changing color can accidentally change shape. If they are stored separately enough, the model can reuse each one. Binding is the step that joins parts without erasing them. Analogies work when the relation, not the surface object, is what transfers. Grammar works when a high-level structure can survive local word changes while still keeping the local words available. The preserved thing is part identity; the allowed change is the new arrangement.

generalization improves when parts remain recoverable after being combined into a new valid structure

Evidence Check

Use held-out combinations of parts, relation-transfer tests, analogy swaps, grammar changes, probes that recover features, tests that remove or change the binding operation, measurements of whether directions interfere, and cases where high training accuracy hides failure on new combinations.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when apparent parts are dataset artifacts, when separating variables prevents needed interaction, when binding preserves symbols but not relations, or when grammar features are readable to people but not actually used by the model.

Connection

GraphGlue transfers relational coordinates across graph domains; Alignment-Sensitive Rates asks where target signal sits in spectra; Neuron-Basis Circuits tests causal feature bases. Compositional representation papers answer the big generalization question: what must be separated so the system can recombine it later?

EXEMPLAR 121

LVLMs-Saliency, KL-Minimal Visual MI, Visual Symbolic Mechanisms, RFCMH, And AGREE

Checking Which Part Of The Image Supported The Claim

Problem

A model may answer "the dog is wet" because it saw water on the fur, or because captions about dogs often mention baths. The answer can be right for the wrong reason. Visual grounding asks whether the claim is controlled by the relevant part of the current image rather than by language habit or a nearby but irrelevant visual clue.

State Space

The state is image region, where attention goes, how changing pixels would change the answer, visual token, text prior, visual relation such as "on top of" or "inside," generated claim, attribute score, image-text consistency check, and hallucination event.

Object

The object is responsibility for a visual claim. The method should name the patch, object relation, color, layout, or image-text match that actually made the answer possible. LVLMs-Saliency combines attention with change-sensitivity to ask whether answers stay grounded in the current image. KL-Minimal Visual MI asks what visual information is necessary, not merely present: remove image information until removing any more would change the claim. Visual Symbolic Mechanisms test whether visual relations support reasoning. RFCMH uses image-text consistency to repair unsupported generations. AGREE treats subjective visual judgment as several attributes rather than one score. Name used later: localized evidence responsibility means naming the region, relation, or attribute that actually supports the claim.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is support. The output claim should trace back to the image region, visual relation, or attribute that justifies it.

Allowed Movement

What may change is attention, image-text fusion, repair, attribution, or aggregation of human judgments. The method should keep the visual evidence route separate from language priors and preserve which attribute or region caused the decision.

Why It Works

It works by asking a simple change test: if the claimed visual evidence were removed or changed, would the answer change? A heatmap helps only if it points to evidence the model actually uses. Minimal visual information helps because it asks how much image evidence is enough for the claim. Symbolic visual relations help when the issue is not a patch of pixels but a relation between things. Multi-attribute judgment helps because one average score can hide whether color, layout, realism, or safety caused the evaluation. Name used later: a counterfactual is this kind of "what if the evidence were different?" test.

a visual claim is grounded when changing the responsible visual evidence changes the claim more than changing irrelevant image or text cues

Evidence Check

Mask image regions, swap text priors, compare attention with change-sensitivity, perturb visual relations, test image-text repair, check attribute-level judgment reliability, and trace hallucinations where attention moves away from the image evidence.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the highlight map is an after-the-fact picture rather than a cause, when masking changes the task accidentally, when symbolic labels omit important continuous cues, when repair models smooth away evidence, or when attribute scores are not reliable enough to split the judgment.

Connection

VISUALSWAP and MINT test modality causality; DLMR preserves multimodal memory provenance; Causal Route Gating intervenes on internal routes. LVLMs-Saliency, KL-Minimal Visual MI, symbolic visual mechanisms, RFCMH, and AGREE make grounding local: which visual evidence was responsible?

EXEMPLAR 122

CAT-Q, ETTFS, Rex, Frozen-PINN, S3GNN, And Solving Time-Dependent Differential Equations With Physical Dynamical Systems

Making Sure The Computation That Runs Still Solves The Same Problem

Problem

A formula on paper can be correct while the version that runs on hardware is not. Smaller numbers can erase rare signals. Spike timing can save work but miss needed events. A solver can be reversible in theory but preserve numerical junk. A graph model can pass local messages while missing long-range physics. The computation that actually runs is part of the scientific claim.

State Space

The state is model weights, number format, GPU budget, event time, spike state, ordinary or noisy differential-equation solver state, physical field, frozen feature map, graph mesh, long-range operator, quantity that physics says should stay true, and hardware trace.

Object

The object is a computation over time as realized by a machine. The paper algorithm, the number format, the timing of events, and the hardware path all become part of the same claim because any one of them can change the result. CAT-Q studies practical three-value weight conversion under GPU constraints. ETTFS uses time-to-first-spike as the inference schedule. Rex uses reversible solver structure for ordinary and noisy differential equations. Frozen-PINN fixes feature bases for physical equation solving. S3GNN mixes local graph messages with long-range graph structure. Physical TDDE solvers use physical systems as part of the computation. Name used later: dynamical computation realized on a machine means the math, number system, timing, and hardware behavior are one object to check.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the claim-bearing quantity after it passes through number format, solver steps, hardware timing, graph structure, and physical field structure.

Allowed Movement

What may change is quantization, event scheduling, reversing solver steps, freezing features, mixing local and long-range graph information, or using physical dynamics directly. The deployed computation must preserve the quantity the claim depends on in the arithmetic and timing regime where it actually runs.

Why It Works

It works when the algorithm matches the machine and the physics. Three-value weights help only if the important behavior survives the smaller alphabet. Event-driven inference helps only if early sparse events carry the needed information. Reversible solvers help only if the preserved state is real signal, not accumulated numerical artifact. Long-range graph mixing helps when distant parts of a physical system coordinate. Physical solvers are valid only when the hardware dynamics implement the target equation closely enough. The preserved thing is the claim-bearing quantity; the allowed change is the cheaper or different way of running the computation.

a deployed scientific computation is valid when the running machine approximates the target operation on the claim-bearing state within the tested tolerance

Evidence Check

Report hardware traces, behavior drift after smaller-number conversion, spike-latency curves, solver reversibility error, physical-equation rule mismatch, graph long-range ablations, tests of quantities physics says should stay true, and sensitivity to production-shaped workloads rather than only idealized runtime or loss.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the smaller number system erases rare directions, when spike timing is sparse but uninformative, when reversible solvers preserve numerical artifacts, when frozen features cannot represent boundary regimes, when long-range graph summaries miss local breaks, or when production hardware differs from the studied machine.

Connection

LiftQuant and TetraJet treat arithmetic as part of the model; TileLang and einx expose how tensor programs fit the machine; IRNO and RealPDEBench require stable physical operators. CAT-Q, ETTFS, Rex, Frozen-PINN, S3GNN, and physical TDDE solvers complete the machine-science bridge: implementation is part of the mathematical claim.

EXEMPLAR 123

Phase Retrieval Dynamics, Low-Rank LM Logits, Token Association Dynamics, And Top-W

Seeing The Smaller Pattern Behind Output Scores

Problem

A menu may have hundreds of dishes, but a customer may really be choosing along a few hidden lines: spicy or mild, cheap or expensive, familiar or new. A language model's next-token scores can look like a giant table, yet the decision may be controlled by a few shared score patterns. If we only inspect the final probabilities, we can miss the smaller pattern that made one token beat another.

State Space

The state is the model's hidden state, the matrix that turns hidden state into token scores, the table of scores for a prompt, links between related tokens, shared score patterns, sign or direction ambiguity, probability after normalization, selection rule, and chosen output token.

Object

The object is the recoverable pattern inside output scores. The model may score thousands of tokens, but many of those scores may move together because they share a smaller cause, such as topic, syntax role, safety pressure, or style. Low-Rank LM Logits asks whether many prompt-output scores are controlled by a small number of shared factors. Phase Retrieval Dynamics studies how a hidden signal can be recovered when the observations show strength but not the full direction. Token Association Dynamics treats token scores as linked choices where one token's score can move related tokens. Top-W studies choosing through a controlled scoring rule rather than always taking raw maximum probability. Name used later: recoverable output-score geometry means the structure inside the score table that can be found and used to explain the decision.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the score gap that matters for the decision: which alternatives are separated, which token associations created that separation, and which shared factors keep the separation stable when prompts or decoding rules change.

Allowed Movement

What may change is splitting the score table into factors, recovering hidden directions, smoothing or thresholding scores, following token associations, or calibrating the output rule. These moves should preserve the score differences that distinguish correct, plausible, unsafe, or irrelevant tokens.

Why It Works

It works when the huge vocabulary choice is not really using all dimensions independently. If many tokens move together, a few shared factors can explain much of the decision. If the model exposes enough information about how strong a hidden signal is, the missing direction can sometimes be recovered. If token choices are linked, the right question is not one isolated probability but how score changes spread through related tokens. The preserved thing is the score gap that changes the decision. The allowed movement is factorizing, smoothing, or changing the selection rule. Those moves are trustworthy only if the decision-making gap remains larger than noise, calibration error, and spillover from related tokens.

output behavior is stable when the score gaps that matter remain larger than noise, calibration error, and spillover from related tokens

Evidence Check

Measure how many shared factors explain the score table, perturb those factors, test direction recovery on held-out prompts, remove token-association links, compare raw-probability and constrained decoding decisions, and report where small score-gap changes flip behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when important behavior is carried by rare score patterns, when recovered directions are numerically real but meaningless for the task, when token-association graphs encode dataset artifacts, or when calibration rules hide uncertainty by forcing sharp choices.

Connection

Alignment-sensitive rates and Jacobian spectra study which directions are learnable; SVD interpretability and neuron-basis circuits study which internal directions explain behavior; speculative decoding asks whether output changes preserve a target distribution. These papers bring that question to the final score surface: what smaller structure inside the token scores is the model really using?

EXEMPLAR 124

Persistent Homology LLM Adversarial Influence, Neural Ricci Flow, Wasserstein GPCA, Riemannian Metric Matching, And Diffusion Spacetime

Measuring Distance Along The Space The Data Actually Uses

Problem

Two towns can be close on a map but far apart by road if a mountain blocks the direct route. Model representations have the same issue. Two internal points can look close by ordinary coordinate distance even when no meaningful data path connects them, or look far apart while being neighbors along the real data surface.

State Space

The state is an embedding vector, the path a representation takes across layers, a probability cloud, local distance rule, bending of the space, shortest meaningful path, holes or loops in the data shape, diffusion time, denoising state, adversarial change, and main direction of variation.

Object

The object is distance and shape measured inside the space real data traces out. The method asks which points are connected by data-like paths, where the space bends, where there are gaps, and which broad shape changes when the model is attacked or denoised. Persistent Homology LLM Adversarial Influence asks whether attacks change holes or connected pieces in activation clouds. Neural Ricci Flow studies smoothing by changing how local distances bend. Wasserstein GPCA searches for main directions of change between probability clouds. Riemannian Metric Matching learns local distance from denoising behavior. Diffusion Spacetime treats sample position and denoising time together. Name used later: intrinsic geometry means distances, paths, bends, and holes measured inside the represented data space rather than in arbitrary outside coordinates.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is neighborhood meaning. States that behave like neighbors should stay close along the learned space, and changes that alter behavior should show up as changes in path, bend, shape, or movement of probability.

Allowed Movement

What may change is smoothing, denoising, transporting probability, bending the distance rule, or re-labeling coordinates. The method should preserve meaningful adjacency and should not treat shortcuts outside the valid data space as real semantic movement.

Why It Works

It works because many failures are failures of the measuring ruler. A direct coordinate ruler can cut through invalid space, like measuring straight through a mountain. A path ruler asks whether there is a meaningful route through data-like states. Shape summaries can notice when an attack changes the cloud, not just one point. Local distance learned from denoising can reveal which changes the model treats as natural. Diffusion time matters because early noise and late clean samples are different stages, not one interchangeable coordinate. The method earns trust when its distance predicts behavior better than a straight-line ruler chosen for convenience.

representation distance is useful when it predicts behavior better than straight-line distance in arbitrary coordinates

Evidence Check

Compare road-like neighbors with straight-line neighbors, track shape summaries under ordinary and adversarial changes, measure whether smoothing preserves rare valid states, test probability-cloud directions under distribution shifts, validate learned distances against denoising transitions, and show that diffusion time changes sampling behavior.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when shape summaries are unstable under sampling noise, when learned distances reflect preprocessing rather than meaning, when smoothing erases rare but valid modes, when probability-transport directions are too expensive or not uniquely supported, or when diffusion time is modeled without behavioral evidence.

Connection

Quotient diffusion removes irrelevant symmetries; manifold perturbation keeps samples on valid spaces; fair transport treats social constraints as allowed matches. These geometry papers ask the broader question underneath them: what space should model movement actually be measured in?

EXEMPLAR 125

NAD, Neural Concept Verifier, CRV, Veritas, And Rashomon Trust

Testing Whether An Explanation Would Fail If It Were Wrong

Problem

An explanation can sound clean and still be weak. A doctor saying "because of fever" is not enough if the fever did not affect the diagnosis. A model explanation has the same problem: it may name a concept, neuron, or graph pattern that is readable to people but not actually responsible for the answer.

State Space

The state is internal activation values, small neuron set, concept encoding, attribution graph, evidence offered by a prover, verifier decision, reasoning trace, another accurate model, explanation set, and output label or answer.

Object

The object is concept evidence that has to pass a check. A concept explanation should not merely be readable; it should give a checker a reason to trust or distrust the answer. NAD turns sparse activations and neuron agreement into evidence about correctness. Neural Concept Verifier makes an explanation persuade a separate verifier. CRV uses structure in attribution graphs to detect reasoning errors. Veritas links generated claims to verification structure. Rashomon Trust asks whether the same explanation holds across many accurate models, not just one. Name used later: verifier-tested concept evidence means the named concept must change what an independent check believes about the answer.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is explanatory responsibility. The named concept, neuron set, graph feature, or evidence item should be necessary or sufficient enough to change the verifier's belief about the answer.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compressing activations into concepts, choosing sparse evidence, comparing attribution graphs, or checking other equally accurate models. The explanation must preserve the causal link between explanation and decision, not only human readability.

Why It Works

It works by putting the explanation under pressure. A sparse explanation has less room to hide irrelevant details. A recurring concept is less likely to be a one-off accident. A prover-verifier setup asks whether evidence can convince a separate decision rule. Comparing several accurate models matters because two models can get the same answer for different reasons. What must stay the same is explanatory responsibility. What may change is the concept set, verifier, graph view, or comparison model. A strong explanation is one that would break, or at least lose support, when the named evidence is removed or swapped.

an explanation is valid when changing its concept evidence changes the verifier decision and the explanation remains stable across relevant competing models

Evidence Check

Remove concepts, test sparse neuron sets, measure agreement across examples, swap prover evidence, build attribution-graph counterexamples, check verifier calibration, and compare several accurate models on the same explanation.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when concepts are chosen after seeing the answer, when the verifier uses the same shortcut as the prover, when sparse neurons are correlated but not responsible, when graph patterns are dataset-specific, or when too few competing models are checked to expose explanation instability.

Connection

FlashTrace and MDA trace responsibility backward to inputs or data; causal route gating tests internal interventions; consequence-based utility verifies by downstream effects. NAD, Neural Concept Verifier, CRV, Veritas, and Rashomon Trust ask the interpretability version of the same question: what evidence would make the explanation false if it were wrong?

EXEMPLAR 126

Time Series Saliency Maps, MemExplainer, Universal Redundancies In TSFMs, And Information Shapes Koopman Representation

Finding Which Past Event Changed The Future Prediction

Problem

A fever today may matter less than a medication taken last week. A traffic jam now may come from an accident that disappeared from the current camera view. In time-series problems, the important evidence is often not the biggest signal now, but the earlier event that changed memory, rhythm, or trajectory.

State Space

The state is observation history, timestamped event, memory attached to a node, recurrent hidden state, time-to-frequency transform, forecast horizon, repeated feature group, simplified evolution operator, hidden dynamics coordinate, and predicted future distribution.

Object

The object is the contribution of history to the state used for prediction. The method asks which past event changed the memory the model is using now, not merely which past event was large or recent. Time Series Saliency Maps follow responsibility through reversible transforms so evidence can be measured in time or frequency and mapped back. MemExplainer traces temporal graph memory back to source events. Universal Redundancies in TSFMs asks which repeated time features can be removed without changing forecasts. Information Shapes Koopman Representation studies coordinates where a messy changing process becomes easier to track over time. Name used later: history contribution to predictive state means the part of the past that actually changes the state from which the future is predicted.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is future-relevant information carried by history. An explanation should identify past events, frequencies, memories, or dynamics coordinates whose change would change the forecast.

Allowed Movement

What may change is converting time signals into frequency, compressing repeated history, backtracking graph memory, or lifting the system into easier-to-track coordinates. The method should preserve which historical information is responsible for the future prediction.

Why It Works

It works by treating prediction as keeping a useful state of the past. A heatmap over time helps only if it follows information through transformations and back to the original signal. A memory explanation helps only if the named event changed the memory later used. Redundancy analysis helps because repeated features may carry the same future information, so removing one copy may not change the forecast. Easier-to-track dynamics coordinates help when a messy changing process has a transformed view where tomorrow's state is easier to follow from today's state. The proof is a change test: alter the past event and see whether the future estimate changes through the claimed memory route.

a past event explains a forecast when changing that event changes the future state estimate through the learned memory or transform

Evidence Check

Delete or replace historical windows, compare time-based and frequency-based attributions, trace memory updates back to source events, remove redundant channels, test forecast degradation, and verify whether simplified dynamics coordinates preserve multi-step predictive information.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when transforms create artifacts, when deleting an event changes the whole data setting rather than just the evidence, when memory attribution confuses recency with responsibility, when redundancy changes with the forecast horizon, or when the transformed dynamics fit the data but do not explain it.

Connection

Latent Hawkes and OU identifiability recover temporal mechanisms; generative filtering treats hidden state as sufficient belief; video evidence pages ask which frames control an answer. These temporal explanation papers add the attribution layer: which part of the past changed the state from which the future was predicted?

EXEMPLAR 127

Hallucination Rate-Distortion, RMT Diffusion, Identity Bridge, And Biased Generalization

Knowing When The Model Did Not Store Enough Evidence

Problem

A student may remember the shape of a map but not whether a tiny town is on it. If forced to answer, they may guess a plausible town. Models can do the same with facts. The issue is not only honesty or calibration; sometimes the model did not store enough evidence, stored it in a hard-to-recover direction, or learned a pattern that reconstructs plausible claims instead of checking membership.

State Space

The state is sparse fact set, membership question, compression budget, representation direction, training split, diffusion sample, disagreement across outputs, uneven data directions, position or reversal bias, memorized example, and generated claim.

Object

The object is an evidence code with limited space and biased directions. A fact has to be stored in enough detail, and in a direction the model can later read, or the model will fill the gap with a plausible guess. Hallucination Rate-Distortion models factual hallucination as what happens when the model stores a compressed, imperfect record of membership in a sparse fact set. RMT Diffusion links output disagreement to finite data and uneven data directions. Identity Bridge shows that reversal failure can come from a bias in how a simple transformer stores direction. Biased Generalization warns that better test loss can still move generated samples closer to training examples. Name used later: capacity-limited evidence coding means the model must store enough evidence bits and directions to answer a query rather than reconstruct a plausible guess.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is evidence relevant to the query. The model should retain the bits or directions needed to distinguish known, unknown, reversed, copied, and merely plausible facts.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compression, generalization, sampling, denoising, or data augmentation. Those changes should not turn missing evidence into confident claims, mistake uneven representation directions for certainty, or improve average loss by copying training examples more closely.

Why It Works

It works by naming the bottleneck. If the fact set is too large or too sparse for the storage budget, some membership questions must be answered with error. If data is finite and uneven, different samples can disagree for structural reasons, not just random noise. If the training setup never teaches reversal symmetry, the model may know "A relates to B" without knowing "B relates to A." If lower loss comes with generated samples closer to training data, factual-looking improvement may be memorization pressure. The preserved thing is query-relevant evidence; the allowed change is compression or generalization. The method is honest when it separates stored evidence from plausible reconstruction.

truthful factual recall requires enough stored evidence to separate membership from plausible reconstruction under the model's capacity

Evidence Check

Test sparse membership questions, reversal pairs, generated-sample closeness to training data, disagreement across training splits, uneven representation directions, abstention when evidence is missing, and calibration separately for known facts, unknown facts, and plausible distractors.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the factual universe is not sparse enough for the model, when disagreement metrics are treated as semantic truth tests, when augmentation fixes one symmetry but breaks another, or when average loss hides a shift toward copying training examples.

Connection

Binary RAR asks for contradiction-free support, evidence-budgeted search asks when to abstain, and provenance pages ask where claims came from. This group explains the deeper limit: a model cannot truthfully answer from evidence it did not encode or cannot recover in the right coordinate.

EXEMPLAR 128

Length Generalization Bounds, Coverage Principle, Intrinsic Entropy, And Noisy Sample Compression

Having Enough Examples To Rule Out The Wrong Rule

Problem

A child may see "2, 4, 6" and guess the rule is "add two," but many other rules also fit those three numbers. A model can be powerful enough to represent the right rule and still not have enough evidence to identify it at longer lengths, rare states, or noisy observations. The issue is not what the model could represent; it is what the data actually proves.

State Space

The state is possible rule family, sequence length, source of examples, covered region, uncertainty in the source, training sample, compressed summary of the sample, added noise, target rule, and target generalization setting.

Object

The object is finite evidence support: the part of the training evidence that rules out wrong alternatives. Seeing a few short examples is useful only if those examples eliminate the wrong rules that would fail later. Length Generalization Bounds asks how short examples constrain longer behavior. Coverage Principle studies whether examples cover the regions needed for reliable extension. Intrinsic Entropy measures how much useful uncertainty the source contains, meaning how many genuinely different clues the examples can provide, rather than raw dataset size. Noisy Sample Compression asks when a small summary can still preserve the family of rules that can be learned even after independent noise is added.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is rule-identifying information. Enough evidence must survive sampling, length shift, uncertainty, and noise to choose the intended rule over other rules that fit the observed data.

Allowed Movement

What may change is compressing samples, extrapolating to longer lengths, ignoring repeated examples, or tolerating noise. The learner cannot claim support for parts of the rule that the training distribution never made distinguishable.

Why It Works

It works by separating "can express the rule" from "has evidence for the rule." A transformer may be able to implement a long rule, but length generalization needs examples that pin down the repeated structure. Coverage matters because missing regions leave many lookalike rules alive. Entropy matters because two same-size datasets can carry very different amounts of rule information. Compression matters because a small summary that preserves the learnable family is a certificate of what the sample really proved. The claim is justified only when the observed examples rule out the competing rules that would behave differently in the target setting.

generalization is justified when observed evidence rules out competing rules in the target setting within the sample, uncertainty, and noise budget

Evidence Check

Report extrapolation by length, holes in coverage, subsets controlled for source uncertainty, noise sensitivity, compression size, counterexamples from competing rules, and sample-size curves showing where the target rule becomes identifiable.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when benchmarks test only familiar-region interpolation, when coverage metrics ignore new combinations of parts, when uncertainty estimates miss hidden structure, when compressed summaries preserve labels but not mechanisms, or when noise changes which rule family can be identified.

Connection

Language Generation in the Limit separates computability from feasible learning; scaling-law papers ask what axis creates evidence; structural reasoning probes expose missing rule support. This group gives the shared first-principles answer: learning needs evidence that distinguishes the intended rule from lookalikes.

EXEMPLAR 129

SoftJAX/SoftTorch, Provable NAM Explanations, Categorical ANOVA, And Optimal Compositional Explanations

Searching For Explanations Without Changing What Counts

Problem

Some explanations are made of hard choices: which feature, which branch, which rule, which ranked item, which subset of concepts. Making those choices soft can help training, but it can also change the question. It is like replacing "which three ingredients caused the flavor?" with "what smooth blend of every ingredient scores well?" The answer may be easier to optimize but no longer the explanation we needed.

State Space
