The state is discrete operation, softened operation, yes/no mask, rank or sort variable, feature subset, category support, feature interaction, additive component, number of explanation pieces, and model prediction.

Object

The object is a search over allowed explanations. The system is looking for the smallest or clearest set of pieces that still makes the prediction possible, while respecting whether the pieces are yes/no choices, ranks, categories, or interacting concepts. SoftJAX/SoftTorch provides trainable versions of Boolean operations, indexing, ranking, sorting, transport, and straight-through choices. Provable NAM Explanations uses additive model structure to find minimal sufficient explanations. Categorical ANOVA separates one-feature effects from interactions when category features depend on each other. Optimal Compositional Explanations shows that overlapping concepts can make simple greedy search miss the best explanation. Name used later: a structured explanation search problem means finding the allowed pieces that are enough to explain the prediction.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is sufficiency under the original structure. The searched or softened explanation should still answer which discrete pieces of the model or input make the prediction possible.

Allowed Movement

What may change is softening hard choices, using additive structure, separating interactions, or searching concept combinations. The method should preserve the allowed explanation set and should not let optimization convenience redefine what counts as an explanation.

Why It Works

It works when structure reduces the search without erasing the question. Soft operators help because they let training signals pass through choices that are otherwise hard to optimize, but the final answer must still map back to the original hard choice. Additive models help because each part can be checked separately. ANOVA-style decomposition helps because it separates one-feature effects from interactions. Compositional explanation theory helps because overlapping concepts mean the best set is not always built by adding the best-looking local piece at each step. The preserved thing is sufficiency under the original explanation rules; optimization convenience is allowed only if it returns to those rules.

a structured explanation is valid when the optimized answer maps back to an allowed discrete explanation that remains sufficient for the prediction

Evidence Check

Compare soft and exact discrete answers on small cases, test whether the explanation is minimal, measure sufficiency after mapping back to hard choices, audit interaction terms under dependence, and build overlapping-concept cases where greedy or beam search fails.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when softening changes the allowed set, when additive assumptions hide interactions, when category dependence is misspecified, when concept libraries are incomplete, or when the explanation is minimal only for a proxy objective.

Connection

Concept verification asks whether explanations change a verifier; mechanistic attribution asks where source evidence enters a model. This group supplies the optimization layer: how do we search the space of structured explanations without silently changing what counts as an explanation?

EXEMPLAR 130

EMP, Exact RL Unlearning, CV Relative Instability, TRECA, UP-OCP, And Anytime Trees

Proving The Thing The System Will Actually Do

Problem

A bridge safety proof is not useful if it proves the bridge before trucks, weather, and repairs enter the picture. Deployment guarantees have the same issue. Pruning, unlearning, cross-validation, abstention, online coverage, and streaming tree growth all change the system. The proof has to cover that actual operation, not a cleaner operation nearby.

State Space

The state is model component, score mass kept after pruning, policy trajectory, deleted sample, distance between before-and-after behavior, cross-validation fold, confidence interval, abstention set, quantile error used for coverage, online coverage state, stream prefix, and candidate tree split.

Object

The object is the condition that stays stable while the real operation happens. The proof has to follow the actual move: deleting a sample, pruning a component, abstaining, updating a coverage rule, comparing validation folds, or repeatedly trying tree splits. EMP bounds pruning by tracking retained score mass and loss change. Exact RL Unlearning checks deletion through behavior-distance stability while keeping regret low. CV Relative Instability argues that intervals should be stable for comparisons, not just single methods. TRECA protects abstention risk estimates when helper estimates are imperfect. UP-OCP turns online coverage into controlled accumulated error. Anytime Trees uses tests that remain valid even when the stream keeps offering chances to split. Name used later: an operational stability condition is the exact property that must survive the operation the system performs.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the guarantee's target after the operation occurs: pruning should preserve loss, unlearning should remove dependence on deleted data, validation should preserve interval validity, abstention should preserve risk control, online coverage should preserve calibration, and streaming tests should preserve false-split control.

Allowed Movement

What may change is removing components, deleting data, resampling folds, abstaining, updating coverage, or growing a tree. Each operation must satisfy the stability condition that makes the final certificate meaningful.

Why It Works

It works when the proof controls the same movement deployment performs. Pruning bounds matter when they connect removed components to loss change. Unlearning stability matters when deletion is visible as independence from the removed data, not merely good accuracy afterward. Relative cross-validation stability matters because we compare methods, not isolated scores. Abstention estimates need protection against imperfect helper models. Online coverage needs accumulated errors controlled over time. Streaming tree tests need protection because repeated split attempts can otherwise make random noise look significant. The proof is useful only when it covers the actual moving system, not a cleaner system nearby.

a deployment guarantee is valid when every allowed operation keeps the certified quantity inside the stated stability, regret, or testing bound

Evidence Check

Report retained score mass, loss changes after pruning, tests for remaining dependence on deleted data, regret and coverage curves, fold-level comparison instability, sensitivity to helper-estimation error, abstention calibration, and false-split rates under streams.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the certificate controls a proxy operation, when pruning scores do not match component function, when deletion is judged only by accuracy, when folds are dependent in an unmodeled way, when helper-estimation errors are too large, or when stream adaptation violates the test design.

Connection

Finite test certification, floating-point expressivity, watermark robustness, and low-bit quantization all ask whether a guarantee describes the object actually deployed. This group states the general rule: a proof matters when its stability condition matches the operation the system will perform.

EXEMPLAR 131

PoPE, MrRoPE, Robust Filter Attention, Attention Mean-Field, And Transformer Outputs

Keeping A Fact And Its Address Separate

Problem

A library needs both the book text and the shelf address. If the address is wrong, the right book cannot be found. If the address overwrites the title, the catalog becomes useless. Long-context models face the same problem: they must remember what a token says and where it appeared, especially when the evidence is far away.

State Space

The state is token content, position index, rotation used for position, distance from the current token, attention rule, bias toward recent text, context length, output sequences the model can reach, uncertainty over old evidence, and positions beyond the training length.

Object

The object is addressable evidence: content plus the pointer needed to find it later. The model needs to preserve both "what was said" and "where it was said," because a fact without an address may be impossible to retrieve and an address without content is empty. PoPE separates content from position. MrRoPE treats longer positions like changing the number system used for addresses. Robust Filter Attention treats old evidence as something that becomes more uncertain with distance. Attention Mean-Field studies what happens when attention flows through very long context. Transformer Outputs asks which sequences an architecture can or cannot produce. Name used later: position-content factorization means keeping the fact and its address recoverable as separate pieces.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is addressable evidence. When a fact appears far away, the model should preserve both the fact and the address needed to retrieve it, without turning position into a shortcut for content.

Allowed Movement

What may change is position scaling, rotation, filtering old tokens, changing attention flow, or bounding reachable outputs. The method must preserve which content is being addressed and how distance changes uncertainty.

Why It Works

It works by separating the note from the location of the note. Content is the evidence. Position is the pointer. Long-context methods help only when they keep those two jobs distinct outside the lengths seen in training. Filtering helps when old context is treated as uncertain evidence, not as dead text, so far-away facts are discounted only when distance really makes retrieval less reliable. Reachability bounds matter because they show when the model's architecture blocks some outputs no matter how much context is supplied. The preserved thing is addressable evidence; the allowed change is how position and attention are scaled.

long-context use is reliable when the fact and its address remain separately recoverable as context length grows

Evidence Check

Swap content while holding positions fixed, swap positions while holding content fixed, test longer lengths, retrieve under distractors, ablate position encodings, measure uncertainty with distance, and check outputs that should be impossible if the architectural bound is real.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when position encodings memorize training-length patterns, when filtering deletes rare but decisive evidence, when separating position and content helps retrieval but hurts local syntax, or when reachability analysis ignores decoding and tool use around the model.

Connection

ThinKV and EntroKV compress context into sufficient state; LLMs Get Lost tests whether long context remains navigable; token sufficiency pages ask which evidence survives a budget. This group names the coordinate problem underneath them: long context works only when evidence and address do not collapse into one brittle signal.

EXEMPLAR 132

Rational Transductors, DiScoFormer, Exact GNN Algorithms, Which Algorithms Can GNNs Learn, Constrained Transformers, And Deep Flow Networks

Building The Rule Into The Machine

Problem

A calculator that memorizes a few multiplication examples is not doing multiplication. It has to carry the right intermediate state and apply the same rule again and again. Neural models can make the same mistake: they can fit small algorithm examples while failing once the graph, sequence, or problem size changes.

State Space

The state is symbol stream, finite memory state, attention rule, graph node state, how far messages can travel, local instruction, remaining constraint error, flow value, discrete function, and number of repeated steps.

Object

The object is a rule the architecture can actually execute. The model needs an internal state that can be updated again and again in the same way, so the rule still works when the sequence or graph grows. Rational Transductors add finite-state recurrence to attention. DiScoFormer shows attention can recover density and multiscale similarity behavior. Exact GNN Algorithms treats message passing as programmable local instructions. Which Algorithms Can GNNs Learn asks when small-graph training can or cannot extend to larger graphs. Constrained Transformers push intermediate states toward valid descent steps. Deep Flow Networks represent value functions for flow problems on discrete structures. Name used later: an executable update rule means the model has the state and step-by-step operation needed to run the algorithm, not only imitate examples.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the computation under scale shift. The same rule should still execute when sequence length, graph size, or problem instance changes.

Allowed Movement

What may change is recurrence, local messages, similarity rules, constraints, or flow structure. The architecture must preserve the algorithmic state transition rather than merely preserve input-output correlations from the training distribution.

Why It Works

It works when the model's basic operations match the computation. Finite-state recurrence helps when the rule needs memory across positions. Attention helps when the task is comparing or aggregating similar items. Message passing helps when a graph algorithm can be made from local instructions. Constraint-shaped layers help when every step should move closer to a valid solution. Flow networks help when the target is about moving limited resources through a discrete structure. The proof step is scale: if the internal update is really the rule, it should keep working on larger states where memorized training examples are no longer enough.

algorithmic generalization holds when the learned step preserves the target rule on larger states, not only observed examples

Evidence Check

Test larger graphs and longer sequences, compare internal states to algorithm variables, run counterexamples outside training size, remove recurrence or constraints, verify required invariances, and report whether errors come from one bad step or accumulated drift.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the computation requires global information outside the architecture's communication radius, when recurrence stores spurious state, when constraints make optimization easier but not algorithmically faithful, or when extrapolation tests stay too close to training templates.

Connection

Transformer Circuits maps clustering into attention blocks; Softmax Turing and HATSolver show formal expressivity; CRAMF and verifier pages check symbolic artifacts. These papers connect the big picture: reasoning is credible only when the system can implement the computation the answer depends on.

EXEMPLAR 133

DFM Bounds, GWF, LoRFS, CoEvol-NO, L2G-Net, And Modified SINNs

Checking The Route, Not Only The Final Answer

Problem

A hiking route can end at the right cabin but still be unsafe if it crossed a cliff. Scientific generators and solvers have the same issue. A final sample, field, or graph result can look plausible even if the probability mass, physical field, mesh, or long-range graph signal took the wrong path to get there.

State Space

The state is probability path, distance between probability clouds, accumulated mismatch, diffusion path, physical field, rule error, frequency coefficient, graph long-range pattern, mesh coordinate, hidden solver state, and predict-then-correct update.

Object

The object is an approximation whose route stays accurate. The method must control what happens at every step, because a small wrong move can be carried forward until the final answer looks plausible but came from the wrong process. DFM Bounds studies whether diffusion flow matching moves probability toward the target correctly. GWF derives generation objectives as movement between probability clouds. LoRFS replaces noisy sample checks with analytic rule errors for physical equations. CoEvol-NO keeps hidden solver state and mesh coordinates evolving together. L2G-Net preserves long-range graph information without computing the full expensive decomposition. Modified SINNs use frequency structure for high-dimensional solvers. Name used later: a path-accurate approximation means the accumulated error along the route stays small enough for the final claim.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the rule that lives along the trajectory: mass should move along the intended probability path, physical fields should satisfy their equations, graph signals should preserve long-range structure, and mesh or solver updates should not invent dynamics.

Allowed Movement

What may change is approximating flows, compressing long-range graph structure, replacing sampled checks with closed-form rule checks, or updating mesh coordinates. The method must control error that compounds along the path.

Why It Works

It works by asking where the error lives. A sampler needs distances between probability clouds, not only final visual quality. A physical solver needs equation-rule errors, not only point labels. A graph solver needs long-range patterns that local messages can miss. A changing mesh needs the hidden solver state to stay coupled to the mesh. What must stay the same is the rule along the route. What may change is the cheaper approximation used to follow it. The output is only trustworthy when the accumulated route error stays below what the final claim can tolerate.

an approximation is valid when accumulated path error stays below the tolerance required by the final claim

Evidence Check

Report convergence of probability paths, rule-error norms over time, recovery of long-range graph patterns, mesh-update stability, long-horizon rollout error, analytic versus sampled rule-check ablations, and cases where final metrics hide path violations.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the chosen metric ignores the decisive rule, when compression loses local breaks, when rule objectives are exact for the wrong boundary regime, when mesh updates chase numerical artifacts, or when convergence bounds require assumptions not met by the data.

Connection

IRNO treats physical surrogates as stable operators; FALCON and FIDIA preserve scientific quantities through sampling; machine-coupled dynamics asks whether implementation preserves the math. This group gives the shared principle: the output is only as trustworthy as the path used to reach it.

EXEMPLAR 134

SlaClip, PRISM, IHM, LM Memorization Capacity, Empirical Privacy Protection Benchmark, OPL/G-OPL, And PetaGAIL++

Adding Protection Where Private Information Actually Leaks

Problem

Blurring every part of a photo by the same amount may hide a face but leave a unique tattoo visible. Privacy in models is similar. Noise, clipping, projection, and synthetic generation help only if they act on the places where individual information is actually encoded.

State Space

The state is private sample, update size, clipping threshold, noise level, low-rank update factors, local update space, second-order sensitivity sketch, memorized bits, membership attack query, trajectory sensitivity, identity-carrying subspace, and task utility.

Object

The object is controlled information release in the directions that carry private traces. The protection should go where one person's data can actually move the model or output, not where noise is easiest to add. SlaClip adapts clipping using slack already present in private training. PRISM adds privacy noise to the actual low-rank update rather than to arbitrary factor coordinates. IHM improves private linear regression by sketching sensitive second-order information, meaning how sharply different directions affect the fitted model. LM Memorization Capacity asks how many bits a model can store when generalization is removed. Empirical Privacy Protection Benchmark tests exposure when adaptation data is close to training data. OPL/G-OPL remove identity-bearing video directions while preserving anomaly signals. PetaGAIL++ generates private trajectories with sensitivity in mind. Name used later: geometry-sensitive information release means privacy protection is placed where private traces actually live.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the population or task signal after individual information has been made hard to distinguish along the directions where private exposure can occur.

Allowed Movement

What may change is clipping, sketching, adding noise, projecting away identity, generating synthetic trajectories, or reparameterizing low-rank updates. The method must account for where private information is actually encoded.

Why It Works

It works when the privacy mechanism matches the leak channel. Adaptive clipping helps because clipping too much wastes utility while clipping too little leaves sensitivity uncontrolled. Low-rank privacy helps only when noise attaches to the actual update, not to a factorization that can be rewritten without changing the update. Second-order sketches help because some directions affect the fitted model more sharply than others. Projection helps when identity lies in a removable direction while the task signal lies elsewhere. Trajectory privacy helps because some paths reveal far more about a person than others. The preserved thing is population usefulness after individual traces become hard to distinguish.

privacy improves when noise or projection targets private-information directions while preserving task-relevant population signal

Evidence Check

Report membership attacks, canary extraction, privacy accounting, utility under clipping changes, tests that low-rank reparameterization does not change private exposure, sensitivity to second-order directions, identity-removal ablations, trajectory-sensitivity curves, and private exposure when adaptation data is close to training data.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the private direction is misidentified, when projection removes the task signal, when clipping statistics leak extra information, when low-rank assumptions break, when synthetic trajectories preserve unique routines, or when privacy tests ignore how close adaptation data is to training data.

Connection

Secret Memory and high-dimensional Gaussian mechanisms give resource and noise limits; certified unlearning asks for deletion indistinguishability; provenance pages ask which data process produced an artifact. This group connects them: privacy is about controlling information flow where data actually leaves traces.

EXEMPLAR 135

Assistant Axis, Robust Harmful Features, Surgery, Suppress And Diversify, Backdoor Self-Awareness, And SVGT

Finding The Hidden Route To Unsafe Behavior

Problem

A machine can look safe while one hidden switch still causes failure. A model can do the same: it may give safe answers on ordinary prompts while keeping an internal route that produces harmful behavior when pressure, triggers, persona drift, or fine-tuning activates it.

State Space

The state is internal activation direction, attention head, sink pattern, harmful feature, value vector, intended assistant region, backdoor trigger, robust pathway, fine-tuning update, bridge token, and decoded output.

Object

The object is the internal route that causally affects unsafe output. The method should find the hidden path where pressure, persona drift, a trigger, or fine-tuning changes the answer before the final words appear. Assistant Axis treats persona drift as movement away from the intended assistant region. Robust Harmful Features finds harmful activations that survive alignment. Surgery controls attention-sink divergence during harmful fine-tuning. Suppress and Diversify keeps robust pathways while reducing corrupt ones. Backdoor Self-Awareness tries to make hidden trigger conditions explicit. SVGT uses a separate value space to steer generation at inference time. Name used later: a localized internal mechanism is a direction, pathway, head, or trigger condition that changes unsafe behavior when intervened on.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is safe useful behavior under pressure. Fluency and helpful capability should remain, while the internal route that causes harmful behavior is detected, bounded, or redirected.

Allowed Movement

What may change is monitoring activations, constraining the assistant region, regularizing attention sinks, steering value directions, diversifying pathways, or asking the model to reveal triggers. The intervention must stay close enough to the model's real computation to control a mechanism rather than a brittle correlate.

Why It Works

It works when unsafe behavior has an internal warning sign that appears before the final text. A direction is useful if moving along it changes harmfulness while preserving ordinary capability. A sink signal is useful if harmful fine-tuning repeatedly routes information through the same heads. Trigger recovery helps if the hidden poisoned condition can become inspectable. Value steering helps if safety values can guide decoding without rewriting the whole model. The preserved thing is safe useful behavior; the allowed change is targeted movement on the suspected route. The test is causal: change the route and harmful output should change in the predicted way.

internal safety control works when changing the named route changes harmful output while preserving useful behavior outside that route

Evidence Check

Patch activations, remove heads, run jailbreak pressure tests, compare traces before and after fine-tuning, measure distance from the intended assistant region, recover triggers, sweep steering strength, and test capability retention on benign prompts.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when unsafe behavior is spread across many weak routes, when a direction detects style rather than harm, when steering pushes the model outside normal behavior, when trigger descriptions are invented, or when benign capability depends on the same route being suppressed.

Connection

Pressure Reveals Character measures hidden tendency under adaptive tests; FlowGuard separates multimodal safety routes; Causal Route Gating tests internal causal interventions. This group adds the safety mechanism layer: the output is downstream of internal state, and safety has to control the state where unsafe behavior is formed.

EXEMPLAR 136

Modern Conservation Laws, Gradient Flow Through Diagram Expansions, Non-Euclidean EoS, WSM, Polar Express, And RACO

Moving The Model Without Breaking What The Path Must Preserve

Problem

Training is not only "make the loss smaller." It is a route through model space. One route may preserve a useful skill, another may erase it. One update may be safe for a smooth direction but too large for a fragile one. Optimizers matter because they decide how the model is allowed to move.

State Space

The state is parameter vector, update direction, rescaling rule, momentum, learning-rate path, checkpoint window, matrix-sign iterate, sensitive direction, nonstandard distance rule, quantity preserved by training, objective conflict, and tradeoff point.

Object

The object is the training path plus what it must preserve. Some quantities should stay fixed while training moves, some directions are fragile, and some objectives pull against each other. Modern Conservation Laws names quantities that stay fixed under certain ideal training flows. Gradient flow means the continuous version of following the loss downhill. Gradient Flow Through Diagram Expansions studies how those update pushes pass through structured expansions. Non-Euclidean EoS shows stability depends on the distance rule used to measure smoothness. WSM interprets learning-rate decay through checkpoint aggregation windows. Polar Express designs matrix-sign iterations around hardware-friendly matrix multiplication. RACO clips conflicting preference updates toward a balanced tradeoff. Name used later: preserved path geometry means the update route is judged by what it improves and what it must not disturb.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is useful movement: updates should reduce the target objective while keeping the preserved quantities, stability margins, checkpoint averages, and multi-objective tradeoffs that make the final model usable.

Allowed Movement

What may change is rescaling updates, averaging checkpoints, making directions independent, clipping conflicts, normalizing matrices, or changing the distance rule. Each move must respect directions that are fragile, preserved, conflicting, or limited by hardware.

Why It Works

It works by replacing "take a step downhill" with "move in the way this model can safely move." Conservation laws tell which quantities the path resists changing. Nonstandard distance rules matter because a step can be large under one ruler and safe under another. Checkpoint-window analysis shows that decay can act like weighted averaging over past states. Matrix-sign methods help when the numerical primitive matches hardware. Conflict-aware clipping helps when alignment objectives point in incompatible directions. The preserved thing is useful capability and stability along the path; the allowed change is the update rule used to move.

an update is justified when it improves the target while respecting preserved quantities, stability, conflict, and machine-realizable movement

Evidence Check

Track preserved quantities, step size in sensitive directions, checkpoint-window ablations, matrix-sign numerical error, objective-conflict angles, remaining tradeoff error, hardware throughput, and downstream capability drift after optimizer changes.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the chosen distance rule ignores a fragile capability, when preserved quantities describe an idealized flow but not finite-step training, when checkpoint averaging hides rare regressions, when hardware-friendly iterations lose accuracy, or when clipping suppresses the update needed for the most important objective.

Connection

DiReCT chooses data by how strongly it can bend training; Beyond Muon and Adam Degeneracy explain optimizer regimes; operational stability pages ask whether guarantees survive the deployed loop. This group connects them: training recipes work when their movement is shaped by the right path rules, not because a named optimizer is inherently good.

EXEMPLAR 137

KDE Kernel Algebra, FlashSketch, FlashSinkhorn, Matroid Algorithms, IO-Aware GNNs, And TileLang

Counting The Cost Of Getting The Data, Not Just Doing The Math

Problem

A recipe can be simple but slow if every step requires walking to another building for an ingredient. Algorithms have the same issue. The bottleneck may be moving memory, asking expensive queries, building a huge matrix, handling sparse layouts, or synchronizing machines, not the number of arithmetic operations on paper.

State Space

The state is query access, set size, pairwise-similarity matrix, compressed sketch, memory hierarchy, sparse graph layout, tensor tile, bandwidth budget, synchronization point, intermediate tensor, and approximation error.

Object

The object is the real access pattern: what information the algorithm reads, moves, materializes, queries, or approximates at each step. A method is not cheap if it avoids arithmetic but secretly builds a huge table, makes many hidden queries, or moves data through memory in an expensive order. KDE Kernel Algebra sharpens access limits for density operations. FlashSketch and FlashSinkhorn redesign sketching and transport around memory-efficient kernels. Matroid Algorithms charge queries by realistic set access. IO-Aware GNNs reorganize graph computation around memory traffic. TileLang makes tensor layout explicit so computation matches hardware. Name used later: an access model means the rules and costs for getting information, not only calculating with it.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the mathematical result under the real cost model. The approximation should still represent the similarity, transport, graph, matroid, or tensor operation while using the access pattern the machine can afford.

Allowed Movement

What may change is sketching, fusing similarity computations, tiling tensors, changing sparse layouts, limiting queries, or approximating transport. The method must account for what information was not accessed and how that omission changes the claim.

Why It Works

It works when the bottleneck is moved into an operation the system can perform cheaply. Sketching helps if the sketch keeps the statistic needed later. Flash-style similarity computations help if they avoid creating huge intermediate arrays without changing the target computation. Query costs matter because a supposedly simple lookup can hide real work. IO-aware graph methods help because graph learning is often limited by memory traffic. Tiling helps because tensor math is executed by concrete memory and compute units. The preserved thing is the mathematical result within error; the allowed change is how the data is reached and moved.

an efficiency claim is valid when the cheaper access pattern preserves the target object within error while reducing reads, writes, queries, or synchronization

Evidence Check

Report memory traffic, materialized intermediates, query size, similarity-approximation error, graph throughput, sparse-layout sensitivity, tile occupancy, synchronization cost, and accuracy under the reduced access pattern.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the cost model omits the real bottleneck, when sketches preserve average behavior but lose rare modes, when fused similarity computations change numerical stability, when queries hide preprocessing cost, or when a layout optimized for one hardware regime becomes bad in another.

Connection

TileLang asks how tensor computation maps to hardware; ParetoPO budgets tool calls; learning-augmented algorithms price advice. This group gives the systems-level first principle: capability expands when the mathematical object can be accessed at the cost the deployment loop can pay.

EXEMPLAR 138

Axiomatic Value Of Regularization, Beyond Log Likelihood, RACO, AuxDPO, DPO Unchained, And What Preferences Can And Cannot Predict

Remembering That A Loss Function Is A Choice Rule

Problem

When people vote, the counting rule changes the winner. Preference training has the same issue. A loss function is not neutral bookkeeping. It decides what counts as evidence, how conflicts are combined, how much to trust the old policy, and what kind of social choice the final model inherits.

State Space

The state is preference comparison, reference policy, strength of the stay-close penalty, supervised token likelihood, model capability level, conflicting update directions, auxiliary variable, social-choice rule, reward proxy, and final policy distribution.

Object

The object is the preference objective as an aggregation rule. It says how human comparisons become a model update: whose preference counts, how conflicts are settled, how much old behavior is protected, and what the data is too weak to decide. Axiomatic Value of Regularization asks which social-choice properties change when training stays close to a reference policy. Beyond Log Likelihood shows that ordinary likelihood can work against strong models and that the right objective depends on capability. RACO clips conflicting preference updates toward a balanced tradeoff. AuxDPO adds variables to better approximate local RLHF behavior. DPO Unchained and preference-limit papers ask what preference data can and cannot identify. Name used later: an alignment objective as decision rule means the loss function is the rule that turns imperfect human comparisons into model movement.

Preserved Quantity

What must stay the same is the intended value ordering after noisy comparisons, reference constraints, model priors, and objective conflicts are converted into policy movement.

Allowed Movement

What may change is regularizing toward a reference, reweighting likelihood, adding auxiliary variables, clipping conflicting updates, or changing the preference objective. The method must state which choice rule it implements and what the data can identify.

Why It Works

It works when the training objective matches the evidence. Staying close to a reference helps only if that reference carries values worth preserving. Likelihood reweighting helps when low-probability supervised tokens are more noise than signal for a strong model. Conflict-aware updates help when objectives genuinely disagree. Auxiliary variables help when direct preference optimization lacks state that local RLHF behavior would use. Preference-limit results matter because no objective can recover a value ordering that the comparison data never distinguished. The preserved thing is intended value order; the allowed change is how noisy comparisons are converted into movement.

an alignment update is valid when the loss-induced movement preserves the intended preference ordering under reference constraints, noise, and conflicts

Evidence Check

Report social-choice rule satisfaction, sensitivity to reference weight, objective comparisons by model capability, conflict between update directions, remaining tradeoff error, preference-noise stress tests, and cases where preference labels cannot distinguish two policies.

Failure Boundary

The approach fails when the objective encodes an unintended social rule, when the reference policy preserves bad behavior, when likelihood reweighting trusts model priors too much, when clipping conflicting updates suppresses minority constraints, or when preference data is too weak to identify the desired ordering.

Connection

Omni-Reward and JudgeBench treat reward as a proxy measurement channel; performative misalignment asks how behavior changes under deployment; ParetoPO handles cost-accuracy tradeoffs. This group names the alignment-level principle: a loss is not neutral. It is a mathematical decision rule over imperfect human evidence.

EXEMPLAR 139

SleepLM, BioX-Bridge, Seizure-Semiology-Suite, WAVE, And Bio-Signal Foundation Interfaces

Turning Body Signals Into Claims A Clinician Can Check

Problem

A heart trace, sleep trace, or seizure video is not just a sequence of numbers or frames. It is noisy evidence about a body event. A useful model must translate that evidence into language a clinician can read while still pointing back to the measured signal and timing.

State Space

The state is waveform, sensor channel, sampling rate, sleep event, hidden physiological state, clinical vocabulary, report sentence, event timing, bridge between modalities, labeled source signal, weakly labeled target signal, and expert judgment.

Object

The object is an interface between raw body measurements and clinical language. Think of a heart trace as a witness, not as a report. The model may translate the trace into words, but the words must still point back to the measured event, the time it happened, the sensor that saw it, and the uncertainty around it. SleepLM aligns sleep recordings with captions, retrieval, event localization, and insight generation. BioX-Bridge transfers information across biosignal types by aligning middle-layer representations instead of retraining whole models. Seizure-Semiology-Suite checks whether clinical video and report reasoning preserve medically meaningful timing. WAVE-style multimodal systems make audio, video, and text access depend on the user's prompt. In plain terms, the interface must keep the hidden body event recoverable while making it readable.

Why It Works

A bridge works if it connects the layer where that shared body state is present, not a layer that only shares device artifacts. A language interface works if captions and reports preserve event timing and medical meaning. A benchmark works if it tests the variables clinicians actually use, not just whether a sentence sounds medical. What must stay the same is signal evidence; what may change is the format used to make that evidence readable.

a biosignal claim is valid when the body event remains recoverable from sensor data and supports the clinical text under expert-checkable timing

Evidence Check

Failure Boundary

Connection
